## Findings by tag

### zig: writing good Zig: practice and idiom from the official docs, std and respected projects; see zig/* (15)

- [[ak-bun-rust-rewrite#^f1]] [zig] The Zig team reads users' source code to see how the language affects them, test breakage and check performance regressions. (B/fair, verified)
- [[kristoff-talk-better-software#^f6]] [zig] Dependencies are technical and design debt: fine to start, but own whatever is core to the product. @4:29 (B/fair, verified)
- [[matklad-comptime-orm#^f16]] [zig] Learning method: build toy versions; recommends a raytracer to learn Zig. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-zig-and-rust#^f1]] [zig] Two reliability styles: Erlang (design for partial faults) vs SQLite (rigorous engineering); rust-analyzer is the first, TigerBeetle the second. (B/strong, verified)
- [[mitchellh-building-large-technical-projects#^f3]] [zig] Early components are good enough, not finished: in-memory data, built-in structures, inputs up front. (C/fair, verified)
- [[mitchellh-building-large-technical-projects#^f4]] [zig] He throws away demo tooling freely (an ASCII grid CLI for the parser). (C/fair, verified)
- [[seguin-learning-zig-index#^f2]] [zig] It targets the latest development version and asks readers to report drift. (B/fair, verified)
- [[tigerbeetle-how-we-use-zig#^f4]] [zig] (inferred) Checked, not there: nothing on TigerBeetle conventions beyond Tiger Style; skip for the guide except as colour. (C/weak, absence)
- [[zig-langref-017-a#^f1]] [zig] Zig's goals include maintainability: code should state intent precisely to the compiler and to readers. Introduction (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-learn-index#^f1]] [zig] The only official guide is the build system one; guides are to move into the std docs later. (A/weak, verified)
- [[zig-learn-index#^f2]] [zig] Official pointers for learning are community works (Zig book, zig.guide, Ziglings, Exercism) and one talk, "Road to Zig 1.0". (A/weak, verified)
- [[zig-repo-docs#^f30]] [zig] Governance is BDFN: Kelley has the final say; language proposals are not accepted; real use cases are the most valued argument. `README.md:461` (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-repo-docs#^f31]] [zig] Strict no-LLM policy for issues, patches and tracker comments. `README.md:489` (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-talk-kelley-tagged-union#^f1]] [zig] Core principle "doing more with less": a small language with powerful constructs (comptime), lean programs, low resource use. @1:55 (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-talk-kelley-tagged-union#^f2]] [zig] Rejected quadrant: "less with more", adding cost or complexity that loses something (his example: an ORM over a database). @2:40 (A/fair, verified)

### zig/api: API and interface design: init and deinit, options structs, decl literals, vtables, std.Io (330)

- [[ak-async-io-text#^f1]] [zig/api] Rule stated outright: set up Io once in main like an allocator; reusable code takes an Allocator if it allocates and an Io if it does I/O. (A/strong, verified)
- [[ak-async-io-text#^f5]] [zig/api] cancel has await's semantics plus a cancellation request; honouring it is up to the Io implementation; both are idempotent with each other. (A/strong, verified)
- [[ak-async-io-text#^f8]] [zig/api] Producer/consumer over `Io.Queue` needs `io.concurrent` (can fail with `error.ConcurrencyUnavailable`); `io.async` there deadlocks with one thread. (A/strong, verified)
- [[ak-async-io-text#^f9]] [zig/api] Example helper naming: free functions take `(gpa, io, ...)` in that order. (A/strong, verified)
- [[ak-async-io-text#^f10]] [zig/api] He says the APIs are not set in stone and asks for real-world feedback. (A/strong, verified)
- [[ak-async-io-text#^f11]] [zig/api] [toolchain] In Oct 2025 `std.Io.Threaded.init` took just the allocator (later an options struct too). (A/strong, verified)
- [[bun-repo#^f4]] [zig/api] Decl literals recommended for initialisation. `src/CLAUDE.md` (A/fair, verified)
- [[bun-repo#^f5]] [zig/api] [zig/errors] std is wrapped: always `bun.*` over `std.*` for fs, posix, paths, strings, because the wrappers keep OS error info (errno, syscall, path) and never use `unreachable`. `src/CLAUDE.md` (A/fair, verified)
- [[ghostty-repo#^f51]] [zig/api] Constructors take `(io, alloc, opts)` with an Options struct whose fields carry documented defaults. `src/terminal/Terminal.zig:281-333` (A/strong, verified)
- [[ghostty-repo#^f52]] [zig/api] Decl literals are used throughout for init and empty values. `src/terminal/Terminal.zig:337`, `src/terminal/stream.zig:593`, `src/datastruct/blocking_queue.zig:66` (A/strong, verified)
- [[ghostty-repo#^f53]] [zig/api] Ownership transfer is written in the doc comment. `src/terminal/stream.zig:56-57` (A/strong, verified)
- [[ghostty-repo#^f54]] [zig/api] A borrowed-or-owned result is a tagged union with its own deinit, so callers free uniformly. `src/terminal/PageList.zig:133-158` (A/strong, verified)
- [[ghostty-repo#^f55]] [zig/api] C API rules: opaque pointers for long-lived objects, sized structs, and a `_MAX_VALUE` sentinel in every C enum to force int size. `src/terminal/c/AGENTS.md`, `AGENTS.md` (A/strong, verified)
- [[ghostty-repo#^f56]] [zig/api] A test checks every Zig enum value against the C header, so the hand-written header can't drift. `src/lib/enum.zig:105-111`, `src/apprt/action.zig:440-442` (A/strong, verified)
- [[ghostty-repo#^f57]] [zig/api] Optional vs required interface: actions are optional to implement; required runtime functions are called directly so a missing one fails to compile. New tags go last for ABI. `src/apprt/action.zig:60-68` (A/strong, verified)
- [[ghostty-repo#^f58]] [zig/api] Generated enums keep removed keys as integer holes so values stay stable in ABI or serialised formats. `src/lib/enum.zig:8-10` (A/strong, verified)
- [[ghostty-repo#^f59]] [zig/api] Library feature gates are a packed struct of bools with defaults, set from one `-Dvt-features` option. `src/terminal/build_options.zig:45-68` (A/strong, verified)
- [[ghostty-repo#^f60]] [zig/api] Renamed config keys keep working through a comptime string map of compatibility handlers. `src/config/Config.zig:62-69` (A/strong, verified)
- [[ghostty-repo#^f61]] [zig/api] `std.Io` is passed explicitly as a parameter (0.16+ style), even to small helpers like home-dir lookup. `src/os/homedir.zig:13` (A/strong, verified)
- [[kristoff-asynchrony-not-concurrency#^f1]] [zig/api] Definitions: asynchrony = may run out of order and stay correct; concurrency = progress several tasks at a time; parallelism = physically simultaneous. (A/strong, verified)
- [[kristoff-asynchrony-not-concurrency#^f2]] [zig/api] A library can use `io.async` without forcing users off single-threaded blocking I/O. (A/strong, verified)
- [[kristoff-asynchrony-not-concurrency#^f3]] [zig/api] Requesting concurrency documents a correctness requirement and errors on an Io that can't provide it. (A/strong, verified)
- [[kristoff-talk-better-software#^f1]] [zig/api] Keep library state in struct instances, never globals, so users can have two; that is also what makes code thread- and async-friendly. @9:36 (B/fair, verified)
- [[kristoff-talk-better-software#^f2]] [zig/api] With Zig's planned Io design, code correct under multithreading should also be fine under async. @8:48 (B/fair, verified)
- [[kristoff-zig-new-async-io#^f1]] [zig/api] `Io` is provided by the caller, like `Allocator`, so the program author picks the I/O implementation for all dependencies. (A/strong, verified)
- [[kristoff-zig-new-async-io#^f2]] [zig/api] `io.async` expresses that order doesn't matter; under blocking I/O it runs the function immediately and `await` is a no-op, so the code stays correct. (A/strong, verified)
- [[kristoff-zig-new-async-io#^f7]] [zig/api] `Writer` gains `sendFile` (kernel-side copy) and vectored `drain` with `splat`. (A/strong, verified)
- [[kristoff-zig-new-async-io#^f8]] [zig/api] Library authors need not use async everywhere: sequential code still fits the user's I/O strategy. (A/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-aphorisms#^f1]] [zig/api] For a ziggit question on threading env into `readHistory`, his start is `HistoryOptions { file: []const u8, pub fn from_environment(environment: *const std.process.Environ.Map) HistoryOptions }` and `readHistory(io, gpa, options)`. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-aphorisms#^f2]] [zig/api] Raise the abstraction level: give the thing a name and a type. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-aphorisms#^f3]] [zig/api] Avoid the midlayer mistake: every option user-configurable (easy with public fields); add a shortcut (`from_environment`) that cuts across layers. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-aphorisms#^f5]] [zig/style] [zig/api] Name the configuration parameter `options`, not config, props or params (TigerBeetle). (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-aphorisms#^f6]] [zig/api] Positional DI: dependencies with unique types go positionally with canonical names (`io`, `gpa`); arguments that directly vary behaviour go by name in an Options struct. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-aphorisms#^f7]] [zig/api] Zig is removing ambient IO: environment access is threaded from main. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-async-mutexes#^f1]] [zig/api] TigerBeetle relies on implicit exclusion from a single thread; a callback mutates shared `*Compaction` state across all outstanding IO. (B/fair, verified)
- [[matklad-async-mutexes#^f2]] [zig/api] Callback pattern: recover the parent with `@fieldParentPtr("grid_read", grid_read)`, release the pool slot, assert the stage, advance the stage. (B/fair, verified)
- [[matklad-async-mutexes#^f3]] [zig/api] Explicit locking would collapse to one global lock taken per callback, i.e. back to implicit locking at the event loop. (B/fair, verified)
- [[matklad-cancelation#^f1]] [zig/api] [zig/errors] Synchronous cancelation is control flow: returning an error unwinds and runs cleanup (`defer`). (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-cancelation#^f2]] [zig/api] Asynchronous cancelation is a protocol: request, then wait for acknowledgement, because e.g. a buffer handed to a thread or io_uring must stay tied up until the work ends. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-cancelation#^f3]] [zig/api] CPU work checks cancelation per chunk, not per byte, so the requester waits up to one chunk. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-cancelation#^f4]] [zig/api] TigerBeetle cancels only Grid asynchronously and resets everything above it synchronously, instead of propagating asynchrony up the stack. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-cancelation#^f5]] [zig/api] TigerBeetle is crash-only, no graceful shutdown; if you survive power loss, Quit can be SIGKILL, which also tests that path. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-comptime-orm#^f1]] [zig/api] Newtype ids `pub const ID = enum(u64) { _ };` nested in each struct; same definition, distinct types. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-comptime-orm#^f3]] [zig/api] [zig/memory] Only the call that gets an allocator can allocate, so allocation sites are visible from the call. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-comptime-orm#^f4]] [zig/api] Results into a caller's stack buffer instead of allocating or iterators: `db.transfer.filter(query, &transfers_buffer)` returns a slice of it. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-comptime-orm#^f6]] [zig/comptime] [zig/api] Idiom: pass a group of comptime functions as a `Functions: type` struct (`key_fn`, `key_cmp`), since fn is not an expression. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-comptime-wont-do#^f8]] [zig/comptime] [zig/api] Comptime can create types but can't add methods; an API is always hand written, so serialisation is a top-level `to_json(comptime T, value, writer)`, with opt-in via a `pub const JSONOptions` declaration. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-diagnostics-factory#^f2]] [zig/errors] [zig/api] TigerBeetle tidy.zig has `const Errors = struct` with methods `add_long_line`, `add_banned`, `add_dead_declaration`; call sites call `errors.add_long_line(file, line_index)`. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-diagnostics-factory#^f11]] [zig/api] Principle: an interface has a user side and an implementor side that may want different shapes; one big error enum couples emitting and reporting code. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-error-codes-control-flow#^f8]] [zig/errors] [zig/api] std's Reader declares exact sets: `readSliceAll` returns `error{ReadFailed, EndOfStream}!void`, `readSliceShort` `error{ReadFailed}!usize`. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-error-codes-control-flow#^f10]] [zig/errors] [zig/api] Diagnostic info goes out of band via a sink: std.zon `fromSlice(T, gpa, source, diag: ?*Diagnostics) error{ OutOfMemory, ParseZon }!T`; pass null to switch on the code, pass Diagnostics to show the user. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-frng-gist#^f4]] [zig/api] `Driver.main` takes an `operation: union(enum) { replay: struct {...}, search: struct { attempts: u32 = 100, size_max: u32 = 4 * 1024 * 1024 } }`: modes as a tagged union with defaulted options. (C/fair, verified)
- [[matklad-io-threaded#^f1]] [zig/api] std.Io.Threaded is a "just use threads" Io implementation using blocking syscalls that fully supports cancelation. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-io-threaded#^f2]] [zig/api] Litmus test: concurrency invariably involves cancelation; parallelism is deterministic partitioning. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-io-threaded#^f3]] [zig/api] On POSIX: the canceler sets a shared flag and signals the thread in a loop until acknowledged; on EINTR the cancelee checks the flag and retries or unwinds (`signalCanceledSyscall`, `fileReadPositionalPosix`). (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-io-threaded#^f5]] [zig/api] [zig/errors] Reader and Writer interfaces type erase errors and so support cancelation, which needs extra care, on top of remembering to flush. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-io-threaded#^f6]] [zig/api] Unlike pthread_cancel, cancelation integrates with `try` and `defer`, so cleanup after cancelation is ordinary unwinding. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-io-threaded#^f7]] [zig/api] Io separates "may run concurrently" (`io.async`, never fails) from "must run concurrently" (`io.concurrent`, always fallible, thread pool backed). (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-lovely-syntax#^f9]] [zig/comptime] [zig/api] Generic args are never inferred, but comptime closures keep the annotation burden low: `ArrayListType(u32)` once, then methods close over T. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-lovely-syntax#^f10]] [zig/api] Decl literal usage: `var xs: ArrayListType(u32) = .init(gpa); defer xs.deinit(gpa);`. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-lovely-syntax#^f12]] [zig/api] Options struct with defaults gives named and default arguments for free: `fn exec(argv, options: struct { working_directory: ?[]const u8 = null })`, called `exec(&.{"git","status"}, .{})`; he uses them liberally. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-newtype-index#^f6]] [zig/api] [zig/style] Typed index: `const ItemIndex = enum(u32) { _ };`, a distinct u32 (popularised by Andrew Kelley); no strong encapsulation, anyone can `@enumFromInt`. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-newtype-index#^f11]] [zig/api] Ranges as `index/count` (names align in length); API shape `tree.method(node)` by default since it works with several node arguments. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-push-ifs-up#^f1]] [zig/api] Push ifs up: move a precondition check to the caller and take the non-optional type, enforcing it by type or assert; it goes viral and leaves fewer checks. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-random-numbers-included#^f1]] [zig/api] [zig/test] Random number APIs work best with closed intervals, unlike most APIs which prefer half-open. (B/fair, verified)
- [[matklad-random-numbers-included#^f2]] [zig/api] Closed range can produce the maximum value, e.g. one of the ten largest u32s, without widening. (B/fair, verified)
- [[matklad-random-numbers-included#^f3]] [zig/api] [zig/safety] Exclusive ranges are panicky (an empty range below zero crashes); inclusive pushes `- 1` to the caller, making the non-zero precondition visible: `prng.int_inclusive(u8, replica_count - 1)`. (B/fair, verified)
- [[matklad-static-alloc-constant-work#^f6]] [zig/memory] [zig/api] Constant work: always a fixed number of objects, with a neutral `pub const reserved: Order` value; init is `@memset(orders, .reserved)`. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-test-minimization#^f6]] [zig/api] Return an index, not a pointer, when picking from a slice: avoids const and mut versions and works across parallel arrays (`xs[try frng.index(xs)]`). (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-test-minimization#^f15]] [zig/api] 0.16 juicy main: `pub fn main(init: std.process.Init)` gives gpa, io and args; Zig avoids ambient IO and threads an Io wherever a syscall happens. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-test-minimization#^f17]] [zig/api] Options as inline anonymous structs in signatures, e.g. `run_once(driver, options: struct { size: u32, seed: u64, quiet: bool })` and union(enum) results. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-types-and-zig#^f4]] [zig/api] [zig/comptime] Methods on a generic type have the type parameters in scope, so `slice.reverse()` needs no types; covers about 80% of uses. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-types-and-zig#^f5]] [zig/comptime] [zig/api] Signatures are always explicit: even `bytesAsSlice(comptime T, bytes: anytype)` returns a computed type `BytesAsSliceReturnType(T, @TypeOf(bytes))`, not anytype. (B/strong, verified)
- [[mitchellh-ghostty-and-useful-zig-patterns#^f12]] [zig/build] [zig/api] The same Zig program builds as an exe (Linux, GTK) and as a static lib with a C API that the Swift macOS app calls. (B/fair, verified)
- [[mitchellh-ghostty-gtk-rewrite#^f7]] [zig/safety] [zig/api] Every other issue (dozens) sat on C API boundaries, where lifetime is transferred or blurred; safety there is only as good as the wrapper. (B/strong, verified)
- [[mitchellh-libghostty-is-coming#^f1]] [zig/api] [zig/build] libghostty-vt is the terminal core as a zero-dependency library, not even libc. (B/fair, verified)
- [[mitchellh-libghostty-is-coming#^f2]] [zig/api] The Zig module shipped first; the C API is a separate design job on the same core. (B/fair, verified)
- [[mitchellh-libghostty-is-coming#^f3]] [zig/api] The older internal C header is called a mess made for one consumer; the public API starts clean. (B/fair, verified)
- [[mitchellh-libghostty-is-coming#^f4]] [zig/api] Library and app are versioned separately (build.zig has its own lib_version). (B/fair, verified)
- [[mitchellh-libghostty-is-coming#^f6]] [zig/api] API design is driven by feedback from real consumers, Ghostty being one. (B/fair, verified)
- [[mitchellh-zig-and-swiftui#^f1]] [zig/api] Split: cross-platform logic in Zig as a C-ABI static library, GUI per platform; ~90% of code shared with Linux. (B/fair, verified)
- [[mitchellh-zig-and-swiftui#^f2]] [zig/api] `export fn` gives the C calling convention; only the signature is limited to C types (no comptime params, error sets, odd int widths), the body can use everything. (B/fair, verified)
- [[mitchellh-zig-and-swiftui#^f3]] [zig/api] The C header is written by hand; the consumer (Swift) learns the API only from it. (B/fair, verified)
- [[mitchellh-zig-comptime-tagged-union-subset#^f1]] [zig/api] Exhaustive switch on a tagged union is the safety net: adding a case breaks every switch that misses it. (B/strong, verified)
- [[mitchellh-zig-comptime-tagged-union-subset#^f2]] [zig/api] An `else` prong loses that safety: the compiler can't flag a new tag the function forgot. (B/strong, verified)
- [[mitchellh-zig-comptime-tagged-union-subset#^f3]] [zig/api] Listing ignored tags as no-ops keeps safety but is verbose and lets the signature accept tags it never handles. (B/strong, verified)
- [[mitchellh-zig-comptime-tagged-union-subset#^f4]] [zig/api] Both subsets and the full union are needed: the config parser must parse all actions. (B/strong, verified)
- [[river-repo#^f10]] [zig/api] [zig/memory] Heap objects: `create()` allocates with `util.gpa.create`, `errdefer destroy`, fills `x.* = .{...}`, then registers listeners; the destroy listener frees itself. `river/XdgPopup.zig` (A/strong, verified)
- [[river-repo#^f11]] [zig/api] Intrusive C callbacks: a `wl.Listener(T)` field with a decl-literal default `.init(handler)`; the handler recovers its object with `@fieldParentPtr` (158 uses). `river/XdgPopup.zig` (A/strong, verified)
- [[roc-zig-compiler#^f14]] [zig/perf] [zig/api] Typed index ranges instead of slices: `SafeRange(Idx)` is a start index plus count, stable across reallocation and serialisation where a slice would dangle. `src/collections/safe_list.zig` (B/strong, verified)
- [[seguin-arena-free-nested#^f4]] [zig/api] A library that builds its own arena from the caller's allocator should be given a non-arena parent, and should document how it uses the allocator. (B/fair, verified)
- [[seguin-comptime-config#^f4]] [zig/api] Use it for hooks on a type the user passes anyway; not for normal options, which become rigid. (B/fair, verified)
- [[seguin-decl-literals#^f1]] [zig/api] A decl literal `.init` resolves to a declaration on the result type, as enum literals do for tags. (B/fair, verified)
- [[seguin-decl-literals#^f2]] [zig/api] Several named presets can live on one type. (B/fair, verified)
- [[seguin-decl-literals#^f3]] [zig/api] They shine with generic types, avoiding repeating the long type name. (B/fair, verified)
- [[seguin-decl-literals#^f4]] [zig/api] Field defaults with `.{}` let callers override one field; with `.init` they must assign afterwards. Decl literals help keep invariants that per-field defaults can break. (B/fair, verified)
- [[seguin-decl-literals#^f6]] [zig/api] Checked, not there: decl literals calling functions (`.init(allocator)`) are not covered. (B/fair, absence)
- [[seguin-getorput-string-keys#^f7]] [zig/api] He uses the unmanaged map variant because managed containers were expected to be removed. (B/fair, verified)
- [[seguin-heap-value-address#^f2]] [zig/api] Option one: the caller supplies the storage, `init(..., pool: *Pool)`, common in C and seen in Zig. (B/fair, verified)
- [[seguin-interfaces#^f1]] [zig/api] A runtime interface is a struct of `ptr: *anyopaque` plus function pointers; the implementation casts back with `@ptrCast(@alignCast(ptr))`. (B/fair, verified)
- [[seguin-interfaces#^f2]] [zig/api] With more functions, put the pointers in a `VTable` struct, as `std.mem.Allocator` does. (B/fair, verified)
- [[seguin-interfaces#^f4]] [zig/api] For a closed set of implementations, a tagged union with `inline else` dispatch is simpler; he says use it first. (B/fair, verified)
- [[seguin-interfaces#^f5]] [zig/api] Its limit: third parties can't add implementations. (B/fair, verified)
- [[seguin-interfaces#^f6]] [zig/api] He described the `@fieldParentPtr` interface style as infrequently used (2023). (B/fair, verified)
- [[seguin-learning-zig-coding-in-zig#^f6]] [zig/api] Wrap such cleanup in your own type's `deinit` rather than open-coding it at call sites. (B/fair, verified)
- [[seguin-learning-zig-coding-in-zig#^f7]] [zig/api] 0.15+ `ArrayList` is unmanaged: start from `.empty`, pass the allocator to `append` and `deinit`. (B/fair, verified)
- [[seguin-learning-zig-coding-in-zig#^f8]] [zig/api] 0.16 juicy main hands `main` an `Io` and a general allocator. (B/fair, verified)
- [[seguin-learning-zig-coding-in-zig#^f11]] [zig/api] Before 0.15, `anytype` was the usual way to take a writer; now take `*std.Io.Writer`. (B/fair, verified)
- [[seguin-learning-zig-heap-memory#^f4]] [zig/api] `init` allocating and `deinit` freeing is a std convention, not a language rule; `open`/`close` where they read better. (B/fair, verified)
- [[seguin-learning-zig-heap-memory#^f7]] [zig/api] No default allocator; functions that allocate take one, by convention first. (B/fair, verified)
- [[seguin-learning-zig-heap-memory#^f8]] [zig/api] Two forms: an allocator per call (explicit) or stored at `init` (less explicit, practical for long-lived objects). (B/fair, verified)
- [[seguin-learning-zig-heap-memory#^f9]] [zig/api] Libraries should accept an `Allocator` and let the user choose. (B/fair, verified)
- [[seguin-learning-zig-heap-memory#^f17]] [zig/api] Prefer a caller-supplied buffer (`bufPrint`) over allocating when limits are known. (B/fair, verified)
- [[seguin-learning-zig-heap-memory#^f18]] [zig/api] Or stream to a `*std.Io.Writer` instead of building an allocated string; writers go last in parameter lists, allocators first. (B/fair, verified)
- [[seguin-learning-zig-heap-memory#^f19]] [zig/api] Declaration literals make init readable: `.init` for DebugAllocator, `.empty` for an empty hash map. (B/fair, verified)
- [[seguin-learning-zig-heap-memory#^f20]] [zig/api] Options/config structs with defaults are passed as `.{}` or `.{ .port = 5882 }`. (B/fair, verified)
- [[seguin-learning-zig-language-overview-2#^f7]] [zig/api] Tagged unions serve as closed interfaces when every implementation is known up front. (B/fair, verified)
- [[seguin-learning-zig-pointers#^f3]] [zig/api] Parameters are constants; take `*T` to mutate the caller's value. Method calls take the address automatically. (B/fair, verified)
- [[seguin-learning-zig-pointers#^f4]] [zig/api] He says the compiler may pass a by-value parameter by reference when it can't be mutated. (B/fair, verified)
- [[seguin-learning-zig-stack-memory#^f4]] [zig/api] Simplest fix: the caller supplies the output buffer, so the caller owns its lifetime. (B/fair, verified)
- [[seguin-leveraging-allocators#^f5]] [zig/api] Because handlers see only `std.mem.Allocator`, the allocation strategy changes without touching them. (B/fair, verified)
- [[seguin-leveraging-allocators#^f6]] [zig/api] (inferred) Stale: the vtable shown (`ptr_align: u8`, no `remap`) predates 0.14's `Alignment` type and `remap`. (B/fair, unquoted)
- [[seguin-linkedlist-fieldparentptr#^f2]] [zig/api] Recover the containing struct from the node with `@fieldParentPtr("node", n)`. (B/fair, verified)
- [[seguin-linkedlist-fieldparentptr#^f4]] [zig/api] He finds exposing `@fieldParentPtr` for a list heavy, but says developers should know it. (B/fair, verified)
- [[seguin-new-io-unsafe#^f2]] [zig/api] The requirement is documented per stream (e.g. zstd.Decompress) but a generic consumer can't know it. (C/fair, verified)
- [[seguin-new-io-unsafe#^f4]] [zig/api] Writers must be flushed: his example ends with `flush()`. (C/fair, verified)
- [[seguin-new-writer#^f1]] [zig/api] `std.Io.Writer` carries its buffer; implementations provide `drain(w, data, splat)`. (B/fair, verified)
- [[seguin-new-writer#^f2]] [zig/api] Pass an empty buffer for unbuffered writing. (B/fair, verified)
- [[seguin-new-writer#^f3]] [zig/api] Minimal drain: recover the implementation with `@fieldParentPtr("interface", w)` and write `data[0]`; the Writer calls again for the rest. (B/fair, verified)
- [[seguin-new-writer#^f4]] [zig/api] Convention: a concrete writer (e.g. `File.Writer`) embeds the interface in a field named `interface`; you pass `&w.interface`, and the concrete writer must stay put in memory. (B/fair, verified)
- [[seguin-new-writer#^f5]] [zig/api] `Writer.fixed(buf)` replaces functions that wrote into a `[]u8`. (B/fair, verified)
- [[seguin-new-writer#^f6]] [zig/api] He objects that buffering should be composition (BufferedWriter), not baked into one interface. (B/fair, verified)
- [[seguin-temporaries-const#^f1]] [zig/api] Take `[]const T` when a function doesn't mutate, so callers can pass literals like `&.{1, 2, 3}`. (B/fair, verified)
- [[seguin-writer-allocating-memory#^f3]] [zig/api] For simple appending of bytes he suggests staying with `ArrayList(u8)`. (C/fair, verified)
- [[superhtml-repo#^f17]] [zig/errors] [zig/api] (2026-10-22, Zig practice pass) A `Fatal` error set means "already reported to the user": the reporter writes the diagnostic, then returns `error.Fatal`, so callers only propagate; OOM is composed in with `//`. `src/errors.zig` (B/strong, verified)
- [[tigerbeetle-architecture#^f5]] [zig/memory] [zig/api] Why Zig: no GC needed; Rust's temporal and thread safety matter less under static allocation and one thread; idiomatic Zig containers take the allocator per method, not in the constructor, which fits static allocation. (A/strong, verified)
- [[tigerbeetle-architecture#^f11]] [zig/memory] [zig/api] Async IO without heap-allocated contexts: each component owns fixed arrays of operation structs, linked by `IO` into an intrusive list; total concurrency is the sum of per-component limits. (A/strong, verified)
- [[tigerbeetle-blog-four-fuzzers#^f2]] [zig/test] [zig/api] Fuzzable design starts with minimal interfaces sketched before code; mechanical extraction ossifies accidental dependencies. (A/strong, verified)
- [[tigerbeetle-blog-io-abstraction#^f1]] [zig/api] Don't weave io_uring handling into business logic; schedule IO through a central dispatcher with a callback and context. (B/fair, verified)
- [[tigerbeetle-blog-io-abstraction#^f2]] [zig/api] The caller drives the event loop by calling `run_for_ns`, a flush with a time limit. (B/fair, verified)
- [[tigerbeetle-blog-io-abstraction#^f3]] [zig/api] [zig/memory] Requests that don't fit the ring go to an overflow queue made of intrusive completion structs rather than allocations. (B/fair, verified)
- [[tigerbeetle-blog-io-abstraction#^f4]] [zig/api] Single-threaded userspace IO is simplest, best for non-parallel workloads and needed for deterministic simulation; parallel workloads may prefer per-thread or shared queues. (B/fair, verified)
- [[tigerbeetle-blog-snapshot-testing#^f2]] [zig/test] [zig/api] The primitive is a value, not a macro: a string literal plus its `SourceLocation`, passed to ordinary check functions. (A/strong, verified)
- [[tigerbeetle-code#^f12]] [zig/safety] [zig/api] `Replica.open` starts with a block of argument assertions, including implications (`if (p) assert(...)`), and passes a long options struct field by field. `vsr/replica.zig` (A/strong, verified)
- [[tigerbeetle-code#^f17]] [zig/api] Two arguments of different integer meaning (address u64, checksum u128) go through an anonymous options struct `expect: struct { address, checksum }`. `vsr/grid.zig` (A/strong, verified)
- [[tigerbeetle-code#^f25]] [zig/api] `stdx.cut` (Go's `strings.Cut`) returns a tuple of before and after, destructured at the call site; preferred over indexOf/split chains. `stdx/stdx.zig` (A/strong, verified)
- [[tigerbeetle-code#^f26]] [zig/api] [toolchain] 0.16 std.Io: `crypto_random_bytes(io, buf)` blocks cancellation around `io.randomSecure` and panics if entropy is missing, because `io.random` silently falls back to a predictable seed. `stdx/stdx.zig` (A/strong, verified)
- [[tigerbeetle-code#^f27]] [zig/memory] [zig/api] `BoundedArrayType(T, capacity)`: TigerBeetle's own fixed capacity array (std's is banned); `push` asserts not full rather than returning an error; count stored as u32 behind `count()`. `stdx/bounded_array.zig` (A/strong, verified)
- [[tigerbeetle-code#^f29]] [zig/api] Time is an interface (context pointer plus vtable of monotonic and realtime) with `TimeOS` and `TimeSim` implementations, so the simulator can fake time. TimeOS panics if the monotonic clock regresses. `stdx/time.zig` (A/strong, verified)
- [[tigerbeetle-code#^f30]] [zig/api] IO backend chosen at comptime by OS (io_uring, kqueue, IOCP; epoll for clients on Linux for old kernels and containers); one API. `io.zig` (A/strong, verified)
- [[tigerbeetle-code#^f31]] [zig/api] [zig/memory] IO operations take a caller-owned `*Completion`, a typed comptime callback and context; `erase_types` builds the `*anyopaque` trampoline at comptime, so no allocation per operation. `io/linux.zig` (A/strong, verified)
- [[tigerbeetle-code#^f33]] [zig/api] [toolchain] On 0.16 the custom IO keeps a `std.Io` only for blocking file operations (`aof_blocking_*`); the event loop stays their own io_uring code. `io/linux.zig` (A/strong, verified)
- [[tigerbeetle-code#^f41]] [toolchain] [zig/api] 0.16 entry points take `std.process.Init` (`init.io`, `init.minimal.args`); CLI parsing via their own `stdx.Flags` into a struct with a `@"--"` marker for positionals. `fuzz_tests.zig` (A/strong, verified)
- [[tigerbeetle-tiger-style#^f18]] [zig/api] Don't act directly on external events; run at your own pace and batch, for control of the control flow, performance, and bounded work per period. (A/strong, verified)
- [[tigerbeetle-tiger-style#^f23]] [zig/api] Pass options explicitly at the call site rather than relying on library defaults, against latent bugs when defaults change. (A/strong, verified)
- [[tigerbeetle-tiger-style#^f34]] [zig/api] Options struct whenever arguments can be mixed up; any function taking two `u64` must use one. Singletons (allocator, tracer) go positionally, general to specific. (A/strong, verified)
- [[tigerbeetle-tiger-style#^f38]] [zig/api] [zig/perf] Pass arguments over 16 bytes as `*const` when a copy isn't meant, to catch accidental stack copies. (A/strong, verified)
- [[tigerbeetle-tiger-style#^f39]] [zig/api] [zig/memory] Construct large structs in place via an out pointer (`fn init(target: *T) !void`), for pointer stability and no stack copies; in-place init is viral up the containing struct. (A/strong, verified)
- [[tigerbeetle-tiger-style#^f41]] [zig/api] [zig/errors] Simpler return types reduce branches at every call site: `void` > `bool` > `u64` > `?u64` > `!u64`. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-012-release-notes#^f8]] [zig/api] @fieldParentPtr dropped the parent type argument; the result type supplies it, and `@alignCast` around it is the portable form. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-012-release-notes#^f11]] [zig/api] std.os renamed std.posix; prefer the cross-platform layers over posix. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-012-release-notes#^f13]] [zig/api] `std_options` became an instance of std.Options instead of a struct type with decls, called less error-prone. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-012-release-notes#^f14]] [zig/api] HTTP rework: header buffer always caller-provided (static), removing OutOfMemory from read; a leaky proxy init takes an arena so nothing needs deinit. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-012-release-notes#^f15]] [zig/api] Removing std.http.Headers simplified logic and eliminated failure paths and deinit code. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-013-release-notes#^f1]] [zig/api] Stated direction: types with minimal lifetimes and minimal state with undefined fields; e.g. spawn takes options and returns the Child, instead of set-fields-then-spawn. (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-013-release-notes#^f3]] [zig/api] ComptimeStringMap renamed StaticStringMap(T).initComptime(kvs): one type parameter, a named struct, to cut very long type names; faster by storing KVs as struct of arrays. (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-013-release-notes#^f5]] [zig/api] std.Progress.Node is passed by value; std.Progress is initialised once, in main; lock stderr (std.debug.lockStdErr) around writes. (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-014-release-notes#^f4]] [zig/api] Decl literals: `.foo` resolves to any declaration on the result type, so `const v: S = .default` and `.init(x)`, also with `try`. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-014-release-notes#^f5]] [zig/api] Field defaults that are tied together break invariants when one is overridden; use a named declaration (`empty`, `default`) instead. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-014-release-notes#^f22]] [zig/api] Renames: popOrNull is pop; mem.split/tokenize must pick Scalar, Any or Sequence; std.rand is std.Random; TailQueue is DoublyLinkedList. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-014-release-notes#^f33]] [zig/api] Prefer arrays as parameters over a runtime length assert: utf8Decode2 and friends take arrays, using the type system. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-015-release-notes#^f5]] [zig/api] Mixins: use a zero-bit field plus @fieldParentPtr (`foo.counter.increment()`); the notes add that mixins are rarely needed. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-015-release-notes#^f11]] [zig/api] Writergate: std.io readers and writers deprecated for non-generic std.Io.Reader/Writer with the buffer in the interface; hot paths work on the buffer and the vtable is only called when it is full. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-015-release-notes#^f12]] [zig/api] Motive: the old generic interface poisoned structs and forced `anytype` on every function; the concrete one keeps APIs off raw sockets and files (http.Server no longer depends on std.net). (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-015-release-notes#^f15]] [zig/api] File.Reader/File.Writer memoise size and position and read positionally, so code that seeked on handles should use them instead. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-015-release-notes#^f16]] [zig/api] Use buffering and flush: stdout writer over a caller buffer, `&w.interface`, then `flush()`; consider a global stdout buffer. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-015-release-notes#^f18]] [zig/api] `{f}` is required to call a format method (`{any}` skips it), so adding or removing a format method can no longer silently change `{}` output. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-015-release-notes#^f19]] [zig/api] Format methods are `fn format(self, w: *std.Io.Writer) std.Io.Writer.Error!void`; variants via separate methods, std.fmt.alt or a returned struct. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-015-release-notes#^f20]] [zig/api] New specifiers: {t} for @tagName/@errorName, {b64}; fmt helpers became specifiers ({x}, {B}, {D}). (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-015-release-notes#^f33]] [zig/api] Roadmap: everything that can block moves behind a std.Io instance, passed like an Allocator, so packages are agnostic to the concurrency model. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-016-release-notes#^f1]] [zig/api] Every input and output takes an `Io`; the rule for what belongs behind it: anything that can block control flow or introduce nondeterminism. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-016-release-notes#^f2]] [zig/api] Implementations: Io.Threaded is feature-complete and well tested (Juicy Main's choice); Io.Evented (green threads) is experimental; Io.failing simulates a system with no operations. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-016-release-notes#^f3]] [zig/api] Making an Io locally (Threaded .init_single_threaded) is a stopgap like reaching for page_allocator; accept an Io parameter or store it on a context struct, and let main construct it. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-016-release-notes#^f5]] [zig/api] io.async expresses asynchrony: infallible, and may simply run the call inline; io.concurrent demands concurrency, allocates, and can fail with ConcurrencyUnavailable. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-016-release-notes#^f11]] [zig/api] Thread sync primitives move to Io (Io.Mutex, Io.Event, Io.Group...), so a contended lock blocks a thread or switches stacks per implementation; lock-free code needs no Io. std.once removed: avoid globals. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-016-release-notes#^f14]] [zig/api] Entropy and time are Io: io.random / io.randomSecure, Io.Timestamp replaces time.Instant/Timer; Clock/Duration/Timestamp/Timeout give unit type safety. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-016-release-notes#^f15]] [zig/api] fs moved under Io (std.Io.Dir, std.Io.File, close(io)); called a large but mechanical upgrade. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-016-release-notes#^f16]] [zig/api] process spawning takes options and returns the Child (std.process.spawn, run, replace). (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-016-release-notes#^f17]] [zig/api] Most std.posix and os.windows wrappers removed as a medium-level abstraction: go up to std.Io or down to std.posix.system. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-016-release-notes#^f19]] [zig/api] Juicy Main: `pub fn main(init: std.process.Init) !void` hands over arena, gpa (leak-checked in Debug), io, environ_map and args; Init.Minimal gives raw args and environ. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-016-release-notes#^f20]] [zig/api] Environment variables and args are only available in main; functions take the values or a `*const process.Environ.Map`. Reason: global environ is unsound with threads and was impossible without libc. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-016-release-notes#^f23]] [zig/api] GenericReader, AnyReader, FixedBufferStream deleted: `std.Io.Reader.fixed(data)` and `std.Io.Writer.fixed(buf)`; `{D}` replaced by Io.Duration's format. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-016-release-notes#^f24]] [zig/api] readFileAlloc takes io and a `.limited(n)` limit, and errors when the limit is reached (StreamTooLong). (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-016-release-notes#^f25]] [zig/api] BitSet and EnumSet replace initEmpty/initFull with decl literals. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-017-release-notes#^f28]] [zig/api] `std.fmt.allocPrint(a, ...)` becomes `a.print(...)` on the Allocator; zig fmt --complexity shows it cuts tokens. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-017-release-notes#^f29]] [zig/api] std.zon.parse takes an options struct and allocates results and diagnostics from an arena; `diag.fatal(...)`; updateFrom* variants layer config files. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-clap-repo#^f2]] [zig/comptime] [zig/api] The parameter spec is a help-text string parsed at comptime; the result array has exactly the parsed length, and a bad spec is a compile error with line and column. `clap.zig` (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-clap-repo#^f6]] [zig/comptime] [zig/api] Value parsers are a comptime anonymous struct of functions keyed by value name (`.usize = int(usize, 0)`), looked up with `@field`; users pass their own struct to add types. `clap/parsers.zig`, `clap.zig` (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-clap-repo#^f8]] [zig/errors] [zig/api] Errors carry detail through an optional Diagnostic out-pointer in the options struct; the error union stays plain and the caller reports with `diag.report`. `clap.zig`, `example/simple.zig` (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-clap-repo#^f11]] [zig/api] [zig/memory] Options struct with defaults: the allocator is a required field; the rest have defaults and doc comments. `clap.zig` (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-clap-repo#^f14]] [zig/api] [toolchain] 0.17 main takes `std.process.Init` and uses its `gpa`, `io` and `minimal.args`. `example/simple.zig` (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-clap-repo#^f19]] [zig/api] Layered API: `parse` (OS args, arena) wraps `parseEx` (any iterator), which wraps a streaming parser for callers who want no allocation. `clap.zig` (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-compiler-dod#^f25]] [zig/api] [toolchain] 0.17 code converts enums with `@backingInt`/`@fromBackingInt`; the std uses them 4045 times vs 74 leftover `@intFromEnum` (grep over lib/std). `std/zig/Ast.zig:2925` (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-devlog-2024#^f1]] [zig/api] [zig/safety] Andrew Kelley names integer indexes as distinct non-exhaustive `enum(u32) { _ }` types in all his projects, so the type system catches mixed-up indexes at compile time. "The Unreasonable Effectiveness of Naming Integers" (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-devlog-2024#^f2]] [zig/api] Optional index without `?`: a sibling enum with a `none = maxInt(u32)` tag and `unwrap`/`toOptional` conversions, keeping the index 4 bytes. (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-devlog-2025#^f1]] [zig/api] std.Io connect example: `io.async` plus `Io.Queue` and `Io.Group` race DNS and TCP connects, cancel the losers in `defer`, with no heap allocation (fixed buffers on the stack). "Async DNS Resolution" (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-devlog-2025#^f2]] [zig/api] The same Io code behaves optimally whether Io is threads or an event loop, and still works with single-threaded blocking Io. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-devlog-2025#^f3]] [zig/api] [zig/errors] Design goal: async code reads like normal Zig, with try, defer and while and the usual resource patterns; no function colouring. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-devlog-2025#^f4]] [zig/api] Io convention: `io: Io` is passed as an explicit parameter, like an allocator, and every blocking call takes it (`getOne(io)`, `close(io)`). (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-devlog-2026#^f11]] [zig/api] Io is swappable: the same `fn app(io: std.Io)` runs on `std.Io.Threaded` or `std.Io.Evented` (io_uring, GCD); only main changes. Evented is experimental. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-devlog-2026#^f12]] [zig/api] [zig/memory] Construction pattern in that example: allocator first, then `var threaded: std.Io.Threaded = .init(gpa, .{...})`, `defer threaded.deinit()`, pass `threaded.io()` down. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-devlog-2026#^f18]] [zig/api] std policy on Windows: prefer ntdll native APIs over kernel32, which adds heap allocations, failure modes and bloat. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-a#^f4]] [zig/api] [toolchain] 0.17 Hello World: `main` takes `std.process.Init` and passes `init.io` to the write; I/O needs an explicit `Io`. Hello World (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-a#^f13]] [zig/api] `@"..."` names exist for names that are keywords or invalid identifiers, mainly for linking with external libraries. String Identifier Syntax (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-a#^f24]] [zig/api] Multiple return values: return an anonymous tuple type and destructure at the call site; same for a labelled block yielding two values. Destructuring Tuples (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-a#^f64]] [zig/api] Sentinel pointers `[*:0]const u8` type C strings, catching a missing terminator at compile time. Sentinel-Terminated Pointers (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-a#^f66]] [zig/api] Slicing with comptime-known bounds yields an array pointer, not a slice. Slices (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-a#^f70]] [zig/api] [toolchain] The 0.17 string example formats into a stack buffer with `std.mem.print`; 0.17 std marks `fmt.bufPrint` "Deprecated in favor of `mem.print`" (lib/std/fmt.zig:599, checked in the installed 0.17 std, not this source). Slices (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-a#^f72]] [zig/api] Methods are plain namespaced functions; dot call is sugar for `Type.fn(value, ...)`. The example constructor is `pub fn init(...) T` returning a value. struct (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-a#^f73]] [zig/api] `@fieldParentPtr` recovers the containing struct from a field pointer (the intrusive-node pattern). struct (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-a#^f75]] [zig/api] Field defaults only when omitting the field can't break an invariant; otherwise drop the defaults and offer a named default declaration (`.default`), or an init function taking the runtime values. Faulty Default Field Values (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-a#^f79]] [toolchain] [zig/api] 0.17 converts packed structs, enums and tagged unions to and from integers with `@backingInt` / `@fromBackingInt` (they replace `@intFromEnum` and `@enumFromInt`); checked, not there: part A never uses `@intFromEnum`. packed struct, enum (A/fair, absence)
- [[zig-langref-017-a#^f82]] [zig/api] Packed structs and unions compare with `==`/`!=` on the backing integer. packed struct (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-a#^f87]] [zig/api] Enums, like structs and unions, carry methods as namespaced functions. enum (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-a#^f90]] [zig/api] Enums crossing the C ABI need an explicit tag type (`enum(c_int)`). extern enum (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-a#^f92]] [zig/api] Non-exhaustive enums (`_` field, explicit tag type) model open sets like protocol codes; a `_` prong still forces every named tag to be handled, unlike `else`. Non-exhaustive enum (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-a#^f95]] [zig/api] Tagged unions (`union(enum)`) are the sum type: switch with payload capture, `/*p/` to mutate in place, a second capture for the tag, `void` payloads written as a bare name and built with `.none`. Tagged union (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-a#^f96]] [zig/api] Tagged unions coerce to their tag enum, so `u == .tag` checks work. Tagged union (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-a#^f98]] [zig/api] `opaque {}` gives distinct handle types for C objects whose layout is hidden, so two handles can't be mixed up. opaque (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-a#^f125]] [zig/api] Function pointers are `*const fn (...) T`; function bodies are comptime-only values, pointers can be runtime. Functions (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-a#^f126]] [zig/api] `pub` makes a declaration importable; `export` gives C ABI and external visibility; `extern "lib"` declares a symbol resolved at link or load time; `callconv` sets the calling convention. Functions (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-a#^f128]] [zig/api] Checked, not there: part A has no section on decl literals or result location semantics as such (Result Location Semantics is in part B; no "decl literal" phrase anywhere in the langref); `.empty` appears only in the leak example (^f32). (A/fair, absence)
- [[zig-langref-017-b#^f10]] [zig/errors] [zig/api] Inferred error sets (`!T`) make the function generic: no function pointer, set may differ by target, no recursion. Use an explicit set there, grown from empty by following compile errors. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-b#^f33]] [zig/perf] [zig/api] `void` as a generic argument deletes the field and its code: `AutoHashMap(K, void)` is a set with no value storage. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-b#^f35]] [zig/api] Result location semantics are language spec, not an optimisation detail, and are the main type inference mechanism. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-b#^f36]] [zig/api] [zig/perf] Anonymous initialisers write fields straight into the destination, which avoids copies and matters for pinned types. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-b#^f38]] [zig/api] Typed initialisers `T{ .a = x }` do not forward the result location; anonymous `.{ .a = x }` with a result type does. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-b#^f45]] [zig/comptime] [zig/api] A generic type is a function returning an anonymous struct; Zig names it after the call (`List(i32)`); assign to a const to name a type. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-b#^f73]] [zig/memory] [zig/api] No default allocator: functions that allocate take an `Allocator`. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-b#^f74]] [zig/memory] [zig/api] Allocator choice 1: libraries accept an Allocator and let the user decide. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-b#^f81]] [zig/memory] [zig/api] Document ownership and lifetime of every returned pointer ("caller owns the returned memory"); e.g. ArrayList `.items` dies on the next resize. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-b#^f84]] [zig/style] [zig/api] A file with top-level fields is a type: TitleCase file name and `const Name = @This();` at the top. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-b#^f86]] [zig/api] Root-file hooks: `std_options: std.Options` (e.g. logFn, segfault handler) and `panic` (e.g. `std.debug.FullPanic(myPanic)` keeps formatted safety panics). (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-b#^f87]] [zig/api] [toolchain] 0.17 main can take `std.process.Init`, which carries args, an arena and preopens. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-learn-overview#^f13]] [zig/api] [toolchain] The 0.17 examples take `main(init: std.process.Init)` and get `io` from it; Io.Dir.cwd().openFile takes `io` first. (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-learn-samples#^f2]] [zig/api] [zig/memory] The generic Queue sample stores its allocator in a field named `gpa` and takes it in `init`. (A/weak, verified)
- [[zig-learn-samples#^f3]] [zig/api] [toolchain] 0.17 hello world writes via `Io.File.stdout().writeStreamingAll` with the `io` from `std.process.Init`. (A/weak, verified)
- [[zig-learn-why#^f2]] [zig/memory] [zig/api] No hidden allocations, because hidden allocation limits where code can be reused. (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-learn-why#^f3]] [zig/memory] [zig/api] Convention: every std feature that heap allocates takes an `Allocator` parameter. (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-repo-docs#^f13]] [zig/api] Accept `*Io.Writer` to write reusable output code; return `Io.Writer.Error!void`. `lib/init/src/root.zig:7` (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-repo-docs#^f14]] [zig/api] [zig/memory] main gets `std.process.Init`: `init.arena` for process-lifetime allocations, `init.io` for I/O; stdout through a stack buffer and `Io.File.Writer`, then flush. `lib/init/src/main.zig:10` (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-repo-docs#^f15]] [zig/api] stdout is for program output only; debug messages go to stderr via `std.debug.print`. `lib/init/src/main.zig:22` (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-std-allocators#^f1]] [zig/api] The interface is a file-as-struct with two fields, a type-erased `ptr` and a `*const VTable` of function pointers; the module doc comment is `//!`. `std/mem/Allocator.zig:1` (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-std-allocators#^f2]] [zig/api] The vtable is small (alloc, resize, remap, free) and each entry documents preconditions; convenience API (create, alloc, dupe, free) lives as methods on the interface, not in the vtable. `std/mem/Allocator.zig:24` (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-std-allocators#^f3]] [zig/api] Raw vtable calls are `pub inline` but documented as implementer-only, so wrappers can forward without a user-facing convenience layer. `std/mem/Allocator.zig:144` (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-std-allocators#^f4]] [zig/api] Implementations expose `pub fn allocator(self: *T) Allocator` building the interface with an anonymous vtable literal; each vtable fn recovers `self` with `@ptrCast(@alignCast(ctx))`. `std/heap/FixedBufferAllocator.zig:19` (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-std-allocators#^f6]] [zig/api] The vtable entries return `?[*]u8` or `bool`; the error union (`Error = error{OutOfMemory}`) is added by the wrapper methods. `std/mem/Allocator.zig:14` (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-std-allocators#^f9]] [zig/api] [toolchain] `allocPrint` is deprecated in 0.17; `Allocator.print` builds the string through `Io.Writer.Allocating` and maps error.WriteFailed to OutOfMemory. `std/mem/Allocator.zig:506` (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-std-allocators#^f18]] [zig/api] Options struct with defaults that depend on build mode, passed by value to `init(backing, options)`; `deinit` returns the leak count instead of a Check enum. `std/heap/SafeAllocator.zig:621` (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-std-allocators#^f23]] [zig/api] [toolchain] 0.17 `main` can take `std.process.Init`: a process-lifetime threadsafe arena, a gpa with leak checking in Debug, an `Io`, the environment map and preopens; `Init.Minimal` gives only args and environ. `std/process.zig:24` (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-std-containers#^f1]] [zig/api] In 0.17 `std.ArrayList(T)` is the unmanaged list: it stores no allocator, every allocating method takes `gpa: Allocator` after `self`. `std/std.zig:52` (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-std-containers#^f2]] [zig/api] [toolchain] The allocator-storing list survives only as deprecated `array_list.Managed`; `ArrayListUnmanaged` is a deprecated alias. `std/array_list.zig:10` (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-std-containers#^f3]] [zig/api] Containers initialise with a `pub const empty: Self` decl literal, used as `var list: ArrayList(i32) = .empty;`; default field init is deprecated. `std/array_list.zig:655` (A/strong, verified)

- [[zig-std-containers#^f17]] [zig/api] [toolchain] 0.17 array hash maps live under `std.array_hash_map` as `Auto`, `String` and `Custom`; the top-level `AutoArrayHashMapUnmanaged` names are deprecated aliases. `std/std.zig:39` (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-std-containers#^f18]] [zig/api] ArrayHashMap keeps insertion order and is the choice when iteration matters; its doc says it stores no allocator and is cheap for few entries. `std/array_hash_map.zig:47` (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-std-containers#^f21]] [zig/api] getOrPut returns pointers plus `found_existing` and leaves the value undefined for the caller to fill: one lookup for insert-or-update. `std/array_hash_map.zig:296` (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-std-io#^f1]] [zig/api] `std.Io` is one interface for all I/O and concurrency (files, net, processes, time, randomness, async/concurrent/cancel, sync primitives), passed as a value like an Allocator. `std/Io.zig:1` (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-std-io#^f2]] [zig/api] Io has the same shape as Allocator: `userdata: ?*anyopaque` plus `vtable: *const VTable`; implementations are Threaded and per-OS Evented backends. `std/Io.zig:15` (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-std-io#^f6]] [zig/api] `async` allows a blocking single-threaded Io; `concurrent` demands real concurrency and can fail with error.ConcurrencyUnavailable. Pick the weaker one unless you need concurrency. `std/Io.zig:2561` (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-std-io#^f7]] [zig/api] `Io.Group` runs an unordered task set awaited or cancelled as a whole; `Group.init` is a decl literal, await and cancel are idempotent. `std/Io.zig:1327` (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-std-io#^f8]] [zig/api] Io.Writer is a concrete struct holding the buffer (`buffer`, `end`) plus a vtable; the vtable is only reached when the buffer cannot take the data. `std/Io/Writer.zig:14` (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-std-io#^f10]] [zig/api] VTable entries have defaults (`sendFile = unimplementedSendFile`, `flush = defaultFlush`), so an implementation fills only `drain`. `std/Io/Writer.zig:71` (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-std-io#^f13]] [zig/api] Implementations embed the interface as a field named `interface` (or `writer`) and recover themselves with `@alignCast(@fieldParentPtr("interface", io_w))`. `std/Io/File/Writer.zig:88` (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-std-io#^f15]] [zig/api] `Writer.fixed(buf)` writes into a caller buffer and returns WriteFailed when full: the no-allocation formatting sink. `std/Io/Writer.zig:124` (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-std-io#^f18]] [zig/api] [toolchain] Custom formatting in 0.17 is `pub fn format(self, w: *Writer) Writer.Error!void` chosen with `{f}`; `{t}` prints enum and error tag names; `{any}` the default format. `std/Io/Writer.zig:688` (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-std-io#^f19]] [zig/api] Io.Reader also holds its buffer (`buffer`, `seek`, `end`); `peek` returns a view into it, `toss` advances, `take` is both; returned slices die on the next peek. `std/Io/Reader.zig:16` (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-std-io#^f22]] [zig/api] `takeDelimiter` returns `?[]u8`, null at end: the line-iteration idiom `while (try r.takeDelimiter('\n')) /line/`. `std/Io/Reader.zig:917` (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-std-io#^f24]] [zig/api] `Io.Limit` is a non-exhaustive `enum(usize)` with named `nothing` and `unlimited` values: a typed integer instead of a bare usize with magic values. `std/Io.zig:730` (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-std-io#^f25]] [zig/api] Files give their stream through `file.writer(io, &buffer)`; the caller owns the buffer, positional mode is preferred for thread safety. `std/Io/File.zig:593` (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-std-io#^f26]] [zig/api] Fixed-buffer and failing implementations are exported constants (`Writer.failing`, `Io.failing`, `Reader.fixed`), giving test doubles for free. `std/Io/Writer.zig:140` (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-std-io#^f27]] [zig/api] [toolchain] Checked, not there in 0.17 lib/std (grep): `std.io` (lowercase), `getStdOut`, `AnyWriter`, `GenericWriter`, `usingnamespace`; stdout is `File.stdout().writer(io, &buf)` and the interface is `*Io.Writer`. (A/strong, absence)
- [[zig-std-json-fmt-args#^f1]] [zig/api] ParseOptions: every field defaulted and doc-commented, so callers pass `.{}` and override one field; inline anonymous enum for a 3-way choice. `std/json/static.zig:16` (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-std-json-fmt-args#^f2]] [zig/api] Missing JSON fields are handled by struct field defaults, not options. `std/json/static.zig:17` (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-std-json-fmt-args#^f8]] [zig/api] The scanner stores `diagnostics: ?*Diagnostics = null`, so diagnostics cost nothing unless enabled. `std/json/Scanner.zig:61` (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-std-json-fmt-args#^f10]] [zig/api] Init variants are named by input shape (`initStreaming`, `initCompleteInput`), and docs say what the allocator is for. `std/json/Scanner.zig:63` (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-std-json-fmt-args#^f13]] [zig/api] [toolchain] `fmt.bufPrint` and `fmt.allocPrint` are deprecated in 0.17 for `mem.print` and `Allocator.print`. `std/fmt.zig:597` (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-std-json-fmt-args#^f14]] [zig/api] `fmt.alt(value, .method)` picks a format method other than `format` for `{f}`, so a type can have several renderings without wrapper types. `std/fmt.zig:283` (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-std-json-fmt-args#^f17]] [zig/api] [toolchain] 0.17 args: `process.Args` holds the vector from main's Init; `iterate()` on POSIX, `iterateAllocator(gpa)` for portable code, `toSlice(arena)` requires an arena. `std/process/Args.zig:453` (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-talk-async-io-zigtoberfest#^f1]] [zig/api] Setting up an Io implementation is like setting up an allocator, done once in main; example code is then the same for every implementation. @2:23 (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-talk-async-io-zigtoberfest#^f2]] [zig/api] In Zig, async means decoupling the call of a function from its return; await is idempotent. @3:24 (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-talk-async-io-zigtoberfest#^f5]] [zig/api] cancel is the same as await plus a cancellation request; both idempotent, so a `defer cancel` after an await is a no-op. Cancellation is a request, not a guarantee. @14:14 (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-talk-async-io-zigtoberfest#^f9]] [zig/api] Asynchrony is not concurrency: a producer/consumer pair under `io.async` deadlocks with one thread; use `io.concurrent`, which can fail with ConcurrencyUnavailable instead of deadlocking. Prefer async when you can. @33:14 (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-talk-async-io-zigtoberfest#^f10]] [zig/api] `io.async` cannot fail: if the task cannot be allocated it runs the function immediately, which async permits. @36:44 (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-talk-async-io-zigtoberfest#^f11]] [zig/api] `Io.Group` is the arena of tasks: async into it, wait or cancel as a whole; finished task resources are freed as they finish, so a server can spawn one per client without leaking. @46:01 (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-talk-async-io-zigtoberfest#^f13]] [zig/api] Io is a fat pointer (pointer plus vtable), the same interface shape as Allocator. @50:06 (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-talk-async-io-zigtoberfest#^f14]] [zig/api] [zig/memory] Pattern from the compiler: a context struct holds gpa and io as fields and functions take the context first; free functions take io and allocator directly. @50:51 (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-talk-async-io-zigtoberfest#^f16]] [zig/api] Timeouts belong in the operation (e.g. receive with timeout) so the code still works single-threaded without concurrency. @53:28 (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-talk-async-io-zigtoberfest#^f17]] [zig/api] Contribution stance: bring use cases from real code, not speculation. @57:06 (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-talk-kelley-dod#^f5]] [zig/api] [zig/safety] Warning: bare integer indexes lose the type safety pointers had; use distinct types where the language allows. @15:18 (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-talk-kelley-flush#^f2]] [zig/api] Every concurrency model makes scheduling decisions; even single-threaded blocking code is a static schedule. @7:19 (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-talk-kelley-flush#^f3]] [zig/api] Goal: write logic agnostic to the concurrency model; only the Io implementation changes. Logic that needs concurrency (a multi-client server) is fundamentally not portable to blocking Io. @9:14 (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-talk-kelley-flush#^f4]] [zig/api] Io is passed like an Allocator: anything that can block takes an Io parameter. @11:27 (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-talk-kelley-flush#^f5]] [zig/api] Benefit: a function without an Io parameter does no I/O, so purity is visible in the signature; the caller controls effects. @12:22 (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-talk-kelley-flush#^f6]] [zig/api] [zig/test] Further benefits listed: freestanding reuse, leak checking for file descriptors and sockets, race testing at the Io layer, faking Io for unit tests, custom scheduling, cancellation in userland. @14:26 (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-talk-kelley-flush#^f9]] [zig/api] [zig/perf] Keep hot paths above the vtable: fields and methods of the interface struct can be inlined; vtable function pointers are opaque to the optimiser. @21:42 (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-talk-kelley-flush#^f10]] [zig/api] [zig/perf] Putting the buffer in the interface (not the implementation) turned per-byte indirect calls into a register op plus a store, while the same machine code still serves any sink. @24:07 (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-talk-kelley-flush#^f11]] [zig/api] The trade-off: more logic in the interface means less flexibility; don't use interfaces or function pointers unless you gain reuse. @25:00 (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-talk-kelley-flush#^f12]] [zig/api] Survey: C, Go, Rust and C++ all buffer below the vtable; Kelley calls the Stack Overflow advice to accept Go's io.Writer in reusable functions the worst code. @30:06 (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-talk-kelley-flush#^f13]] [zig/api] Interface features he landed on: vectored writes and splats, file-to-file transfer (sendfile), discarding input, reader streaming straight into a writer. @31:54 (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-talk-kelley-flush#^f14]] [zig/api] Giving vtable functions access to all state including the buffer handles compression without an internal ring buffer. @31:08 (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-talk-kelley-flush#^f15]] [zig/api] Implementing a stream: use short reads and writes (even returning 0) to keep implementations simple; pick buffer sizes from the protocol (TLS: max frame) and at the endpoint trade a few cache lines for fewer syscalls. @35:10 (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-talk-kelley-flush#^f16]] [zig/api] Naming: he finds reader/writer ambiguous (a writer reads your buffer) and prefers source/sink; the shipped std kept Reader and Writer. @19:20 (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-talk-roadmap-2026#^f5]] [zig/api] Io joins Allocator as a parameter you pass everywhere; anything that can block the thread, including large CPU tasks, belongs in it. @1:01:39 (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-talk-roadmap-2026#^f6]] [zig/api] Library code encodes what can run in parallel; the caller picks the Io implementation, as the caller picks the allocator. @1:04:14 (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-talk-roadmap-2026#^f7]] [zig/api] Writer and Reader moved from generic (`GenericWriter`) to one non-generic struct with a vtable: the same machine code serves every stream. @1:18:02 (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-talk-roadmap-2026#^f8]] [zig/api] Stream transforms (compression) take a reader and give a writer and need no Io at all; only endpoint implementations (files, sockets) take an Io. @1:20:11 (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-talk-roadmap-2026#^f9]] [zig/api] Allocator stays out of the Io interface: a blocking, eager Io implementation needs no allocation, so it would be inappropriate there. @1:58:40 (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-talk-roadmap-2026#^f10]] [zig/api] No syntax sugar for vtable interfaces, on purpose: hand-built interfaces allow fields (the buffer) above the vtable, which traits forbid. @2:08:43 (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-talk-roadmap-2026#^f11]] [zig/api] Creating an interface should be annoying and rare: the friction is appropriate. @2:10:42 (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-talk-roadmap-2026#^f12]] [zig/api] Interfaces that need shared state (a mutex) keep the state and fast path in the interface, calling the vtable only on contention. @1:53:41 (A/strong, verified)
- [[ziggit-arraylist-unmanaged-015#^f1]] [zig/api] In 0.15 `ArrayListUnmanaged` became `ArrayList`; the managed one is `std.array_list.Managed`, slated for removal. (B/fair, verified)
- [[ziggit-arraylist-unmanaged-015#^f2]] [zig/api] Reasons: smaller struct for fields, explicit about which calls allocate, one implementation, static init with `initBuf`. (B/fair, verified)
- [[ziggit-arraylist-unmanaged-015#^f4]] [zig/api] squeek502: the unmanaged list is not going away, it is renamed. (B/fair, verified)

- [[zls-repo#^f11]] [zig/api] `io` and `allocator` are passed explicitly as leading parameters through the whole call tree, `io` first. `src/main.zig` (A/fair, verified)
- [[zls-repo#^f12]] [zig/api] Long-lived heap objects use `create(options) !*T` / `destroy()`, with an options struct whose fields carry doc comments on their contracts (thread safety); value types use `init`/`deinit`. `src/Server.zig` (A/fair, verified)

- [[zls-repo#^f27]] [zig/perf] [zig/api] Typed indices: `enum(u32) { _ }` for handles into arrays, with a separate OptionalIndex using maxInt as `none` instead of `?u32` (no extra tag byte). 0.17 converts with `@fromBackingInt`/`@backingInt`. `src/DocumentScope.zig` (A/fair, verified)
- [[zls-repo#^f37]] [zig/api] Vtable interface done by hand the std way: a struct with `ptr: *anyopaque` and `vtable: &.{...}`, implementations cast back with `@ptrCast(@alignCast(ctx))`. `src/testing.zig` (A/fair, verified)

### zig/build: build.zig, modules, packages, cross-compilation, C interop (241)

- [[ak-bun-rust-rewrite#^f5]] [zig/build] [zig/perf] LTO was supported all along (it was on by default until LLVM bugs). (B/fair, verified)
- [[ak-bun-rust-rewrite#^f6]] [zig/build] [zig/perf] Compile speed reference: the 600k-line Zig compiler builds from clean in 16 s and rebuilds an edit in 90 ms with incremental. (B/fair, verified)
- [[bun-repo#^f14]] [zig/build] [zig/perf] Zig was one compilation unit: a debug build took about 2.5 minutes on Zig changes, much slower than C++ which is many units. `CONTRIBUTING.md` (A/fair, verified)
- [[ghostty-repo#^f82]] [zig/build] build.zig delegates to a `src/build/` package: one file per artifact (GhosttyExe, GhosttyLib, GhosttyLibVt...) plus custom steps. `src/build/main.zig:1-3` (A/strong, verified)
- [[ghostty-repo#^f83]] [zig/build] One `Config` struct gathers every `-D` option and target tweak; artifacts take `*const Config` and `*const SharedDeps`. `build.zig:21-23`, `src/build/GhosttyExe.zig:13` (A/strong, verified)
- [[ghostty-repo#^f84]] [zig/build] Config writes itself into a `build_options` module; app code rebuilds it via `fromOptions()` and maps generated enums back with `stringToEnum(@tagName)`. `src/build/Config.zig:684-802` (A/strong, verified)
- [[ghostty-repo#^f85]] [zig/build] A comptime check fails the build unless the Zig major.minor matches exactly, reading the minimum from build.zig.zon. `src/build/zig.zig:4-17`, `build.zig:13-18` (A/strong, verified)
- [[ghostty-repo#^f86]] [zig/build] All `b.step` calls sit at the top of build() so the list of steps documents itself. `build.zig:61-63` (A/strong, verified)
- [[ghostty-repo#^f87]] [zig/build] Platform libraries are lazy dependencies, fetched only when the target needs them. `src/build/SharedDeps.zig:234` (A/strong, verified)
- [[ghostty-repo#^f88]] [zig/build] [toolchain] The 0.16 module model requires every file to be in exactly one module per compile, so a module shared with a dependency is created once eagerly. `src/build/SharedDeps.zig:24-59` (A/strong, verified)
- [[ghostty-repo#^f89]] [zig/build] The test binary uses a baseline CPU, Debug, frame pointers and LLVM; `-Dtest-filter` is a repeatable list option. `build.zig:44-48,379-392` (A/strong, verified)
- [[ghostty-repo#^f90]] [zig/build] Being a dependency is detected (`dep_prefix`) and switches the defaults to the lib-only build. `src/build/Config.zig:86-93` (A/strong, verified)
- [[ghostty-repo#^f91]] [zig/build] C exports happen only when this file is the root, so Zig-module consumers don't get the symbols. `src/lib_vt.zig:174-177` (A/strong, verified)

- [[ghostty-repo#^f94]] [zig/build] The Valgrind run step rebuilds the exe for a baseline CPU, since Valgrind can't handle newer instructions. `build.zig:331-336` (A/strong, verified)
- [[ghostty-repo#^f95]] [zig/build] An `.Exe`/`.Lib`/wasm artifact kind is detected at comptime from builtin, and an unsupported mode fails with @compileLog plus @compileError. `src/build_config.zig:73-98` (A/strong, verified)
- [[kristoff-comptime-sqlite#^f5]] [zig/build] Code generation in a build step can beat comptime for readability: output you can goto-definition into. (B/strong, verified)
- [[kristoff-fix-your-asserts#^f5]] [zig/build] Build mode can differ per dependency, and per block with `@setRuntimeSafety`. (A/strong, verified)
- [[kristoff-snapshot-testing#^f3]] [zig/build] `zig build test` rebuilds each fixture into its committed `snapshot/`, then `git add` and `git diff --cached --exit-code` fail the step on any change. (B/strong, verified)
- [[kristoff-snapshot-testing#^f5]] [zig/build] Prefer `b.addRunArtifact` to running `zig build` as a subprocess; Zine is an exception. (B/strong, verified)
- [[kristoff-talk-better-software#^f4]] [zig/build] Gold standard: on any machine `zig build` just works; minimise and document system dependencies. @14:44 (B/fair, verified)
- [[kristoff-talk-better-software#^f5]] [zig/build] `zig init`'s root.zig plus main.zig hint: libraries ship a CLI, programs expose reusable logic as a module. @8:01 (B/fair, verified)
- [[kristoff-zls-experience#^f1]] [zig/build] ZLS can't resolve complex comptime, so type errors need the compiler. (B/fair, verified)
- [[kristoff-zls-experience#^f2]] [zig/build] Add a `check` step that builds the same artifact without installing it; that passes `-fno-emit-bin`, so analysis runs and codegen is skipped. (B/fair, verified)
- [[kristoff-zls-experience#^f3]] [zig/build] Point ZLS at it with `enable_build_on_save` and `build_on_save_step: check`. (B/fair, verified)
- [[matklad-comptime-wont-do#^f9]] [zig/comptime] [zig/build] No IO at comptime: evaluation is hermetic and cacheable; generate code that needs IO through build.zig and `@import` it. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-mechanical-habits#^f9]] [zig/build] Avoid multiplying entry points (O(1) build file). (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-o1-build-file#^f1]] [zig/build] Build or CI config size should be mostly independent of project size: a new test shouldn't need a new build line or CI line. (B/fair, verified)
- [[matklad-o1-build-file#^f2]] [zig/build] Enumerate O(1) entry points: run, test, fuzz, deploy; consider folding lint and fmt into test. (B/fair, verified)
- [[matklad-o1-build-file#^f3]] [zig/build] Use filters (`--test-filter=tidy`) rather than opt-in steps. (B/fair, verified)
- [[matklad-o1-build-file#^f4]] [zig/build] Few artifacts: a production and a test build; subcommands or multicall binaries instead of related binaries, which also ships debug tools to production. (B/fair, verified)
- [[matklad-o1-build-file#^f5]] [zig/build] The rule's value: pause whenever you add to build.zig. (B/fair, verified)
- [[matklad-zig-and-rust#^f14]] [zig/build] Bundle a pinned `./zig` per project and drive automation in Zig rather than shell scripts. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-zig-version-manager#^f1]] [toolchain] [zig/build] TigerBeetle puts the right Zig version in `./zig` at the repo root and runs `./zig/zig build test`, not zig from PATH. (B/fair, verified)
- [[mitchellh-ghostty-and-useful-zig-patterns#^f12]] [zig/build] [zig/api] The same Zig program builds as an exe (Linux, GTK) and as a static lib with a C API that the Swift macOS app calls. (B/fair, verified)
- [[mitchellh-libghostty-is-coming#^f1]] [zig/api] [zig/build] libghostty-vt is the terminal core as a zero-dependency library, not even libc. (B/fair, verified)
- [[mitchellh-libghostty-is-coming#^f5]] [zig/build] Further pieces will be a family of small libs to keep dependencies and code size down. (B/fair, verified)
- [[mitchellh-simdutf-no-libcxx#^f1]] [zig/build] Dropping libc++ makes a library more portable (embedded, wasm, freestanding), cross-compiles simpler, and shrinks binaries. (B/strong, verified)
- [[mitchellh-simdutf-no-libcxx#^f2]] [zig/build] libghostty-vt with SIMD off has zero dependencies, not even libc; SIMD brought in C++ via simdutf. (B/strong, verified)
- [[mitchellh-simdutf-no-libcxx#^f3]] [zig/build] Hidden runtime deps show only in object symbols, so a script decompiles objects and CI checks for `__cxa_*` and similar. (B/strong, verified)
- [[mitchellh-zig-and-swiftui#^f4]] [zig/build] Static libraries don't carry their C dependencies, so a custom LibtoolStep merges them into one archive. (B/fair, verified)
- [[mitchellh-zig-and-swiftui#^f5]] [zig/build] Platform tools (libtool, lipo, xcframework) are wrapped as custom build steps in build.zig. (B/fair, verified)
- [[mitchellh-zig-and-swiftui#^f6]] [zig/build] He picked old, stable system tools because they are unlikely to be brittle. (B/fair, verified)
- [[mitchellh-zig-build-internals#^f1]] [zig/build] `zig build` compiles build.zig into a cached build binary, then runs it; the build binary shells out to the compiler for each artifact. (B/fair, verified)
- [[mitchellh-zig-build-internals#^f2]] [zig/build] build() only declares steps and dependencies; nothing runs until the runner executes the graph. (B/fair, verified)
- [[mitchellh-zig-build-internals#^f3]] [zig/build] Pitfall: reading a file a step generates must happen inside a step's make function, not in build(). (B/fair, verified)
- [[mitchellh-zig-build-internals#^f4]] [zig/build] Steps over files that exist before the build should be created in build() so the graph is complete. (B/fair, verified)
- [[mitchellh-zig-build-internals#^f5]] [zig/build] build.zig is ordinary Zig compiled for the host, so anything is possible there. (B/fair, verified)
- [[mitchellh-zig-builds-getting-faster#^f1]] [zig/build] [toolchain] Compiling Ghostty's build.zig itself fell from 7.2s (0.14) to 1.7s (0.15), measured by `zig build --help`. (B/fair, verified)
- [[mitchellh-zig-builds-getting-faster#^f2]] [zig/build] A well-written build script rebuilds itself rarely; its cost is paid by every fresh source build. (B/fair, verified)
- [[mitchellh-zig-builds-getting-faster#^f3]] [zig/build] [toolchain] Full uncached build 41s to 32s, still mostly LLVM because the self-hosted x86_64 backend couldn't build all of Ghostty yet. (B/fair, verified)
- [[mitchellh-zig-builds-getting-faster#^f4]] [zig/build] [toolchain] A one-line change rebuilt libghostty-vt in 0.98s on the self-hosted backend, against 2.9s before. (B/fair, verified)
- [[mitchellh-zig-builds-getting-faster#^f5]] [zig/build] (inferred) Keeping a library-sized module that builds on the self-hosted backend gives a sub-second edit loop; he cites staying in flow. (B/fair, verified)
- [[mitchellh-zig-builds-getting-faster#^f6]] [zig/build] [toolchain] Incremental compilation wasn't functional in 0.15.1; rebuilds still recompile the module. (B/fair, verified)
- [[mitchellh-zig-comptime-conditional-disable#^f7]] [zig/test] [zig/build] He builds both sides of every comptime switch in CI because the failure mode is silent. (B/strong, verified)
- [[naskos-talk-comptime#^f4]] [zig/build] Q&A: generating a Zig file in a build step is easier to inspect than comptime-generated structures (stack traces, reading the output). @29:55 (C/fair, verified)
- [[river-repo#^f18]] [zig/build] C interop on 0.16: C headers go through the translate-c package's `Translator` from build.zig (a module named "c" from c.h), not `@cImport`. `build.zig`, `river/c.h` (A/strong, verified)
- [[river-repo#^f19]] [zig/build] [zig/errors] C varargs callbacks are handled by a small C shim compiled into the exe (`addCSourceFile`) that formats into a buffer and calls an `export fn` in Zig. `river/wlroots_log_wrapper.c`, `river/main.zig` (A/strong, verified)
- [[river-repo#^f20]] [zig/build] Generated Zig bindings: the zig-wayland Scanner generates protocol code at build time into a module; each interface version pinned in build.zig with a comment on why stale versions are harmless. `build.zig` (A/strong, verified)
- [[river-repo#^f21]] [zig/build] Version string from build.zig.zon imported as a module, plus `git describe` for -dev builds; build options exported with `addOptions`. `build.zig` (A/strong, verified)
- [[roc-zig-compiler#^f6]] [zig/test] [zig/build] Zig collects tests lazily: `refAllDecls(@This())` is one level and pub-only, an unreferenced import adds nothing, so a test file can silently never run.
