Roc checks every `test` decl appears in some test binary's metadata list. `ci/check_test_wiring.zig` (B/strong, verified)
- [[roc-zig-compiler#^f17]] [zig/test] [zig/build] Tests run through custom build steps (`zig build run-test-zig -- --test-filter`), not `zig test`; a `zig build -Dno-bin -fincremental --watch` loop type-checks without emitting binaries for fast feedback. `AGENT.md`, `src/README.md` (B/strong, verified)
- [[roc-zig-compiler#^f19]] [zig/build] build.zig defines named check steps per rule (unreachable rationale, test wiring, `@enumFromInt(0)` ban, panic ban in interpreter, format, lints) so each runs alone; a `minici` step runs them all. `ci/zig_lints.zig`, `AGENT.md` (B/strong, verified)
- [[seguin-learning-zig-coding-in-zig#^f13]] [zig/build] (inferred) Stale for 0.17: `"a" ** 85` (array `**` removed in 0.17), `b.createModule("calc", .{...})` (createModule takes only options), and a dummy `.hash` to learn the real one (`zig fetch --save` writes it). (B/fair, unquoted)
- [[seguin-learning-zig-coding-in-zig#^f17]] [zig/build] He admits not understanding the build system well enough to explain it. (B/fair, verified)
- [[superhtml-repo#^f24]] [zig/build] build.zig split into `setupXStep` helper functions (test, check, fuzz, CLI, wasm); `test` depends on `check` so the CLI must compile before tests run. `build.zig` (B/strong, verified)
- [[tigerbeetle-architecture#^f4]] [zig/build] No dependencies beyond Linux (io_uring), the Zig compiler and parts of std; usefulness of dependencies falls with project lifetime; Zig's std APIs suit static allocation. (A/strong, verified)
- [[tigerbeetle-architecture#^f14]] [zig/build] Dev loop: pinned Zig downloaded into the repo (`./zig/download.ps1`, then `./zig/zig build`); `zig build test -- <filter>`; `zig build fuzz -- smoke`; `zig build check` to compile without emitting a binary; `zig build ci` mirrors CI. `docs/internals/HACKING.md` (A/strong, verified)
- [[tigerbeetle-blog-automation#^f1]] [zig/build] Automation lives in the main repo (`./src/scripts`) and in the project language, so every developer can find, change and deploy it. (B/fair, verified)
- [[tigerbeetle-blog-automation#^f2]] [zig/build] Cost of Zig over bash is verbosity, closed by a helper library; gains are no context switch, no sed dialects, no ShellCheck. (B/fair, verified)
- [[tigerbeetle-blog-automation#^f3]] [zig/build] Auto-deploy: deployed automation is a tiny loop that clones main, fetches the pinned Zig, builds and runs for 30 minutes, then repeats. (B/fair, verified)
- [[tigerbeetle-code#^f1]] [toolchain] [zig/build] build.zig pins an exact Zig version at comptime and fails with a clear message on any other. `build.zig` (A/strong, verified)
- [[tigerbeetle-code#^f2]] [toolchain] [zig/build] Pinned 0.16.0 is downloaded per repo by `zig/download.sh` with SHA-256 checksums per platform. `zig/download.sh` (A/strong, verified)
- [[tigerbeetle-code#^f3]] [toolchain] [zig/build] On 0.16.0 they force the LLVM backend for every compile step because the self-hosted x86_64 backend miscompiles; `-Dllvm=false` opts out for quicker Debug builds. `build.zig` (A/strong, verified)
- [[tigerbeetle-code#^f4]] [zig/build] [zig/safety] Default optimise mode is ReleaseSafe, not ReleaseFast: safety checks stay on in shipped builds. `build.zig` (A/strong, verified)
- [[tigerbeetle-code#^f5]] [zig/build] All top-level steps are declared in one struct literal with descriptions, and all `-D` options in another, before any wiring; build helpers take an anonymous `options: struct {...}`. `build.zig` (A/strong, verified)
- [[tigerbeetle-code#^f6]] [zig/build] `build.zig` imports nothing from src/ to keep build-script compile time low. `build.zig` (A/strong, verified)
- [[tigerbeetle-code#^f7]] [zig/build] `zig build ci` is a build step with modes (smoke, test, aof, default, all); each sub-run is a child `zig build` with a `max_rss` budget, stderr hidden unless it fails; the default runs fmt check, `check`, tests, `fuzz -- smoke` and the VOPR for both state machines. `build.zig` (A/strong, verified)
- [[tigerbeetle-code#^f8]] [zig/build] [zig/test] Test filters come straight from `zig build test -- <args>` via `.filters = b.args`. `build.zig` (A/strong, verified)
- [[tigerbeetle-how-we-use-zig#^f3]] [zig/build] Build steps can declare an expected memory ceiling so the build runner limits parallelism. @21:30 (C/weak, verified)
- [[tigerbeetle-tidy#^f9]] [zig/build] Dead files: because Zig compiles lazily, a file nothing imports is never checked; tidy greps `@import("x.zig")` and flags never-imported files except listed entry points. `src/tidy.zig` (A/strong, verified)
- [[tigerbeetle-tidy#^f12]] [zig/build] Repo hygiene tests: no blob over 256 KiB in history (three named exceptions), only expected executable bits and file extensions, changelog line length and no `?si=` tracking params. `src/tidy.zig` (A/strong, verified)
- [[tigerbeetle-tiger-style#^f17]] [zig/build] All compiler warnings at the strictest setting from day one. (A/strong, verified)
- [[tigerbeetle-tiger-style#^f46]] [zig/build] Zero dependencies apart from the Zig toolchain: supply chain, safety, performance, install time. (A/strong, verified)
- [[tigerbeetle-tiger-style#^f47]] [zig/build] Scripts in Zig (`scripts/*.zig`), not shell, for portability and type safety across the team. (A/strong, verified)
- [[tigerbeetle-tiger-style-talk#^f2]] [zig/build] Avoid dependencies chosen for popularity; TigerBeetle was "painted" from scratch because dependencies would slow testing and add risk. @16:34 (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-011-release-notes#^f10]] [zig/build] A source file may belong to only one module; the team calls the shape a likely mistake. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-011-release-notes#^f14]] [zig/build] Build system and package manager stop being experimental; "package" now means a hashed directory tree, "module" the importable unit. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-011-release-notes#^f15]] [zig/build] Dependencies live in build.zig.zon, separate from build.zig, so fetching needs no build script run and tools can see the graph without running untrusted code. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-011-release-notes#^f16]] [zig/build] Packages take target and optimize as options (standardTargetOptions, standardOptimizeOption) and options are not inherited by dependencies. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-011-release-notes#^f17]] [zig/build] Use LazyPath for every input and output, not hardcoded paths. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-011-release-notes#^f18]] [zig/build] Prefer Run steps over custom steps, for cache integration; addTest no longer runs the test, addRunArtifact does. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-011-release-notes#^f19]] [zig/build] Build steps run in parallel on a thread pool sized to CPU cores (-j). (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-012-release-notes#^f18]] [zig/build] Lazy dependencies are fetched only when used; the notes call always using lazyDependency probably best practice. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-012-release-notes#^f20]] [zig/build] b.path replaces `.{ .path = ... }` and LazyPath.relative, so lazy paths from dependencies carry their owner and avoid footguns. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-012-release-notes#^f21]] [zig/build] System package mode: `systemIntegrationOption`, `--system`, `-fsys=`, and `--release` honour the project's preferred_optimize_mode. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-012-release-notes#^f22]] [zig/build] installHeader adds headers to the compile step, not the top-level install step. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-013-release-notes#^f7]] [zig/build] `zig-cache` renamed `.zig-cache` (ephemeral, hidden from editors); `zig-out` unchanged. (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-013-release-notes#^f8]] [zig/build] A Run step that inherits stdio has side effects, always runs, and takes a global lock. (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-013-release-notes#^f9]] [zig/build] Windows DLLs install to bin/ by default, matching CMake. (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-014-release-notes#^f25]] [zig/build] `--watch` keeps the build runner alive and reruns dirty steps; debounce 50ms. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-014-release-notes#^f26]] [zig/build] Compile steps take a `root_module` built with createModule, instead of root_source_file/target/optimize directly, so module graphs are clearer and reusable (e.g. a test step per module). (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-014-release-notes#^f27]] [zig/build] addLibrary(.{ .linkage }) replaces addStaticLibrary/addSharedLibrary. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-014-release-notes#^f28]] [zig/build] Incremental compilation opt-in with -fincremental; suggested workflow is a `-Dno-bin` option plus --watch for fast compile errors (14s then 63ms on 500k lines). (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-014-release-notes#^f30]] [zig/build] ZON imports at comptime with a result type: `const foo: Foo = @import("foo.zon")`; std.zon.parse/stringify at runtime. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-015-release-notes#^f27]] [zig/build] Deprecated root_source_file/target/optimize fields on addExecutable removed; root_module is required. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-015-release-notes#^f28]] [zig/build] The team's advice for large projects: `--watch -fincremental` with a `-Dno-bin` option for compile errors. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-015-release-notes#^f29]] [zig/build] `--time-report` in the build web UI shows which files and declarations are slow to analyse and compile. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-015-release-notes#^f30]] [zig/build] Self-hosted x86_64 backend is the Debug default (about 5x faster compiles, slower machine code; `-fllvm` or use_llvm to opt out). (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-015-release-notes#^f32]] [zig/build] `zig init` template shows a reusable module plus an executable; the old static library template misled newcomers. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-016-release-notes#^f27]] [zig/build] @cImport deprecated: put includes in a .h file and use b.addTranslateC, imported as a module, or the translate-c package. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-016-release-notes#^f35]] [zig/build] Packages fetch into project-local zig-pkg/ (not committed, usually), to make dependencies easy to read and tinker with; `--fork=path` overrides a package for a session; fingerprint and enum-literal name required. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-016-release-notes#^f37]] [zig/build] RemoveDir step and makeTempPath removed (configure phase is the wrong place); use b.addTempFiles / tmpPath, cleaned up by the runner. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-016-release-notes#^f38]] [zig/build] Incremental now works with the LLVM backend and the new ELF linker; the team encourages `zig build -fincremental --watch` despite known bugs. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-016-release-notes#^f39]] [zig/build] With the new ELF linker, a `-Dno-bin` step brings little benefit (stale 0.14/0.15 advice for ELF self-hosted builds). (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-017-release-notes#^f33]] [zig/build] Configure cache poisoning: build.zig logic with side effects (findProgram) defeats configuration caching; declare inputs with dependOnFileContents etc., or use findProgramLazy. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-017-release-notes#^f34]] [zig/build] Option paths must say file, directory or untracked (addOptionPath, addOptionPathDirectory, addOptionPathUntracked). (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-017-release-notes#^f35]] [zig/build] dependencyLazy returns error.LazyDependencyNeeded so build code can `try`. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-017-release-notes#^f36]] [zig/build] Incremental now usable for most x86_64-linux projects: `zig build -fincremental --watch`. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-017-release-notes#^f38]] [zig/build] The 0.17 break of ZLS: the maker/configurer split stops ZLS working with 0.17 until the Build Server Protocol catches up. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-clap-repo#^f17]] [zig/build] build.zig exposes the module with `b.addModule`, tests that module, builds examples in a loop, and emits autodocs from the test artifact (`getEmittedDocs`). `build.zig` (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-clap-repo#^f18]] [zig/build] Examples are compiled as part of the default `all` step, so README code cannot rot without breaking the build. `build.zig` (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-devlog-2024#^f7]] [zig/build] `zig build --watch` reruns invalidated steps on any file-system input change (macOS and BSDs from Oct 2024). (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-devlog-2024#^f8]] [zig/build] [toolchain] Incremental compilation in 2024 needed `use_llvm = false` and was buggy (stale; see [[zig-devlog-2026]] for LLVM incremental). (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-devlog-2025#^f6]] [zig/build] Fast dev loop the creator uses: `--watch -fincremental` on a native-only test step; errors appear on save. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-devlog-2025#^f7]] [zig/build] [zig/test] Add a build option that narrows the test matrix to native for inner-loop work (`-Dno-matrix`). (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-devlog-2025#^f13]] [zig/build] [zig/perf] Self-hosted x86_64 backend default for Debug on Linux/macOS from 0.15: hello world 918 ms to 275 ms (70%), compiler 75 s to 20 s. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-devlog-2025#^f14]] [zig/build] Parallel codegen on self-hosted backends: 5% to 50% faster wall time; the LLVM backend stays single-threaded. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-devlog-2025#^f16]] [zig/build] Loris Cro's build-system video shows exposing a module from a package and importing it from another project (lead, [[zig-learn-build-system]] lacks this). (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-devlog-2026#^f1]] [zig/build] [toolchain] build.zig now compiles into a small Debug "configurer" whose graph is serialised and cached; a Release "maker" runs it. Goal: faster `zig build`. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-devlog-2026#^f2]] [zig/build] [zig/perf] Measured: `zig build --help` 150 ms to 14.3 ms (90.4%) because the cached configuration skips rerunning build.zig. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-devlog-2026#^f3]] [zig/build] [toolchain] `if (b.args) /args/ run_cmd.addArgs(args)` becomes `run_cmd.addPassthruArgs()`: build scripts can no longer observe run args, so changing them no longer rebuilds the script. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-devlog-2026#^f4]] [zig/build] (inferred) Design consequence for build.zig authors: configure-phase logic must be a pure function of options and files it observes; the result is cached. (from the serialised, cached configuration) (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-devlog-2026#^f5]] [zig/build] Package management now lives in the maker (ReleaseSafe), so fetching runs with safety checks and is patchable without rebuilding the compiler. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-devlog-2026#^f6]] [zig/build] Fetched packages go to `zig-pkg/` next to build.zig; the team highly recommends git-ignoring it; a filtered, recompressed copy sits in the global cache. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-devlog-2026#^f7]] [zig/build] `zig build --fork=<path>` overrides a package across the whole tree, ephemerally, for fixing ecosystem breakage without editing build.zig.zon. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-devlog-2026#^f8]] [zig/build] Same fingerprint and equal version with different hashes is now an error (fork without renaming, unbumped version, or unreleased changes): bump the version when a package changes. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-devlog-2026#^f9]] [zig/build] Naked (no build.zig.zon) dependencies can declare `version` and `paths` to filter files and get stable, readable hashes (zlib 4.3M to 1.2M). (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-devlog-2026#^f10]] [zig/build] New `zig build --print-tree` (package tree) and `--configure`; `--fetch` now fetches all including lazy deps. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-devlog-2026#^f17]] [zig/build] Incremental works with LLVM since 0.16; the team has used `-fincremental --watch` daily for about a year and calls it relatively stable. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-devlog-2026#^f19]] [zig/build] zig libc: libc functions are moving to Zig std wrappers sharing one compilation unit, which removes redundant code across the libc boundary. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-b#^f83]] [toolchain] [zig/build] `builtin.os`, `builtin.cpu`, `builtin.abi`, `builtin.object_format` are deprecated (removed in 0.18) for `builtin.target.*`. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-b#^f85]] [zig/test] [zig/build] Only the root module's tests run, and only from files that are discovered; reference test files with `test { _ = @import("tests.zig"); }`. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-b#^f88]] [zig/build] The langref defers build-system docs to an external page; its examples use `root_module = b.createModule(...)`, `b.addLibrary(.{ .linkage = .dynamic })` and `exe.root_module.addCSourceFile` / `linkLibrary`. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-b#^f89]] [zig/build] Without `-target` the binary targets the host CPU and is not portable. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-b#^f90]] [zig/build] Avoid C pointers `[*c]T` outside generated bindings; use `*T`/`[*]T`/optional. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-b#^f91]] [zig/build] translate-c must get the same `-target` and cflags as the real build, or ABI mismatches follow. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-b#^f92]] [zig/build] Untranslatable C demotes to opaque, extern or @compileError, and only errors if used (lazy analysis). (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-b#^f93]] [zig/build] [toolchain] Checked, not there: the 0.17 C chapter has no @cImport section; C headers come in through the `zig translate-c` CLI. C `void` is `anyopaque`. (A/strong, absence)
- [[zig-langref-017-b#^f94]] [zig/build] Exporting a C ABI library is a primary use case: `export fn`, build-lib, mixed object files. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-b#^f95]] [zig/build] Browser/node wasm: freestanding executable, `-fno-entry --export=name`. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-b#^f110]] [zig/build] Inline asm uses AT&T syntax on x86 and its constraints are unstable. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-learn-build-system#^f1]] [zig/build] Use the build system when the command line gets unwieldy, there are many steps, options, target-dependent logic, dependencies, or to avoid cmake/make/python. (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-learn-build-system#^f2]] [zig/build] The build is a DAG of steps run independently and concurrently; the default step is Install, which starts empty, so a build script must add what to install. (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-learn-build-system#^f3]] [zig/build] Never hardcode output paths: the user picks the prefix; hardcoding breaks caching, concurrency and composability. (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-learn-build-system#^f4]] [zig/build] `.zig-cache` is not for source control and can be deleted at any time. (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-learn-build-system#^f5]] [zig/build] Run convenience step pattern: `b.addRunArtifact(exe)` then `b.step("run", ...).dependOn(&run_exe.step)`. `build-system` "Adding a Convenience Step" (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-learn-build-system#^f6]] [zig/build] `b.option` makes the script configurable for end users and for projects that depend on it as a package; options appear in `zig build --help` automatically. (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-learn-build-system#^f7]] [zig/build] Prefer `standardTargetOptions` and `standardOptimizeOption` over hand-rolled options: they set the naming convention (-Dtarget, -Doptimize). (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-learn-build-system#^f8]] [zig/build] No release mode is the default preference: the user must choose to make a release build. (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-learn-build-system#^f9]] [zig/build] [zig/comptime] Pass build config into code with `b.addOptions()` and `root_module.addOptions("config", options)`; the values are comptime-known in the code, so `@compileError` can gate on them. (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-learn-build-system#^f10]] [zig/build] Every artifact is built with `.root_module = b.createModule(.{...})`; the page never shows `b.addModule`. (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-learn-build-system#^f11]] [zig/build] Declaring an artifact costs nothing unless a requested step depends on it: the DAG builds only what is needed. (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-learn-build-system#^f12]] [zig/build] [zig/test] Tests are two steps, Compile and Run; without `addRunArtifact` the tests are built but never run. (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-learn-build-system#^f14]] [zig/build] [zig/test] Test across targets by looping over `std.Target.Query` values and set `skip_foreign_checks = true` so foreign-target tests skip instead of failing. (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-learn-build-system#^f16]] [zig/build] Get libraries through the build system (packages) by default: reproducible, every OS, cross-compiles, exact versions; system libraries are for distro packagers. (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-learn-build-system#^f17]] [zig/build] Prefer a Zig tool in the source tree over a system tool (jq) for generating files: system dependencies make the project harder to build. (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-learn-build-system#^f18]] [zig/build] Generated outputs flow as LazyPaths: `addOutputFileArg` on a Run step, then `addAnonymousImport` to import generated Zig or embed a generated asset. (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-learn-build-system#^f19]] [zig/build] Build tools for the host (`b.graph.host`), not the user's target. (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-learn-build-system#^f20]] [zig/build] Commit generated files only with UpdateSourceFiles behind an explicit step; never during the normal build (caching and concurrency bugs). (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-learn-build-system#^f21]] [zig/build] [toolchain] Stale: the UpdateSourceFiles example passes `.root_source_file` straight to `addExecutable` and uses `std.fs.cwd()` and `std.io.getStdIn()`, pre-0.15 API, and shows no 0.17 CI output. (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-learn-build-system#^f22]] [zig/build] Cross-compiled releases: loop over target queries with `b.resolveTargetQuery`, install each into its own `zigTriple` subdirectory. (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-learn-build-system#^f23]] [zig/build] Checked, not there: no section on `b.addModule` vs `b.createModule`, `b.dependency`, build.zig.zon, lazy dependencies or exposing artifacts to dependants; the system libraries section points to a "Package Management" section the page lacks. (A/fair, absence)
- [[zig-learn-build-system#^f24]] [zig/build] [toolchain] The 0.17 help lists `--fork` to override projects in the dependency tree, `--test-timeout`, `-fincremental` and `--fuzz[=limit]`. (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-learn-getting-started#^f1]] [zig/build] [toolchain] Tagged releases suit projects with dependencies; dev builds are for people helping develop Zig. (A/weak, verified)
- [[zig-learn-getting-started#^f2]] [zig/build] `zig init` creates build.zig, build.zig.zon, src/main.zig and src/root.zig: the default split of an executable root and a library root. (A/weak, verified)
- [[zig-learn-overview#^f15]] [zig/build] [toolchain] Stale: the C interop and build system examples use `@cImport` (removed in 0.17), `callconv(.C)` and `std.build.Builder` with `addSharedLibrary`. (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-learn-overview#^f16]] [zig/build] Cross-compiling needs no separate toolchain; Zig ships libc sources for many targets so `-lc` needs no system files; musl allows static linking. (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-learn-overview#^f17]] [zig/build] Non-debug build modes are reproducible. (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-learn-overview#^f18]] [zig/build] System integration is explicit: `--system` disables fetching and enables `-fsys` options so packagers and upstream cooperate. (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-learn-samples#^f4]] [zig/build] [toolchain] Stale: the cURL sample still uses `@cImport` and the managed `std.ArrayList(u8).init(arena)`, neither valid on 0.17. (A/weak, verified)
- [[zig-learn-why#^f6]] [zig/build] The build system and package manager are meant to replace cmake/make and system package managers for developers, even for pure C/C++ projects. (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-repo-docs#^f1]] [zig/build] build.zig only mutates a graph that an external runner executes, which is what lets it parallelise and cache. `lib/init/build.zig:3` (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-repo-docs#^f2]] [zig/build] Default to `standardTargetOptions(.{})` and `standardOptimizeOption(.{})` with no preferred release mode; the user decides. `lib/init/build.zig:14` (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-repo-docs#^f3]] [zig/build] Modules are the preferred way to share Zig code; `b.addModule` needs a name because a package can expose several. `lib/init/build.zig:26` (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-repo-docs#^f4]] [zig/build] [zig/style] A module's root file is its public surface: re-export from it what consumers need. `lib/init/build.zig:32` (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-repo-docs#^f5]] [zig/build] The template splits a library module (src/root.zig) from the CLI (src/main.zig) and says to delete the split if neither part needs the other. `lib/init/build.zig:44` (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-repo-docs#^f6]] [zig/build] `b.createModule` defines a module without exposing it, so it needs no name; use it for executable root modules. `lib/init/build.zig:63` (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-repo-docs#^f7]] [zig/build] Target and optimize must be wired explicitly into the root module of every executable or library (or hardcoded, e.g. firmware). `lib/init/build.zig:67` (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-repo-docs#^f8]] [zig/build] Imports are named by the importer: `.imports = &.{ .{ .name = ..., .module = mod } }`, renamable to avoid collisions. `lib/init/build.zig:75` (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-repo-docs#^f9]] [zig/build] Run step pattern for 0.17: `addRunArtifact`, depend on the install step so it runs from the prefix, `addPassthruArgs()` for `zig build run -- args`. `lib/init/build.zig:105` (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-repo-docs#^f10]] [zig/build] [zig/test] A test executable tests one module, so the template makes one `addTest` per module and one `test` step depending on both run steps, which run in parallel. `lib/init/build.zig:119` (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-repo-docs#^f11]] [zig/build] The build system is userland and simple: watch the subcommand invocations and read its source to master it. `lib/init/build.zig:145` (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-repo-docs#^f12]] [zig/style] [zig/build] root.zig is the conventional root source file of a package. `lib/init/src/root.zig:1` (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-repo-docs#^f18]] [zig/build] `name` should not contain "zig" (redundant inside the Zig package namespace). `doc/build.zig.zon.md:20` (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-repo-docs#^f19]] [zig/build] `fingerprint` never changes; regenerate it when forking a maintained project, else the fork is hostile. `doc/build.zig.zon.md:29` (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-repo-docs#^f20]] [zig/build] `hash` is the source of truth, `url` only a mirror; change the url, delete the hash. `doc/build.zig.zon.md:81` (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-repo-docs#^f21]] [zig/build] `lazy = true` fetches a dependency only if used; `path` deps skip hashing. `doc/build.zig.zon.md:100` (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-repo-docs#^f22]] [zig/build] `paths` rule of thumb: files needed to compile plus licences; only listed files are hashed and kept. `lib/init/build.zig.zon:66` (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-repo-docs#^f23]] [zig/build] `minimum_zig_version` is advisory only. `doc/build.zig.zon.md:65` (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-repo-docs#^f24]] [zig/build] [zig/test] The compiler's build.zig derives options from options (`skip-release` defaults to `no-matrix`) so one flag narrows the test matrix. `build.zig:108` (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-repo-docs#^f25]] [zig/build] Enum-typed and list-typed build options: `b.option(IoMode, ...)`, `b.option([]const []const u8, "test-filter", ...)` passed to each test step. `build.zig:203` (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-repo-docs#^f26]] [zig/build] [zig/style] A `fmt` step (rewrite) and a `test-fmt` step (`.check = true`) over the same path lists; `test` depends on the check. `build.zig:466` (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-repo-docs#^f27]] [zig/build] Helper functions take `b` plus an options struct (`addCompilerMod(b, options)`) to share module setup between the exe and its tests. `build.zig:890` (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-repo-docs#^f28]] [zig/build] [zig/perf] Memory budgets as build facts: `max_rss` on the compiler step, "should never be raised". `build.zig:907` (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-std-json-fmt-args#^f19]] [zig/build] Build API takes options structs with a required `root_module`; the executable is built from a module, not a root source file. `std/Build.zig:559` (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-std-json-fmt-args#^f21]] [zig/build] [toolchain] 0.17 build: configure phase serialises to a `Configuration` for a separate make phase. `std/Build.zig:24` (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-talk-build-system-intro#^f1]] [zig/build] The build system is userland code that invokes compiler subcommands; read the CLI invocations (`--verbose`) to debug. @0:29 (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-talk-build-system-intro#^f2]] [zig/build] build.zig describes a graph, it does not perform the build; that is what buys dependency tracking, parallelism and caching. @0:29 (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-talk-build-system-intro#^f3]] [zig/build] A package can expose modules, static and dynamic libraries, objects, executables, any source file, and files generated on demand. @1:27 (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-talk-build-system-intro#^f4]] [zig/build] build.zig.zon `paths` lists the package's files; anything not listed is deleted after download (keep test data out). @3:09 (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-talk-build-system-intro#^f5]] [zig/style] [zig/build] Convention: sources under `src/`, a module's entry point named `root.zig`; consumers see only the root file, so re-export from it. @4:39 (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-talk-build-system-intro#^f6]] [zig/build] `b.addModule(name, ...)` registers a named module in the graph that dependants can get by name, even if the return value is discarded. @5:55 (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-talk-build-system-intro#^f7]] [zig/build] Use `b.createModule` for modules you don't want to expose, such as an executable's root module; add registers in the graph, create does not, and the build API uses add/create with that meaning often. @7:21 (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-talk-build-system-intro#^f8]] [zig/build] Split business logic (an exposed library module) from the CLI (its own unexposed root module importing the library), to keep CLI-only deps away from consumers. @9:09 (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-talk-build-system-intro#^f9]] [zig/build] Import names are set by the importer and can rename modules; the module's registered name is only the lookup key. @8:14 (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-talk-build-system-intro#^f10]] [zig/build] Without target and optimize options an executable can't be cross-compiled or release-built by users. @9:09 (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-talk-build-system-intro#^f11]] [zig/build] Installing is explicit because some executables only run inside the build pipeline or behind a flag. @10:22 (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-talk-build-system-intro#^f12]] [zig/build] `.imports = ...` in createModule and `mod.addImport(...)` are equivalent; addImport suits conditional or circular imports. @14:08 (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-talk-build-system-intro#^f13]] [zig/build] Share Zig code as modules, not static libraries; static libraries are for C ABI interop (the 0.14 zig init template misled people). @13:18 (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-talk-build-system-intro#^f14]] [zig/build] `b.dependency(name, .{ .target = ..., .optimize = ... })` passes the dependency's own -D options; `dep.module("json")` fetches its named module. @14:08 (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-talk-build-system-intro#^f15]] [zig/build] `zig fetch --save git+https://...` pins a ref to a commit hash in the zon. @12:26 (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-talk-build-system-intro#^f16]] [zig/build] [toolchain] Stale for 0.17 detail: setting `root_source_file` directly on addExecutable was deprecated then in favour of an explicit root module (now the only way). @7:21 (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-talk-kelley-build-from-source#^f1]] [zig/build] The install prefix is the key choice; pick one in your home folder and you never need root. @13:06 (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-talk-kelley-build-from-source#^f2]] [zig/build] Multiple prefixes compose like collections of software. @14:36 (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-talk-kelley-build-from-source#^f3]] [zig/build] When building from source, use the latest bug-fix release, not the dev branch, and stick to the default toolchain path. @19:34 (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-talk-kelley-build-from-source#^f4]] [zig/build] mtime-based caching (make) is unreliable; zig build samples file-system timestamp granularity and treats risky mtimes as misses. @30:01 (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-talk-kelley-build-from-source#^f5]] [zig/build] zig build uses all cores by default, unlike make's opt-in -j. @31:32 (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-talk-kelley-build-from-source#^f6]] [zig/build] Package hashes are of the unpacked, filtered files, so the URL is just a mirror. @35:21 (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-talk-kelley-build-from-source#^f7]] [zig/build] Dependencies are declared in the zon (declarative) and only those can be used from build.zig (imperative), so tools can know the possible set. @36:51 (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-talk-kelley-build-from-source#^f8]] [zig/build] Goal of zig build: one command, one dependency (Zig) on every OS, to make contributing easy. @38:21 (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-talk-kelley-tagged-union#^f6]] [zig/build] Shipped: self-hosted x86 backend default, own linker, incremental compilation, cross-compilation including arbitrary glibc versions. @44:05 (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-talk-roadmap-2026#^f15]] [zig/build] Incremental compilation front end works; binary emission was blocked on linker work in mid 2025 (stale: works with LLVM since 0.16, [[zig-devlog-2026#^f17]]). @42:52 (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-talk-roadmap-2026#^f16]] [zig/build] [toolchain] A pure-Zig translate-c (on Aro) replaces the clang-based one; part of removing clang, LLD and LLVM dependencies. @50:08 (A/strong, verified)
- [[zls-repo#^f1]] [toolchain] [zig/build] Targets Zig 0.17.0 as both build and runtime minimum; the default branch follows Zig master and the ZLS version must match Zig. `build.zig.zon`, `README.md` (A/fair, verified)
- [[zls-repo#^f2]] [zig/build] Dependencies pinned by archive URL of a commit plus content hash; heavy optional ones (tracy) are `.lazy = true` and fetched only when enabled via `b.lazyDependency`. `build.zig.zon`, `build.zig` (A/fair, verified)
- [[zls-repo#^f3]] [zig/build] build.zig factors module construction into helper functions taking an anonymous options struct, so the release loop over 12 targets and the normal build share one definition. `build.zig` (A/fair, verified)
- [[zls-repo#^f4]] [zig/build] Generated code (version data from the langref, Config.zig and schema.json) is produced by a host-built tool run from build.zig; `zig build gen` copies outputs back into the source tree with `addUpdateSourceFiles`. `build.zig` (A/fair, verified)
- [[zls-repo#^f5]] [zig/build] Root-only steps (gen, release, test) are guarded by `b.isRoot()` so ZLS as a dependency exposes only the module; the module is registered with `b.modules.put`. `build.zig` (A/fair, verified)
- [[zls-repo#^f6]] [zig/build] A `check` step compiles without installing, for fast editor feedback; `-Dtest-filter` is a repeatable option passed to every test artifact. `build.zig` (A/fair, verified)
- [[zls-repo#^f7]] [zig/memory] [zig/build] Build-script OOM is not handled: `catch @panic("OOM")` in build.zig only (4 uses in all of src/ and build.zig). `build.zig` (A/fair, verified)

### zig/comptime: comptime, generics, type functions, anytype, reflection (196)

- [[ak-bun-rust-rewrite#^f4]] [zig/comptime] [zig/perf] The team warned Bun about comptime abuse for years and built the time report (0.15) to audit comptime and inline cost on compile times. (B/fair, verified)
- [[bun-in-rust#^f9]] [zig/comptime] [zig/perf] Too much comptime: the port cut the binary by 3.8 to 6.8 MB per platform, largely because of comptime overuse in the Zig code. (B/strong, verified)
- [[bun-in-rust#^f10]] [zig/comptime] Comptime format strings let `Output.pretty` rewrite colour markers before arguments are substituted, which Rust could not do. (B/strong, verified)
- [[ghostty-repo#^f38]] [zig/comptime] Backends are picked at comptime from build options, so each binary has exactly one apprt and no vtable. `src/apprt.zig:39-50` (A/strong, verified)
- [[ghostty-repo#^f39]] [zig/comptime] The renderer is a generic over a GraphicsAPI type (Metal or OpenGL); optional capabilities are probed with `@hasDecl`, falling back to void. `src/renderer.zig:37-42`, `src/renderer/generic.zig:96` (A/strong, verified)
- [[ghostty-repo#^f40]] [zig/comptime] Duck-typed Context parameter with documented required and optional methods; the optional one is called only if `@hasDecl`. `src/datastruct/cache_table.zig:20-28,97` (A/strong, verified)
- [[ghostty-repo#^f41]] [zig/comptime] Stream(H) calls `handler.vt(comptime action, value)` where the value type depends on the action, so handlers switch on a comptime tag and dispatch is static; tests define a tiny handler inline. `src/terminal/stream.zig:3952` (A/strong, verified)
- [[ghostty-repo#^f42]] [zig/comptime] The CLI/config parser walks struct fields at comptime and calls a type's `parseCLI` hook, picking the call shape by its parameter count. `src/cli/args.zig:311,339-346` (A/strong, verified)
- [[ghostty-repo#^f43]] [zig/comptime] Optional capabilities of the destination struct are detected by name: `_arena`, `_diagnostics`, `compatibility`, `help`, `parseManuallyHook`. `src/cli/args.zig:67,90,100` (A/strong, verified)
- [[ghostty-repo#^f44]] [zig/comptime] `changed(comptime key)` finds the field at comptime; the change iterator turns a runtime index into comptime via `inline` switch prongs. `src/config/Config.zig:5301-5343` (A/strong, verified)
- [[ghostty-repo#^f45]] [zig/comptime] Generic code ends with `@compileLog(T)` plus `@compileError` for unsupported types, so the error names the type. `src/config/Config.zig:5285-5288` (A/strong, verified)
- [[ghostty-repo#^f46]] [zig/comptime] [toolchain] Types are built with the 0.17 builtins `@Union`, `@Enum`, `@Int` (no `@Type`), with the field lists read through `field_names`/`field_types`. `src/apprt/action.zig:446-466`, `src/lib/enum.zig:43`, `src/terminal/page.zig:2419` (A/strong, verified)
- [[ghostty-repo#^f47]] [zig/comptime] Comptime-generated bit masks over packed structs turn multi-field questions into one AND and compare, which vectorises. `src/terminal/page.zig:2428-2440` (A/strong, verified)
- [[ghostty-repo#^f48]] [zig/comptime] `comptime` blocks assert ABI sizes and alignment invariants. `src/apprt/action.zig:475-484`, `src/terminal/page.zig:158-166` (A/strong, verified)
- [[ghostty-repo#^f49]] [zig/comptime] A tripwire module compiles to nothing outside tests: its check functions use the inline calling convention when disabled. `src/tripwire.zig:86-90,219-223` (A/strong, verified)
- [[ghostty-repo#^f50]] [zig/comptime] An error set can be derived from a function type at comptime. `src/tripwire.zig:116-124` (A/strong, verified)
- [[ghostty-repo#^f96]] [zig/comptime] [toolchain] The tagged-union subset from [[mitchellh-zig-comptime-tagged-union-subset]] at 0.17: filter the union's SoA field lists, then build the union and its tag with `@Union` and `@Enum`. `src/input/Binding.zig:1468-1505` (A/strong, verified)
- [[kristoff-comptime-sqlite#^f1]] [zig/comptime] A type generic over a comptime string can parse the query and build a `StaticStringMap` of column indices at compile time. (B/strong, verified)
- [[kristoff-comptime-sqlite#^f2]] [zig/comptime] Getters take `comptime col: @EnumLiteral()` and turn a wrong name into `@compileError`, at no runtime cost. (B/strong, verified)
- [[kristoff-comptime-sqlite#^f3]] [zig/comptime] A deliberately incomplete parser is fine when its failure modes are compile errors, not runtime surprises. (B/strong, verified)
- [[kristoff-comptime-sqlite#^f4]] [zig/comptime] He picks a thin, mostly comptime-free dependency on nightly Zig, because complex comptime in dependencies breaks. (B/strong, verified)
- [[kristoff-comptime-sqlite#^f7]] [zig/comptime] Abstraction always costs; a simple partial solution can beat a complete complex one. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-comptime-orm#^f6]] [zig/comptime] [zig/api] Idiom: pass a group of comptime functions as a `Functions: type` struct (`key_fn`, `key_cmp`), since fn is not an expression. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-comptime-orm#^f9]] [zig/comptime] Use `std.meta.FieldEnum(T)` rather than strings for field parameters: errors at the call site and greppable `.debit_account`. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-comptime-orm#^f10]] [zig/comptime] `inline for` over comptime field list unrolls into direct comparisons (`order_by`). (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-comptime-orm#^f11]] [zig/comptime] Struct type built at comptime with `@Type(.{ .@"struct" = ... })` from `std.builtin.Type.StructField`s (stale: 0.16 `@Struct`). (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-comptime-orm#^f14]] [zig/comptime] `anytype` for an untypeable query: not dynamic typing, a fresh copy of the function per query type. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-comptime-orm#^f15]] [zig/comptime] Caveat: avoid comptime reflection if possible; it leads to mind-bending code. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-comptime-wont-do#^f1]] [zig/comptime] Comptime doesn't leak the host: cross compiling to big-endian 32-bit shows target `usize` bytes at comptime. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-comptime-wont-do#^f2]] [zig/comptime] No eval: there is no dynamic source generation; instead partial evaluation/specialisation covers most uses. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-comptime-wont-do#^f3]] [zig/comptime] Marking a parameter `comptime` guarantees the function is partially evaluated per value, leaving one branch. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-comptime-wont-do#^f4]] [zig/comptime] `comptime for` evaluates the whole loop at compile time; `inline for` needs only the length known, for bodies that depend on runtime values. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-comptime-wont-do#^f5]] [zig/comptime] Struct printing by recursive `print(comptime T: type, value: T)` over `@typeInfo(T)` with `inline for (info.fields)` and `else => comptime unreachable`. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-comptime-wont-do#^f6]] [zig/comptime] No DSLs: Zig syntax can't be passed to comptime functions; a custom syntax goes as a comptime string, as `print(comptime fmt, args: anytype)` does. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-comptime-wont-do#^f7]] [zig/comptime] [zig/perf] Types exist only at comptime; one copy of print per type costs code size and compile time, so roll your own RTTI: compute it at comptime, store it as data, use one `print_dyn(rtti, *const anyopaque)`. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-comptime-wont-do#^f8]] [zig/comptime] [zig/api] Comptime can create types but can't add methods; an API is always hand written, so serialisation is a top-level `to_json(comptime T, value, writer)`, with opt-in via a `pub const JSONOptions` declaration. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-comptime-wont-do#^f9]] [zig/comptime] [zig/build] No IO at comptime: evaluation is hermetic and cacheable; generate code that needs IO through build.zig and `@import` it. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-comptime-wont-do#^f10]] [zig/comptime] Cost: no declaration-site type checking of comptime code. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-diagnostics-factory#^f9]] [zig/safety] [zig/comptime] `emit` takes `comptime fmt` and does `comptime assert(fmt[fmt.len - 1] == '\n');`: a comptime assert on a format string. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-frng-gist#^f2]] [zig/comptime] [zig/safety] `int` asserts at comptime the integer is unsigned and the target little-endian before `@bitCast`. (C/fair, verified)
- [[matklad-fun-zig-program#^f1]] [zig/comptime] Arbitrary expressions in type position, and types can use preceding comptime parameters: `fn f(comptime x: bool) if (x) u32 else bool`. (B/fair, verified)
- [[matklad-fun-zig-program#^f2]] [zig/comptime] Signatures must be explicit; Zig computes the signature ignoring the body, then checks call site against signature and body against signature. (B/fair, verified)
- [[matklad-lovely-syntax#^f6]] [zig/style] [zig/comptime] Loops are expressions with `else`: a comptime search `for (.{ u8, u16, ... }) /W/ { if (...) break W; } else unreachable`. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-lovely-syntax#^f9]] [zig/comptime] [zig/api] Generic args are never inferred, but comptime closures keep the annotation burden low: `ArrayListType(u32)` once, then methods close over T. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-lovely-syntax#^f11]] [zig/comptime] Result location semantics: type and result location flow down the expression tree; `.whatever` is shorthand for `@ResultType().whatever`. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-partial-enums#^f1]] [zig/comptime] Problem: common code for some variants; runtime `else => unreachable` works but is theoretically brittle. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-partial-enums#^f2]] [zig/comptime] [zig/safety] Fix: `inline .a, .b => /_, ab/ { handle_ab(); switch (ab) { .a => ..., .b => ..., else => comptime unreachable } }`. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-partial-enums#^f3]] [zig/comptime] `inline` generates the prong once per variant with `ab` comptime-known; `comptime unreachable` fails compilation if reached. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-partial-enums#^f4]] [zig/comptime] [zig/safety] Adding `.c` to the inline prong fails with "reached unreachable code", so a later refactor bug is a compile error. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-test-minimization#^f3]] [zig/comptime] `array(comptime size)` returns `[size]u8` by slicing with a comptime length (`[0..size].*`). (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-test-minimization#^f4]] [zig/comptime] [zig/safety] `int(frng, Int: type)` asserts at comptime that Int is unsigned and the target is little-endian, rather than being endian-clean. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-test-minimization#^f5]] [zig/comptime] Target info is Zig code: `@import("builtin")` exposes `cpu` etc. as comptime constants, no `--cfg` side channel. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-test-minimization#^f8]] [zig/test] [zig/comptime] `weighted(weights: anytype)` returns `std.meta.FieldEnum(@TypeOf(weights))`, picking an action proportional to weight; used as `switch (try world.frng.weighted(.{ .request = 10, .message = 20, .crash = 1 }))`. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-test-minimization#^f9]] [zig/comptime] Study std.meta and std.enums to learn Zig reflection. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-types-and-zig#^f1]] [zig/comptime] Struct types are anonymous but nominal: two identical `struct { f: i32 }` expressions are different types; name it with `const S = struct {...}`. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-types-and-zig#^f2]] [zig/comptime] Anonymous struct literals are structural and coerce to a named type. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-types-and-zig#^f3]] [zig/comptime] No unification: generic functions take the type explicitly (`reverse(i32, &xs)`). (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-types-and-zig#^f4]] [zig/api] [zig/comptime] Methods on a generic type have the type parameters in scope, so `slice.reverse()` needs no types; covers about 80% of uses. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-types-and-zig#^f5]] [zig/comptime] [zig/api] Signatures are always explicit: even `bytesAsSlice(comptime T, bytes: anytype)` returns a computed type `BytesAsSliceReturnType(T, @TypeOf(bytes))`, not anytype. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-types-and-zig#^f6]] [zig/errors] [zig/comptime] Inferred error sets (`!T`) are the exception: checking the caller needs the callee's body. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-zig-and-rust#^f9]] [zig/comptime] Comptime is one dynamically typed feature subsuming much of Rust's type machinery; trade-off: instantiation-time errors are worse in complex cases. (B/strong, verified)
- [[mitchellh-ghostty-and-useful-zig-patterns#^f1]] [zig/comptime] Comptime interfaces: a value whose implementation is picked from compile-time information (font face: CoreText, FreeType or web Canvas). (B/fair, verified)
- [[mitchellh-ghostty-and-useful-zig-patterns#^f2]] [zig/comptime] Main benefit: zero runtime overhead for implementations that never change at runtime; field access and dispatch resolved at compile time. (B/fair, verified)
- [[mitchellh-ghostty-and-useful-zig-patterns#^f3]] [zig/comptime] [zig/test] Cost: Zig analyses only referenced code, so an unbuilt backend can rot; Ghostty builds every option in CI. (B/fair, verified)
- [[mitchellh-ghostty-and-useful-zig-patterns#^f4]] [zig/comptime] Lazy analysis is a feature: situational code needs no ifdef-style guards. (B/fair, verified)
- [[mitchellh-ghostty-and-useful-zig-patterns#^f5]] [zig/comptime] Data tables: a non-pub raw tuple table, easy to edit, is converted at comptime into runtime-friendly structs; raw entries take no binary space. (B/fair, verified)
- [[mitchellh-ghostty-and-useful-zig-patterns#^f6]] [zig/comptime] Platform-specific tables keep only the target's column at comptime (smaller struct and binary, no runtime lookup). (B/fair, verified)
- [[mitchellh-ghostty-and-useful-zig-patterns#^f7]] [zig/comptime] Repetitive tables are generated by a function run at comptime instead of shell or Python codegen scripts. (B/fair, verified)
- [[mitchellh-ghostty-and-useful-zig-patterns#^f8]] [zig/comptime] Wrapping a function body in `comptime {}` makes it impossible to call at runtime. (B/fair, verified)
- [[mitchellh-ghostty-and-useful-zig-patterns#^f9]] [zig/comptime] Type generation (`@Type` then) is powerful but should be limited: it harms readability and slows compilation. (B/fair, verified)
- [[mitchellh-ghostty-and-useful-zig-patterns#^f10]] [zig/comptime] Ghostty generates the modes enum from an entries table so each mode's extra data stays packed next to its name. (B/fair, verified)
- [[mitchellh-ghostty-devlog-004#^f1]] [zig/comptime] Every XTGETTCAP request and response is generated at comptime from the same source that generates the terminfo file, so the two can't drift. (B/fair, verified)
- [[mitchellh-ghostty-devlog-004#^f2]] [zig/comptime] [zig/perf] The runtime path is a comptime lookup table: no allocation or formatting, a stable pointer written to the pty. (B/fair, verified)
- [[mitchellh-tripwire#^f7]] [zig/comptime] [zig/perf] Zero cost outside tests via `builtin.is_test` and an inline calling convention; an empty non-inline function still emitted a call in real builds. (B/strong, verified)
- [[mitchellh-tripwire#^f8]] [zig/comptime] Unreferenced globals aren't emitted, so the tripwire state costs no binary space when disabled. (B/strong, verified)
- [[mitchellh-zig-build-internals#^f6]] [zig/comptime] The runner accepted `build` returning void or any error union by inspecting its type at comptime. (B/fair, verified)
- [[mitchellh-zig-comptime-conditional-disable#^f1]] [zig/comptime] Reasons to disable code: platform-specific code, debug-only code, build-configured features; omitting also shrinks the binary and removes runtime branches. (B/strong, verified)
- [[mitchellh-zig-comptime-conditional-disable#^f2]] [zig/comptime] A plain `if` on comptime-known values (builtin) skips analysis of the dead branch; the `comptime` keyword is redundant there but makes intent explicit. (B/strong, verified)
- [[mitchellh-zig-comptime-conditional-disable#^f3]] [zig/comptime] A comptime operand first in an `and` chain gates the runtime conditions and the block, so they may name types that only exist in some builds. (B/strong, verified)
- [[mitchellh-zig-comptime-conditional-disable#^f4]] [zig/comptime] Check twice when needed: a build-time version check and a runtime check of the dynamically linked library. (B/strong, verified)
- [[mitchellh-zig-comptime-conditional-disable#^f5]] [zig/comptime] Gotcha: comptime doesn't cross a function call; `if (hasFeature())` compiles both branches unless the call is `comptime`. (B/strong, verified)
- [[mitchellh-zig-comptime-conditional-disable#^f6]] [zig/comptime] If the helper mixes comptime and runtime checks, make it `inline fn`. (B/strong, verified)
- [[mitchellh-zig-comptime-conditional-disable#^f8]] [zig/comptime] Comptime is his favourite Zig feature after two years of Ghostty, though he first thought it a gimmick. (B/strong, verified)
- [[mitchellh-zig-comptime-tagged-union-subset#^f5]] [zig/comptime] One exhaustive `scope()` function classifies each tag; a type function filters the union's fields by it and builds a new union. (B/strong, verified)
- [[mitchellh-zig-comptime-tagged-union-subset#^f7]] [zig/comptime] `@unionInit(Action, name, undefined)` makes a value just to call a tag-only method at comptime; touching undefined at comptime is a compile error, so it is safe. (B/strong, verified)
- [[mitchellh-zig-comptime-tagged-union-subset#^f8]] [zig/comptime] Ordinary functions run at comptime unchanged; `scope()` doesn't know which it is in. (B/strong, verified)
- [[mitchellh-zig-comptime-tagged-union-subset#^f9]] [zig/comptime] The subset types cost nothing at runtime and generate no new code. (B/strong, verified)
- [[mitchellh-zig-comptime-tagged-union-subset#^f10]] [zig/comptime] `scoped(self, comptime s) ?ScopedAction(s)`: `inline else` makes the tag comptime-known per prong; a comptime `if` prunes tags outside the scope. (B/strong, verified)
- [[mitchellh-zig-comptime-tagged-union-subset#^f11]] [zig/comptime] A parameter used in the return type must be `comptime`. (B/strong, verified)
- [[mitchellh-zig-comptime-tagged-union-subset#^f13]] [zig/comptime] He calls this advanced and likely overwhelming for newcomers; he is a type pragmatist. (B/strong, verified)
- [[naskos-talk-comptime#^f1]] [zig/comptime] Comptime values have static lifetime: references to comptime locals never dangle. @0:44 (C/fair, verified)
- [[naskos-talk-comptime#^f2]] [zig/comptime] Comptime slice concatenation creates a new value each time; keep it to a minimum. @2:16 (C/fair, verified)
- [[naskos-talk-comptime#^f3]] [zig/comptime] He shows hacks (comptime closures, relying on peer-type quirks) and says not to rely on them. @14:18 (C/fair, verified)
- [[river-repo#^f22]] [zig/comptime] Comptime API: `flags.parser(&.{...})` builds a result struct type with one field per declared flag using the `@Struct` builtin, and parses with `inline for` over the flags. `common/flags.zig` (A/strong, verified)
- [[river-repo#^f23]] [zig/comptime] Generic container as a type function returning `struct`, `const Map = @This();`, an `empty` decl for decl-literal init, allocator passed to each method. `common/slotmap.zig` (A/strong, verified)
- [[river-repo#^f25]] [zig/comptime] Idiom counts (15k lines): 1 `anytype` (the log fn), 6 `inline for`: comptime kept to the flag parser and generics. (A/strong, unquoted)
- [[roc-zig-compiler#^f21]] [zig/comptime] Idiom counts in src/ (1.1M lines): 1232 `anytype`, 585 `inline for`, 250 `@compileError`, 2580 `errdefer`, 1805 `std.debug.assert(`, 3752 `invariant(`, 70 `catch unreachable`, only 2 `std.log.scoped`. (B/strong, unquoted)
- [[seguin-comptime-config#^f1]] [zig/comptime] httpz detects an optional `handle` method on the user's type at comptime and rejects a wrong return type with `@compileError`. (B/fair, verified)
- [[seguin-comptime-config#^f2]] [zig/comptime] Scalar settings via a nested decl struct, falling back to defaults with `@hasDecl`/`@field`. (B/fair, verified)
- [[seguin-interfaces#^f3]] [zig/comptime] A generic `init(ptr: anytype)` can generate the type-erasing thunk so implementations keep normal methods. (B/fair, verified)
- [[seguin-learning-zig-coding-in-zig#^f10]] [zig/comptime] `anytype` is compile-time duck typing: a missing method is a compile error at the call. (B/fair, verified)
- [[seguin-learning-zig-generics#^f1]] [zig/comptime] Generics are ordinary functions taking `comptime T: type` and returning a struct type; nothing special-cased. (B/fair, verified)
- [[seguin-learning-zig-generics#^f2]] [zig/comptime] The same arguments give the same type: instantiations are memoised. (B/fair, verified)
- [[tigerbeetle-architecture#^f6]] [zig/comptime] Zig is "a DSL for machine code": comptime lets you express directly what the machine should do, at the cost of no declaration-site interfaces. (A/strong, verified)
- [[tigerbeetle-blog-copy-hunting#^f3]] [zig/perf] [zig/comptime] `std.meta.eql` on plain byte structs caused copies; replaced by `equal_bytes`, which asserts at comptime that bytewise comparison is valid. (B/strong, verified)
- [[tigerbeetle-blog-copy-hunting#^f4]] [zig/comptime] Each comptime parameter value monomorphises a new copy; counting IR lines per generic name finds bloat. A `comptime` field name and value in a print helper cost 300 bytes for nothing. (B/strong, verified)
- [[tigerbeetle-blog-no-dynamic-memory#^f3]] [zig/memory] [zig/comptime] Memory needed is a comptime sum over every user of a message, each term commented with its owner. (B/fair, verified)
- [[tigerbeetle-blog-swarm-testing#^f2]] [zig/test] [zig/comptime] `std.meta.DeclEnum(Queue)` turns the public API into an enum; switching on it makes the compiler demand a test arm for every new public method. (A/strong, verified)
- [[tigerbeetle-blog-swarm-testing#^f4]] [zig/test] [zig/comptime] Weights struct from an enum via `std.enums.EnumFieldStruct(E, u64, null)`. (A/strong, verified)
- [[tigerbeetle-code#^f14]] [zig/comptime] [zig/safety] Constants are checked against each other in `comptime` blocks with long comments giving the invariant. `constants.zig` (A/strong, verified)
- [[tigerbeetle-code#^f15]] [zig/test] [zig/comptime] Config presets are comptime structs; tests and fuzzers default to `test_min` (tiny WAL, block size, queues) to reach edge cases cheaply; a root file can override via `pub const tigerbeetle_config`. `config.zig`, `fuzz_tests.zig` (A/strong, verified)
- [[tigerbeetle-code#^f22]] [zig/comptime] [zig/safety] `no_padding(T)` and `equal_bytes(T, a, b)` check at comptime that a type has no implicit padding and no pointers before comparing raw bytes; extern structs with u128 fields must be 16-aligned. `stdx/stdx.zig` (A/strong, verified)
- [[tigerbeetle-code#^f23]] [zig/comptime] `array_print` checks at compile time that a fixed buffer fits the worst case of the format (all integer args at max). `stdx/stdx.zig` (A/strong, verified)
- [[tigerbeetle-tiger-style#^f10]] [zig/safety] [zig/comptime] Assert relationships between compile-time constants: checks design integrity before the program runs. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-011-release-notes#^f7]] [zig/comptime] A `comptime` block is now really evaluated at compile time; runtime operations inside are a compile error. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-011-release-notes#^f8]] [zig/comptime] New @inComptime() lets a function branch on whether it is being evaluated at comptime. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-012-release-notes#^f4]] [zig/comptime] Generic types are equal when declared at the same source location with the same captures, not because comptime calls are memoised. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-012-release-notes#^f5]] [zig/comptime] A pointer to a `comptime var` can never become runtime-known nor sit in a global; reason: soundness bugs, writes to data that had become constant. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-012-release-notes#^f6]] [zig/comptime] Fix: copy a comptime-built buffer into a `const` before returning a pointer to it; comptime consts have infinite lifetime. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-012-release-notes#^f7]] [zig/comptime] Global mutable comptime state (a comptime counter) is not and will not be supported. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-014-release-notes#^f3]] [zig/comptime] A labelled switch targeted by continue is not implicitly comptime, like loops. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-014-release-notes#^f12]] [zig/comptime] Anonymous struct types removed; untyped `.{ .a = 1 }` gets a normal struct type; all tuples use structural equivalence and cannot have aligned fields or defaults. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-014-release-notes#^f13]] [zig/comptime] std.builtin.Type fields lowercased (`.@"struct"`, `.int`, `.pointer`), pointer sizes `.one`/`.many`/`.slice`. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-014-release-notes#^f14]] [zig/comptime] @FieldType(T, "name") replaces std.meta.FieldType. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-014-release-notes#^f23]] [zig/comptime] std.c marks missing symbols as `void` types and `{}` functions instead of @compileError, so feature checks use `@TypeOf(f) != void` rather than @hasDecl; last std usingnamespace removed. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-015-release-notes#^f3]] [zig/comptime] Conditional inclusion: declare unconditionally (lazy analysis), or make the value a @compileError per target; for feature detection use `{}` and test `@TypeOf(x) == void`, less prone to typos than @hasDecl. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-015-release-notes#^f4]] [zig/comptime] Implementation switching: make each definition a switch on target, e.g. `pub const init = switch (target) { .windows => initWindows, else => initOther };`. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-016-release-notes#^f26]] [zig/comptime] @Type replaced by @Int, @Struct, @Union, @Enum, @Pointer, @Fn, @Tuple, @EnumLiteral: @Type was clunky in practice. Error sets can no longer be reified. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-016-release-notes#^f32]] [zig/comptime] Lazy field analysis: types used as namespaces (files are structs) are not resolved unless size or a field type is needed. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-017-release-notes#^f19]] [zig/comptime] @hasDecl now sees only pub declarations, from any file. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-017-release-notes#^f23]] [zig/comptime] std.meta.fieldInfo, fieldNames, fieldTypes deprecated in favour of @typeInfo, which is struct-of-arrays: `inline for (info.field_names, info.field_types)`. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-017-release-notes#^f32]] [zig/comptime] `@import("builtin").cpu/os/abi/object_format` deprecated for `.target.cpu` etc. (removed in 0.18). (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-clap-repo#^f2]] [zig/comptime] [zig/api] The parameter spec is a help-text string parsed at comptime; the result array has exactly the parsed length, and a bad spec is a compile error with line and column. `clap.zig` (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-clap-repo#^f3]] [zig/comptime] Comptime string parsing needs a raised branch quota, set with a comment on why it stays. `clap.zig` (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-clap-repo#^f4]] [zig/comptime] The same parser works at run time (`parseParams(allocator, str)`), so the comptime path is a convenience over ordinary code, not a separate implementation. `clap.zig` (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-clap-repo#^f5]] [zig/comptime] The result type is generated: one struct field per named parameter, type from the parameter's value parser, built with `@Struct`; the doc comment shows the struct a given spec produces. `clap.zig` (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-clap-repo#^f6]] [zig/comptime] [zig/api] Value parsers are a comptime anonymous struct of functions keyed by value name (`.usize = int(usize, 0)`), looked up with `@field`; users pass their own struct to add types. `clap/parsers.zig`, `clap.zig` (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-clap-repo#^f7]] [zig/comptime] Generic functions return a function: `int(T, base)` returns a parser via a local struct's fn. `clap/parsers.zig` (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-devlog-2024#^f4]] [zig/comptime] [toolchain] Anonymous struct types are gone (0.14): `.{ .x = 123 }` without a result type gets an ordinary struct type with no structural coercion. (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-devlog-2024#^f9]] [zig/comptime] Self-referential global values (a const holding a pointer to itself) compile since late 2024. (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-devlog-2025#^f8]] [zig/memory] [zig/comptime] DebugAllocator rework: metadata inline on the page, slot counts computed by a comptime function, with an assert that turns bad constants into a compile error instead of a handcrafted table. "No-Libc Zig Now Outperforms Glibc Zig" (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-devlog-2026#^f15]] [zig/style] [zig/comptime] Types that double as namespaces are a common modern pattern; since the 0.16 type resolution redesign their fields are analysed only if the type is instantiated. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-devlog-2026#^f16]] [zig/comptime] Dependency loop errors now name every edge of the loop. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-devlog-2026#^f20]] [zig/comptime] [toolchain] `@bitCast` is redefined on logical bits, endian-agnostic: `[2]u8` to `u16` puts element 0 in the low bits on every target (matches old little-endian behaviour). (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-a#^f36]] [zig/comptime] Namespace-level variables have global lifetime, are order-independent and lazily analysed (unused ones are never compiled); initialisers run at comptime. Namespace Level Variables (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-a#^f39]] [zig/comptime] `comptime var` loads and stores happen during analysis; conditions on it are evaluated statically, so dead branches with `@compileError` never fire. Local Variables (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-a#^f47]] [zig/comptime] `++` needs comptime-known lengths of both sides. Arrays (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-a#^f49]] [zig/comptime] A labelled block computes a complex initial value at comptime (here a global array filled in a loop) and `break`s it out. Arrays (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-a#^f74]] [zig/comptime] Generics are functions returning `type`; comptime calls are memoised so `List(i32) == List(i32)`; such types are named after the call. struct, Struct Naming (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-a#^f85]] [zig/comptime] Tuples (anonymous structs without names) have `.len`, comptime indexing, `++` and `inline for`; fields are `@"0"`. Tuples (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-a#^f86]] [zig/comptime] `anytype` parameters plus anonymous struct arguments (`.{ .int = ... }`) are how std passes ad-hoc argument packs (`print`'s args). Anonymous Struct Literals (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-a#^f88]] [toolchain] [zig/comptime] 0.17 type info is struct-of-arrays: `field_names`, `field_types`, `field_values`, `param_types` instead of a `fields` array of structs. enum, inline else, Function Reflection (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-a#^f102]] [zig/comptime] A switch on a comptime-known value (e.g. target OS) only analyses the taken prong, so per-target code and `@compileError` sit in plain switches. switch (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-a#^f107]] [zig/comptime] `inline` prongs (values, ranges or `inline else`) instantiate the body per value, making the capture comptime-known: runtime value to comptime dispatch. Inline Switch Prongs (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-a#^f108]] [zig/comptime] Prefer `inline else` over `inline for` across union fields: it is a real switch the compiler checks for exhaustiveness, while `inline for` is a chain of ifs needing a trailing `unreachable`. Inline Switch Prongs (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-a#^f109]] [zig/comptime] An inline prong on a union can also capture the tag at comptime while the payload stays runtime. Inline Switch Prongs (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-a#^f112]] [zig/perf] [zig/comptime] Use `inline` loops only when comptime semantics need them or a benchmark shows unrolling is faster. inline while, inline for (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-a#^f119]] [zig/comptime] Reaching `unreachable` at comptime is a compile error, so `comptime unreachable` asserts a branch is statically dead. unreachable, Default Field Values (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-a#^f123]] [zig/comptime] Legitimate `inline fn` uses: comptime-known arguments propagate to the return value (the example's `@compileError` check passes only when inlined). inline fn (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-a#^f127]] [zig/comptime] `anytype` parameters infer their type per call; return types can be computed from them with `@TypeOf`. Function Parameter Type Inference (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-b#^f39]] [zig/comptime] Generics are comptime parameters: compile-time duck typing, checked when each instantiation is analysed. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-b#^f40]] [zig/comptime] A comptime-known `if`/`switch` condition skips analysis of the untaken branch, so type-specific code can sit in one generic function. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-b#^f41]] [zig/comptime] [zig/perf] comptime is about guarantees, not speed. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-b#^f42]] [zig/comptime] Inside a `comptime` block anything runtime-dependent is a compile error; the same function can run at comptime and runtime unchanged. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-b#^f43]] [zig/comptime] Comptime evaluation has a 1000 backwards-branch budget, raised per scope with @setEvalBranchQuota (smaller values are ignored). Known flaw: runaway comptime recursion can overflow the compiler stack instead (#13724). (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-b#^f44]] [zig/comptime] [zig/perf] Container-level expressions are implicitly comptime: lookup tables can be computed by ordinary functions at declaration. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-b#^f45]] [zig/comptime] [zig/api] A generic type is a function returning an anonymous struct; Zig names it after the call (`List(i32)`); assign to a const to name a type. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-b#^f46]] [zig/comptime] std's print is userland comptime code: format strings are parsed at compile time and mismatched arguments are compile errors. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-b#^f47]] [zig/comptime] Avoid @hasDecl for conditional compilation: a rename or typo silently disables code; check `builtin.target.os.tag` and similar instead. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-b#^f48]] [zig/comptime] @inComptime is for comptime-friendly alternative implementations, not for keeping functions out of comptime. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-b#^f49]] [zig/comptime] @compileLog is comptime printf debugging that always fails the build, so it can't be left in. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-b#^f50]] [toolchain] [zig/comptime] 0.17 type construction: per-kind builtins @Int, @Tuple, @Pointer, @Fn, @Struct, @Union, @Enum (struct-of-arrays arguments); checked, not there: no `@Type` builtin in the 0.17 list. (A/strong, absence)
- [[zig-langref-017-b#^f51]] [toolchain] [zig/comptime] 0.17 language types live in `std.lang` (std.lang.Type, AtomicOrder, CallModifier, SourceLocation, Signedness); checked, not there: `std.builtin` appears nowhere in the reference. (A/strong, absence)
- [[zig-langref-017-b#^f52]] [zig/comptime] @typeInfo fields and decls come in source order. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-b#^f53]] [zig/comptime] @TypeOf evaluates its operands without runtime side effects. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-b#^f54]] [zig/comptime] [zig/style] `const Self = @This();` inside a returned struct; at file scope @This() is the file's struct. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-learn-build-system#^f9]] [zig/build] [zig/comptime] Pass build config into code with `b.addOptions()` and `root_module.addOptions("config", options)`; the values are comptime-known in the code, so `@compileError` can gate on them. (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-learn-overview#^f11]] [zig/comptime] A generic data structure is a function returning a `type`; `@typeInfo` reflection implements std formatted printing with no compiler special case. (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-learn-overview#^f12]] [zig/comptime] [toolchain] The reflection example uses the 0.17 struct-of-arrays type info (`field_types`, `field_names`). (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-learn-why#^f7]] [zig/comptime] No macros: things like formatted printing live in std, not the compiler. (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-std-containers#^f14]] [zig/comptime] `print` is only valid for byte lists and says so with `comptime assert`, then maps the Writer's error.WriteFailed back to error.OutOfMemory with an exhaustive switch. `std/array_list.zig:1169` (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-std-containers#^f19]] [zig/comptime] Hash map behaviour is a comptime `Context` type with `hash` and `eql` decls; a zero-size context is inferred, a stateful one forces the `*Context` variant via @compileError. `std/array_hash_map.zig:301` (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-std-containers#^f22]] [zig/comptime] Bad comptime parameters fail at type construction with a readable @compileError, not at use. `std/hash_map.zig:513` (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-std-containers#^f27]] [zig/comptime] [toolchain] MultiArrayList computes its layout at comptime from 0.17's struct-of-arrays type info (`field_names`, `field_types`, `field_attrs`) and sorts fields by alignment in a labelled block. `std/multi_array_list.zig:178` (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-std-io#^f5]] [zig/comptime] `io.async(function, args)` type-erases a call: args typed as `std.meta.ArgsTuple(@TypeOf(function))`, a local struct with a `start` fn casts back and `@call`s; the result type comes from @typeInfo. `std/Io.zig:2526` (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-std-json-fmt-args#^f11]] [zig/comptime] Custom parsing is duck-typed: if the target type has a `jsonParse` decl, the parser calls it (`std.meta.hasFn`). `std/json/static.zig:260` (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-std-json-fmt-args#^f16]] [zig/comptime] `comptimePrint` returns a pointer to a comptime array sized by `count`, the pattern for building strings at compile time. `std/fmt.zig:639` (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-std-json-fmt-args#^f18]] [zig/comptime] Platform-specific parts are chosen with a comptime `switch (native_os)` over types, and unsupported paths are a @compileError naming the alternative. `std/process/Args.zig:37` (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-std-testing#^f8]] [zig/comptime] Its `extra_args` type is computed from the test fn's signature and wrong shapes give a readable @compileError; 0.17 builds the tuple with `@Tuple`. `std/testing.zig:1216` (A/strong, verified)
- [[ziggit-code-smells#^f2]] [zig/comptime] Smell: `reader: anytype` where an interface type would document the contract; tools can't help. (C/fair, verified)
- [[ziggit-error-payload-data#^f4]] [zig/comptime] Keep diagnostics in sync with the error set via `union(std.meta.FieldEnum(MyErrorType))`: a missing case is a compile error. (C/fair, verified)
- [[ziggit-generic-anytype#^f1]] [zig/comptime] Each distinct `anytype` argument type instantiates a new copy of the function. (C/fair, verified)
- [[ziggit-generic-anytype#^f2]] [zig/comptime] Heavy `anytype` makes signatures unreadable: callers dig into bodies to learn the contract. (C/fair, verified)
- [[ziggit-generic-anytype#^f3]] [zig/comptime] Prefer an explicit `comptime T: type` that shapes the other parameters, as `std.mem.eql` does. (C/fair, verified)
- [[zls-repo#^f24]] [zig/safety] [zig/comptime] comptime asserts check buffer-size relations at compile time. `src/main.zig` (A/fair, verified)
- [[zls-repo#^f38]] [zig/comptime] Idiom counts in src/ (37.6k lines): 62 `anytype`, 12 `inline for`, 10 `@compileError`, 0 `usingnamespace`: comptime used sparingly. (A/fair, unquoted)

### zig/errors: error sets, error handling, diagnostics, panics (180)

- [[ak-async-io-text#^f3]] [zig/errors] [zig/memory] Leak footgun: `try a.await(io); try b.await(io);` leaks `b` when `a` fails. (A/strong, verified)
- [[ak-async-io-text#^f4]] [zig/errors] The fix pattern: `defer x.cancel(io) catch {};` right after each `io.async`, then `try x.await(io)` normally. (A/strong, verified)
- [[ak-async-io-text#^f7]] [zig/errors] Benefit: code below the defers stays ordinary Zig with try and return. (A/strong, verified)
- [[bun-in-rust#^f4]] [zig/memory] [zig/errors] defer must be written at every call site, so cleanup is easy to forget (leak) or run twice in rarely reached error paths (double free). (B/strong, verified)
- [[bun-repo#^f5]] [zig/api] [zig/errors] std is wrapped: always `bun.*` over `std.*` for fs, posix, paths, strings, because the wrappers keep OS error info (errno, syscall, path) and never use `unreachable`. `src/CLAUDE.md` (A/fair, verified)
- [[bun-repo#^f6]] [zig/errors] System calls return `Maybe(T)`, a tagged union of result or a rich `bun.sys.Error`, instead of a Zig error union, so the error carries data; `.unwrap()` converts to `try`. `src/CLAUDE.md` (A/fair, verified)
- [[bun-repo#^f8]] [zig/memory] [zig/errors] OOM crashes the process via `bun.handleOom(expr)`, which only catches `error.OutOfMemory`; `catch bun.outOfMemory()` is banned because it would swallow other errors too. `src/CLAUDE.md` (A/fair, verified)
- [[ghostty-repo#^f29]] [zig/errors] `errdefer comptime unreachable;` after the last fallible step asserts at compile time that nothing below can fail (27 sites). `src/terminal/PageList.zig:677` (A/strong, verified)
- [[ghostty-repo#^f30]] [zig/errors] Each acquisition in init is followed immediately by its errdefer. `src/terminal/PageList.zig:647-675` (A/strong, verified)
- [[ghostty-repo#^f31]] [zig/errors] When an error means internal corruption, code switches over every member of the error set, with no else, before panicking, so a new error is a compile error. `src/terminal/PageList.zig:181-196` (A/strong, verified)
- [[ghostty-repo#^f32]] [zig/errors] Public functions name narrow error sets (`Allocator.Error!T`) rather than inferring. `src/terminal/PageList.zig:635-638` (A/strong, verified)
- [[ghostty-repo#^f33]] [zig/errors] Config parsing collects diagnostics (key, message, file:line) in a list and keeps going; only OOM is returned. `src/cli/args.zig:40-42,159-161` (A/strong, verified)
- [[ghostty-repo#^f34]] [zig/errors] Diagnostic location is a tagged union (none, cli index, file+line), and a `format(writer)` method prints it. `src/cli/diagnostics.zig:19,49-55` (A/strong, verified)
- [[ghostty-repo#^f35]] [zig/errors] In generic code, the inferred error set is merged with known ones and narrowed with `@errorCast`, so a switch with `else` compiles for any T. `src/cli/args.zig:155-158` (A/strong, verified)
- [[ghostty-repo#^f36]] [zig/errors] At a void interface boundary the fallible implementation is wrapped: the error is logged as a warning and a flag is set. `src/terminal/stream_terminal.zig:609-618` (A/strong, verified)
- [[ghostty-repo#^f37]] [zig/errors] At the C ABI, Zig errors become an `enum(c_int)` Result with negative codes; out params return objects. `src/terminal/c/result.zig`, `src/terminal/c/osc.zig:21-31` (A/strong, verified)
- [[kristoff-asynchrony-not-concurrency#^f4]] [zig/errors] `io.async` can't fail: if no task slot is free, it runs the function directly. (A/strong, verified)
- [[kristoff-zig-new-async-io#^f3]] [zig/errors] Don't `try` the first of several awaits: an early return skips awaiting the others, and every future must be awaited or cancelled. (A/strong, verified)
- [[kristoff-zig-new-async-io#^f4]] [zig/errors] Pattern: `defer future.cancel(io)` right after `io.async`, then `try future.await(io)`; cancel and await are idempotent. (A/strong, verified)
- [[kristoff-zig-new-async-io#^f9]] [zig/errors] Code that needs two tasks to overlap (server accept plus client connect) must request concurrency explicitly; `async` alone is a bug there. (A/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-cancelation#^f1]] [zig/api] [zig/errors] Synchronous cancelation is control flow: returning an error unwinds and runs cleanup (`defer`). (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-comptime-orm#^f12]] [zig/memory] [zig/errors] `create` reserves in objects and every index, then `errdefer comptime unreachable`, then inserts; else a value could land in only some indexes. (B/strong, verified)
