- [[matklad-defer-patterns#^f4]] [zig/errors] [zig/safety] `errdefer comptime unreachable` statically proves no error path after that point; std hash_map grow: `try map.allocate(...)` then `errdefer comptime unreachable;` then swap and deinit. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-defer-patterns#^f5]] [zig/errors] Errors give only a code and a trace; an application often has enough context where the error starts to report it there. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-defer-patterns#^f6]] [zig/errors] Inside a labeled block: `errdefer /err/ log.err("failed to read the port number: {}", .{err});` before the `try`s. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-diagnostics-factory#^f1]] [zig/errors] His default for reporting: don't design error payload types; give a set of functions that construct errors. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-diagnostics-factory#^f2]] [zig/errors] [zig/api] TigerBeetle tidy.zig has `const Errors = struct` with methods `add_long_line`, `add_banned`, `add_dead_declaration`; call sites call `errors.add_long_line(file, line_index)`. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-diagnostics-factory#^f3]] [zig/errors] Fail fast is `errors.add_...(...)` then `return error.Tidy;`: the error code is deliberately independent of which error was reported. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-diagnostics-factory#^f4]] [zig/errors] Why: callers don't care what happens inside, so you needn't decide up front what to do with errors and can change your mind later. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-diagnostics-factory#^f5]] [zig/errors] The constructor is the natural place to convert data to a user form (e.g. byte offset to line and column). (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-diagnostics-factory#^f7]] [zig/errors] Uniform call syntax makes all error sites greppable (`rg 'errors.add_'`). (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-diagnostics-factory#^f8]] [zig/errors] [zig/test] Polymorphism for tests: in a run errors go to stderr, in tests to an in-memory buffer (`if (errors.captured) /*captured/` else `std.debug.print`). (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-diagnostics-factory#^f10]] [zig/errors] No giant `union(enum)` of all errors, because the use case doesn't need it. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-error-abi#^f1]] [zig/perf] [zig/errors] Large error types inflate the result size and push functions across the call stack to return through memory; one large rare error worsens code everywhere. (B/fair, verified)
- [[matklad-error-abi#^f4]] [zig/errors] (claim) Unwinding may be optimal; no reproducible benchmarks known. (B/fair, verified)
- [[matklad-error-abi#^f5]] [zig/errors] (inferred) Zig's error codes plus an out-of-band Diagnostics sink keep the error part of `E!T` register-sized, which is this post's argument applied. (B/fair, unquoted)
- [[matklad-error-codes-control-flow#^f1]] [zig/errors] Reporting an error to a user and branching on an error are different aspects; think about them separately. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-error-codes-control-flow#^f2]] [zig/errors] In Zig error sets are strongly typed error codes, not poor man's sum types. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-error-codes-control-flow#^f3]] [zig/errors] Two destinations: bubble to an isolation boundary and show the operator, or handle with a recovery action. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-error-codes-control-flow#^f4]] [zig/errors] If you know the final destination up front, render the error immediately; otherwise make it a structured product type. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-error-codes-control-flow#^f5]] [zig/errors] Branching needs a sum type, but only a finite number of handling paths exist, so a lean handling type is an enumeration: an error code. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-error-codes-control-flow#^f6]] [zig/errors] Zig requires `catch {}` to ignore an error, and `_ = x` for a value; `_ = can_fail()` fails with "error union is discarded", so an infallible call that later becomes fallible can't silently drop errors. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-error-codes-control-flow#^f7]] [zig/errors] (claim) After using Zig, mandatory use of all return values is a non-issue: trivial discards are rare. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-error-codes-control-flow#^f8]] [zig/errors] [zig/api] std's Reader declares exact sets: `readSliceAll` returns `error{ReadFailed, EndOfStream}!void`, `readSliceShort` `error{ReadFailed}!usize`. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-error-codes-control-flow#^f9]] [zig/errors] Sets compose additively and subtractively; whole-program compilation infers sets and gives each error a unique numeric code, allowing `anyerror`. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-error-codes-control-flow#^f10]] [zig/errors] [zig/api] Diagnostic info goes out of band via a sink: std.zon `fromSlice(T, gpa, source, diag: ?*Diagnostics) error{ OutOfMemory, ParseZon }!T`; pass null to switch on the code, pass Diagnostics to show the user. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-error-context#^f1]] [zig/errors] Zig error codes cover handling; reporting is left to the user, and the idiomatic solution is a Diagnostics out parameter (sink). (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-error-context#^f2]] [zig/errors] For script-y code the sink is too much friction; a plain `try` gives an error trace but not which file failed. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-error-context#^f3]] [zig/errors] He rejects per-call `catch /err/ { log.err(...); return err; }`: high friction, obscures the happy path, repeated per fallible call. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-error-context#^f4]] [zig/errors] [zig/memory] Pattern: `errdefer log.err("path={s}", .{path});` at the top of the block, then plain `try`s; context is per block and each frame in the call stack adds its own. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-error-context#^f5]] [zig/errors] Drawback: the context is logged even when a caller later handles the error, which matters in 0.16 where cancelation can surface from any IO operation and is meant to be handled. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-error-context#^f6]] [zig/errors] (inferred) So the errdefer log suits CLI and tool code where errors bubble to main; not library code whose callers handle errors (e.g. error.Canceled). (B/strong, unquoted)
- [[matklad-error-model-convergence#^f1]] [zig/errors] Modern languages mark fallible calls at the call site (`try make_widget()`); changing infallible to fallible ripples through the call chain, adding a new error doesn't. (B/fair, verified)
- [[matklad-error-model-convergence#^f3]] [zig/safety] [zig/errors] An operational error from a lower layer can be a programming error for the layer above, so there is a way to escalate a result to a panic; bugs in ordinary code are catastrophic and invisible in types. (B/fair, verified)
- [[matklad-error-model-convergence#^f4]] [zig/errors] Handling is localised to one expression: `make_widget() catch /err/ switch (err) { error.NotFound => default_widget(), }`. (B/fair, verified)
- [[matklad-error-model-convergence#^f5]] [zig/errors] Tension: low levels want exhaustive errors, high levels want to string subsystems together; midpoint is poorly served and leads to "bag of everything" types. (B/fair, verified)
- [[matklad-error-model-convergence#^f6]] [zig/errors] Zig assumes a mostly closed world and infers who can throw what across functions. (B/fair, verified)
- [[matklad-fix-deeper#^f1]] [zig/errors] A poor out-of-disk message showed a missing fatal-error infrastructure; `NoSpaceLeft` was one of a kind (TigerBeetle #2289). (B/fair, verified)
- [[matklad-invariant#^f6]] [zig/errors] Reading from disk can't fail at the API: failed local reads are repaired from other replicas, so code above needn't handle errors. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-io-threaded#^f4]] [zig/errors] Cancelation reaches user code as `error.Canceled`; Zig implements cancelation and branching of error management, not reporting. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-io-threaded#^f5]] [zig/api] [zig/errors] Reader and Writer interfaces type erase errors and so support cancelation, which needs extra care, on top of remembering to flush. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-look-for-bugs#^f4]] [zig/memory] [zig/errors] Zig smell: an allocator and a `try` in the same scope need care (see reserve first). (B/fair, verified)
- [[matklad-reserve-first#^f1]] [zig/memory] [zig/errors] Bug 1 (internString in Andrew Kelley's talk): getOrPut inserts a key, then a later `try` fails, leaving an uninitialised table entry. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-reserve-first#^f2]] [zig/memory] [zig/errors] Bug 2 (Ghostty Atlas.grow): the errdefer restores `data_old` but the `defer alloc.free(data_old)` still frees it. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-reserve-first#^f3]] [zig/errors] Both are exception safety problems: strong (state as before), basic (valid but different), none (invalid). (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-reserve-first#^f5]] [zig/errors] [zig/safety] `errdefer comptime unreachable;` marks "no errors after this point" (end of reservation phase), checked at compile time. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-reserve-first#^f9]] [zig/memory] [zig/errors] Applications should consider aborting on OOM; he has seen only one (xit) pass a 30-minute errdefer grep. Libraries: leave allocation to the caller or test with an allocator that really fails. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-retry-loop#^f2]] [zig/errors] On final failure report the underlying error from the last attempt, not just "all attempts failed". (B/fair, verified)
- [[matklad-retry-loop#^f3]] [zig/errors] The action returns `!enum { ok, retry: anyerror }`: success, fatal error (propagated by try), or transient error carrying the error itself. (B/fair, verified)
- [[matklad-retry-loop-retry#^f1]] [zig/errors] Reframe: loop while the action returns an error; retries left is an extra early exit; a loop body may run zero times, the condition always runs. (B/fair, verified)
- [[matklad-retry-loop-retry#^f2]] [zig/errors] Idiom: `const err = if (action()) /ok/ break ok else /err/ err; if (!is_transient_error(err)) break err;` then the loop value goes through `try`. (B/fair, verified)
- [[matklad-test-minimization#^f12]] [zig/errors] He often wants to peel off one error: `catch /err/ switch (err) { error.OutOfEntropy => return, else => return err }`. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-types-and-zig#^f6]] [zig/errors] [zig/comptime] Inferred error sets (`!T`) are the exception: checking the caller needs the callee's body. (B/strong, verified)
- [[mitchellh-tripwire#^f1]] [zig/test] [zig/errors] errdefer cleanup is called one of the most error-prone parts of Zig programs, because error paths rarely run in tests. (B/strong, verified)
- [[mitchellh-tripwire#^f9]] [zig/errors] Result: ~6 errdefer bugs fixed in the first integration PR, none ever seen in the wild, each now held by a test. (B/strong, verified)
- [[river-repo#^f9]] [zig/memory] [zig/errors] Deliberately no leak cleanup on error in startup init, since the process exits anyway: simpler code. `river/Server.zig` (A/strong, verified)
- [[river-repo#^f12]] [zig/errors] Errors cannot cross a C callback, so handlers end the error chain: OOM is reported to the Wayland client with `postNoMemory()` (46 uses) and the handler returns. `river/XdgPopup.zig`, `river/Seat.zig` (A/strong, verified)
- [[river-repo#^f13]] [zig/errors] Signatures name narrow error sets (`error{OutOfMemory}!void`); `main` returns `anyerror!void`. `river/XdgPopup.zig`, `river/main.zig` (A/strong, verified)
- [[river-repo#^f14]] [zig/errors] User-facing fatal errors go through `std.process.fatal` with a message; errors switched on by name, `{t}` for the tag. `river/main.zig` (A/strong, verified)
- [[river-repo#^f15]] [zig/errors] Unsupported platform state is a `@panic` with a message (the only one in src); `catch unreachable` appears once, on a bufPrint into a buffer sized for it. `river/util.zig`, `river/TransientSeatManager.zig` (A/strong, verified)
- [[river-repo#^f19]] [zig/build] [zig/errors] C varargs callbacks are handled by a small C shim compiled into the exe (`addCSourceFile`) that formats into a buffer and calls an `export fn` in Zig. `river/wlroots_log_wrapper.c`, `river/main.zig` (A/strong, verified)
- [[roc-zig-compiler#^f2]] [zig/safety] [zig/errors] One rule for compiler invariants everywhere: a debug-only assertion in debug builds, `unreachable` in release; never a release runtime check, panic, error return or recovery. User-facing failures (bad input, missing files, allocation failure, corrupt caches) are reported... (B/strong, verified)
- [[roc-zig-compiler#^f13]] [zig/memory] [zig/errors] Every count that grows with input has a width chosen per field; narrowing a host-sized count is checked, and overflow returns `error.OutOfMemory` like a failed allocation, never an unchecked `@intCast`. `design.md`, `src/collections/safe_list.zig` (B/strong, verified)
- [[roc-zig-compiler#^f20]] [zig/errors] Error sets composed with `//` and named per module (`ChildError`, `Error`) with doc comments. `src/base/death_test.zig` (B/strong, verified)
- [[seguin-interfaces#^f7]] [zig/errors] His vtable signature returns `anyerror!void`, erasing which errors can happen. (B/fair, verified)
- [[seguin-learning-zig-language-overview-2#^f1]] [zig/errors] `catch /err/ switch (err)` is the idiomatic way to handle specific errors and pass or map the rest. (B/fair, verified)
- [[seguin-learning-zig-language-overview-2#^f2]] [zig/errors] `try` is shorthand for `catch /err/ return err`, and errors must be handled. (B/fair, verified)
- [[seguin-learning-zig-language-overview-2#^f3]] [zig/errors] He uses inferred error sets heavily; libraries may prefer explicit sets as documentation. Function pointers to inferred-set functions need `anyerror`. (B/fair, verified)
- [[seguin-learning-zig-language-overview-2#^f4]] [zig/errors] `anyerror` in a return type overstates what can fail; keep it for parameters or fields that hold any error. (B/fair, verified)
- [[seguin-learning-zig-language-overview-2#^f5]] [zig/errors] `!?T` returns are common; `(try f()) orelse default` unwraps both. (B/fair, verified)
- [[seguin-threads-freeing#^f7]] [zig/errors] (inferred) His `put` registers `errdefer allocator.destroy(entry)` and then `errdefer entry.release()`; a failure after both would run release (which destroys the entry at rc 1) and then destroy again, a double free. Once ownership moves into a release-style cleanup, end the earlier ... (B/fair, verified)
- [[superhtml-repo#^f17]] [zig/errors] [zig/api] (2026-10-22, Zig practice pass) A `Fatal` error set means "already reported to the user": the reporter writes the diagnostic, then returns `error.Fatal`, so callers only propagate; OOM is composed in with `//`. `src/errors.zig` (B/strong, verified)
- [[superhtml-repo#^f18]] [zig/errors] The CLI's main ends every error chain in one place: subcommand errors go to a `fatal` that prints and exits 1; OOM gets its own `oom()` noreturn helper. `src/main.zig` (B/strong, verified)
- [[superhtml-repo#^f19]] [zig/errors] [zig/safety] A root `pub fn panic` override routes panics and stack traces to the log file in LSP mode (stderr belongs to the protocol) and breaks into the debugger in Debug. `src/main.zig` (B/strong, verified)
- [[tigerbeetle-code#^f13]] [zig/memory] [zig/errors] Init with errdefer guarded by an `initialized` flag, so the owner deinitialises components only on the error path before handover. `vsr/replica.zig` (A/strong, verified)
- [[tigerbeetle-code#^f21]] [zig/test] [zig/errors] In tests `log.err` is downgraded to `warn`, because Zig fails any test that logs an error and they want to test error paths. `stdx/stdx.zig` (A/strong, verified)
- [[tigerbeetle-code#^f24]] [zig/errors] `parse_int` is strict by default (no leading zeros, separators, or `-0`) where std accepts them; the std functions are banned by tidy. `stdx/stdx.zig` (A/strong, verified)
- [[tigerbeetle-code#^f32]] [zig/errors] Each IO operation declares its own explicit error set merged with `posix.UnexpectedError`; init uses `errdefer /err/ switch (err)` to log the likely cause of known failures. `io/linux.zig` (A/strong, verified)
- [[tigerbeetle-tidy#^f3]] [zig/style] [zig/errors] Ban list (string match): std types replaced by stdx (`std.BoundedArray`, `StaticBitSet`, `std.time.Instant`), `@memcpy(` for `stdx.copy_disjoint`, std int parsing and PRNG range functions; library and language footguns. `src/tidy.zig` (A/strong, verified)
- [[tigerbeetle-tidy#^f4]] [zig/errors] Comparing errors with `==`/`!=` is banned: use `switch`, because `err == error.Foo` silently upcasts to `anyerror` and won't catch a misspelt or removed error. `src/tidy.zig` (A/strong, verified)
- [[tigerbeetle-tiger-style#^f21]] [zig/errors] All errors must be handled; cites the OSDI'14 finding that 92% of catastrophic failures came from mishandled non-fatal errors. (A/strong, verified)
- [[tigerbeetle-tiger-style#^f41]] [zig/api] [zig/errors] Simpler return types reduce branches at every call site: `void` > `bool` > `u64` > `?u64` > `!u64`. (A/strong, verified)
- [[tigerbeetle-tiger-style-talk#^f8]] [zig/errors] [zig/safety] Operating errors (e.g. out of memory: refuse new connections) are handled; programming errors can't be predicted, so the only safe response is to shut down. @39:08 (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-011-release-notes#^f20]] [zig/errors] Error return traces exist in safe modes and show where an unhandled error came from, where a stack trace would say less. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-012-release-notes#^f9]] [zig/errors] @errSetCast replaced by @errorCast, which also narrows an error union. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-015-release-notes#^f13]] [zig/errors] Motive: old streams passed errors through, as useful as anyerror; the new one defines precise, actionable error sets per function (e.g. ReadFailed, then ask the implementation for diagnostics). (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-016-release-notes#^f6]] [zig/errors] Pattern: after io.async, `defer` a cancel that frees any resource the task returned; cancel is needed to release the task even on error, and await and cancel are idempotent. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-016-release-notes#^f7]] [zig/errors] error.Canceled: only the code that requested cancelation may ignore it; otherwise propagate it (most common), or recancel and swallow, or swapCancelProtection. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-016-release-notes#^f8]] [zig/errors] Cancelation is like await: the task may have succeeded anyway, so its side effects (an opened file) must be handled. checkCancel is rarely needed, mainly in long CPU-bound tasks. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-016-release-notes#^f13]] [zig/errors] Thread.Pool migration example adds `errdefer g.cancel(io)` even where the function cannot fail, so a later fallible change is not a bug. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-016-release-notes#^f34]] [zig/errors] Stack traces use safe unwinding by default; captureCurrentStackTrace, writeStackTrace, dumpCurrentStackTrace are the API. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-017-release-notes#^f21]] [zig/errors] `errdefer /err/` capture removed; to act on the returned error, split the function and `catch /err/` at the outer call. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-clap-repo#^f8]] [zig/errors] [zig/api] Errors carry detail through an optional Diagnostic out-pointer in the options struct; the error union stays plain and the caller reports with `diag.report`. `clap.zig`, `example/simple.zig` (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-clap-repo#^f9]] [zig/errors] The parser's error set is a named `Error` in the streaming module, switched on by the reporter with an else for user parser errors. `clap/streaming.zig`, `clap.zig` (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-clap-repo#^f10]] [zig/errors] A parser that cannot fail declares the empty error set `error{}`. `clap/parsers.zig` (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-devlog-2025#^f3]] [zig/api] [zig/errors] Design goal: async code reads like normal Zig, with try, defer and while and the usual resource patterns; no function colouring. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-devlog-2025#^f5]] [zig/errors] Error handling style in std: switch over the error, return the fatal ones, aggregate the rest, map impossible `WouldBlock` to `error.Unexpected`. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-a#^f5]] [zig/errors] For diagnostics, write to stderr with `std.debug.print`, where a failed write does not matter; `main` can then be `void`. Hello World (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-a#^f46]] [zig/errors] `x.?` is `x orelse unreachable`; the right side of `orelse` and `catch` may be `noreturn` (`return`, `unreachable`, a panic). Table of Operators (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-a#^f103]] [zig/errors] A switch without `else` must list every value, a compile error otherwise; so omitting `else` makes adding an enum member flag every switch. Exhaustive Switching (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-a#^f104]] [zig/errors] Switching on errors: `else => unreachable`, `else => return` and `else => /e/ return e` are allowed even when unreachable; in generic code, prongs for errors outside the set are `comptime unreachable`. Switching on Errors (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-a#^f120]] [zig/errors] `noreturn` (`return`, `break`, `unreachable`, exit functions) fits any type in if and switch, enabling `const a = if (c) b else return;` and `catch exit(1)`. noreturn (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-b#^f1]] [zig/errors] Error names are global small integers: every error name in the compilation gets one unsigned integer, the same value wherever it is declared; default type u16. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-b#^f2]] [zig/errors] Avoid `anyerror`: a known error set gives better docs and lets switch flag a forgotten error. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-b#^f3]] [zig/errors] Four ways to consume an error union: default with `catch value`, propagate with `try`, assert with `catch unreachable`, or branch per error with `if ... else /err/ switch`. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-b#^f4]] [zig/errors] `try x` is shorthand for `x catch /err/ return err`. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-b#^f5]] [zig/errors] [zig/safety] `catch unreachable` is an assertion: it panics in debug and safe builds if the assumption is wrong, and is the sanctioned way to ignore an error. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-b#^f6]] [zig/errors] Handle some errors and pass the rest on: the `else` prong of the switch captures a narrower error set. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-b#^f7]] [zig/errors] [zig/memory] `errdefer` runs only when the block exits with an error; the cleanup sits right after the acquisition, so every exit path is covered. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-b#^f8]] [zig/errors] An unchecked error is a compile error; error types let the compiler weight branches towards success. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-b#^f9]] [zig/errors] Merge sets with `//`, mainly for functions whose errors differ by comptime branch (std: `LinuxFileOpenError // WindowsFileOpenError`); left-hand doc comments win. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-b#^f10]] [zig/errors] [zig/api] Inferred error sets (`!T`) make the function generic: no function pointer, set may differ by target, no recursion. Use an explicit set there, grown from empty by following compile errors. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-b#^f11]] [zig/errors] Error return traces record every point an error was returned through, not just the final stack, so `try` everywhere stays debuggable; on by default only in debug. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-b#^f12]] [zig/errors] [zig/perf] Trace cost: one memory write on the success path, a couple of math ops per error return. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-b#^f13]] [zig/errors] Traces print when an error is returned from main, reaches `catch unreachable` (default panic handler), or via @errorReturnTrace and `std.debug.dumpErrorReturnTrace`. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-b#^f14]] [zig/errors] Don't persist error integers: @intFromError and @errorFromInt are discouraged because the numbering changes with the source. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-b#^f15]] [zig/errors] [zig/perf] The error name table is only emitted if @errorName is called with a runtime value. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-b#^f16]] [zig/errors] [zig/memory] Allocation failure is an error (`error.OutOfMemory`), not a crash; the doc rejects the "Linux overcommits anyway" argument (Windows, embedded and real-time don't overcommit; OOM killer is nondeterministic; libraries get reused). (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-b#^f17]] [zig/errors] main may return void, E!void, u8 or E!u8; an error prints the trace and exits 1. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-b#^f18]] [zig/errors] [zig/safety] Checked arithmetic: std.math.add/sub/mul/divTrunc/divFloor/divCeil/ divExact/shl return errors; @addWithOverflow and friends return a (result, u1) tuple; `+%` `-%` `*%` wrap by definition. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-b#^f20]] [zig/errors] `x orelse return null` unwraps once and the name is non-null for the rest of the function, as concise as C's null check. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-b#^f55]] [zig/errors] Prefer `std.debug.panic` over @panic, except in libraries calling the root's handler or mixed C/Zig objects. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-learn-overview#^f7]] [zig/memory] [zig/errors] defer and errdefer cover all resource management, not only memory: each acquisition is followed by its errdefer release. (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-learn-overview#^f8]] [zig/errors] Errors are values and may not be ignored; `try` is shorthand for `catch /err/ return err`; error return traces cost no stack unwinding. (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-learn-overview#^f9]] [zig/errors] An exhaustive `switch` on an error forces every possible error to be handled; the compiler lists the missing ones. (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-learn-overview#^f10]] [zig/errors] [zig/safety] `catch unreachable` asserts no error; it is undefined behaviour in unsafe modes, so only where success is guaranteed. (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-learn-overview#^f14]] [zig/errors] [toolchain] Stale on the page: the errors-as-values example calls `std.fs.cwd()`, which does not compile on 0.17 (output shows the error). (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-learn-why#^f5]] [zig/errors] The language aims to make correct error handling the lazy path. (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-std-allocators#^f24]] [zig/errors] Leaks at exit are logged but do not change the exit code. `std/start.zig:806` (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-std-allocators#^f25]] [zig/errors] An error returned from main is logged with `{t}` (tag name) and the error return trace dumped, exit code 1. `std/start.zig:843` (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-std-allocators#^f26]] [zig/errors] Error sets are declared as named `pub const XError = error{...}` with a doc comment per error, composed with `//`. `std/process.zig:60` (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-std-allocators#^f27]] [zig/errors] `process.fatal` logs and exits 1: the std's own idiom for unrecoverable CLI errors, used by start.zig itself. `std/process.zig:698` (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-std-containers#^f13]] [zig/errors] Integer overflow of a requested size is reported as error.OutOfMemory via a small helper using @addWithOverflow, not a panic. `std/array_list.zig:1558` (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-std-containers#^f25]] [zig/errors] `errdefer comptime unreachable;` after the last fallible call proves at compile time that nothing below can fail, so no cleanup path is needed. `std/hash_map.zig:1437` (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-std-io#^f3]] [zig/errors] Cancellation is an error, `error.Canceled` in `Io.Cancelable`, which most Io functions add to their error set; ignoring it is called a bug. `std/Io.zig:815` (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-std-io#^f4]] [zig/errors] `error.Unexpected` is documented as a std gap to be patched, not a normal outcome. `std/Io.zig:820` (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-std-io#^f11]] [zig/errors] Writer errors are one opaque `error.WriteFailed`; the detail lives on the implementation, not in the error set. `std/Io/Writer.zig:91` (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-std-io#^f12]] [zig/errors] The file writer stores the real OS error in an `err: ?Error` field and returns WriteFailed, so callers inspect `fw.err` after a failure. `std/Io/File/Writer.zig:12` (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-std-io#^f21]] [zig/errors] Reader errors: ReadFailed (details on the implementation), EndOfStream, and for delimiters StreamTooLong, with state unchanged on that error. `std/Io/Reader.zig:105` (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-std-json-fmt-args#^f5]] [zig/errors] Building the arena uses a double errdefer: destroy the arena struct, then deinit it once initialised, in reverse order of setup. `std/json/static.zig:108` (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-std-json-fmt-args#^f6]] [zig/errors] The error set is computed by a type function from the source type, merged with `//`. `std/json/static.zig:192` (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-std-json-fmt-args#^f7]] [zig/errors] Diagnostics out-param: the caller declares a `Diagnostics` and registers a pointer; after an error it reads line, column and byte offset. The error stays a plain error code. `std/json/Scanner.zig:1542` (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-std-json-fmt-args#^f15]] [zig/errors] ParseIntError is two errors, each doc-commented with its meaning. `std/fmt.zig:304` (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-talk-async-io-zigtoberfest#^f3]] [zig/errors] [zig/memory] Footgun: `try a.await(io)` before awaiting `b` leaks `b` when `a` fails (the leak checker catches it). @11:26 (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-talk-async-io-zigtoberfest#^f4]] [zig/errors] [zig/memory] Rule: if you async, defer a cancel, as you defer a free after allocating; then `try ... await` is safe and failure cancels the rest at once. @13:26 (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-talk-async-io-zigtoberfest#^f7]] [zig/errors] Many Io functions now include `error.Canceled`; ordinary errdefer and defer cleanup then runs on cancellation and propagates it, so cancellation comes "for free"; explicit checks only in long CPU-bound loops. @21:30 (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-talk-kelley-flush#^f7]] [zig/errors] Cancellation fits Zig errors: `defer` cancels in-flight work on error, and `error.Canceled` propagates up, cancelling towers of operations. @16:55 (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-talk-roadmap-2026#^f13]] [zig/errors] Every function doing Io gains `error.Canceled`; usually just propagate it, and errdefer runs on cancellation. @1:42:40 (A/strong, verified)
- [[ziggit-code-smells#^f1]] [zig/errors] Smell: `try` everywhere up to `main` in an application, never handling anything; decide what is recoverable. (C/fair, verified)
- [[ziggit-errdefer-double-free#^f1]] [zig/errors] A callee's `errdefer` and the caller's `errdefer` for the same resource don't both run: the caller's is only registered after the callee returned successfully. (B/strong, verified)
- [[ziggit-errdefer-scope#^f1]] [zig/errors] `errdefer` is block-scoped like `defer`; an errdefer inside an `if` stops at the `if`. (C/fair, verified)
- [[ziggit-errdefer-scope#^f2]] [zig/errors] Clean `getOrPut` shape: return early when found, then `errdefer` the removal and the value's deinit at function scope. (C/fair, verified)
- [[ziggit-errdefer-scope#^f4]] [zig/errors] A proposal for parent-scoped errdefer (`errdefer :label`) was closed as not planned. (C/fair, verified)
- [[ziggit-error-payload-data#^f1]] [zig/errors] Zig errors carry no payload; return the error as usual and fill a caller-provided Diagnostics object with details (line/column etc.). (C/fair, verified)
- [[ziggit-error-payload-data#^f2]] [zig/errors] Diagnostics go either per call (zig-clap) or into a session/init (std.json Scanner `enableDiagnostics`). (C/fair, verified)
- [[ziggit-error-payload-data#^f3]] [zig/errors] Cursor out-parameter: the callee advances `*usize`, so on error the caller logs where it stopped. (C/fair, verified)
- [[ziggit-error-payload-data#^f5]] [zig/errors] When a failure is a value to inspect rather than a refusal, return a tagged union. (C/fair, verified)
- [[zls-repo#^f18]] [zig/errors] Error sets are explicit and narrow in signatures (185 `error{OutOfMemory}` in src/) and composed with `//`; inferred `!T` mostly at the top (main). `src/main.zig`, `src/Server.zig` (A/fair, verified)
- [[zls-repo#^f19]] [zig/errors] `catch unreachable` is kept for errors ruled out by an earlier check, often with a comment saying why (18 uses). `src/Uri.zig` (A/fair, verified)
- [[zls-repo#^f20]] [zig/errors] [zig/api] Fatal errors in build logic use `std.process.fatal` with a message, not a bare panic; `{t}` prints an error or enum tag name. `build.zig` (A/fair, verified)

### zig/memory: allocators, ownership, arenas, defer and errdefer, static allocation, containers (293)

- [[ak-async-io-text#^f2]] [zig/memory] [zig/test] Setup pattern: DebugAllocator with `defer assert(deinit() == .ok)` so leaks (including leaked tasks) fail the run. (A/strong, verified)
- [[ak-async-io-text#^f3]] [zig/errors] [zig/memory] Leak footgun: `try a.await(io); try b.await(io);` leaks `b` when `a` fails. (A/strong, verified)
- [[ak-async-io-text#^f6]] [zig/memory] When the task returns an owned resource, the deferred cancel frees it on success and ignores errors: `defer if (a.cancel(io)) /s/ gpa.free(s) else /_/ {};` (A/strong, verified)
- [[bun-in-rust#^f1]] [zig/memory] Bun's main stability problem: lifetimes of GC values mixed with manually managed memory, giving small leaks and some crashes; every allocation had to be reviewed. `raw/bun-in-rust.md` (B/strong, verified)
- [[bun-in-rust#^f2]] [zig/memory] Not blamed on Zig in general: other Zig users do not have these bugs, and mixing GC with manual memory is uncommon. (B/strong, verified)
- [[bun-in-rust#^f3]] [zig/memory] Their Zig lifetime strategy: arenas where scope is clear (parser state, AST nodes), reference counting, and close attention. (B/strong, verified)
- [[bun-in-rust#^f4]] [zig/memory] [zig/errors] defer must be written at every call site, so cleanup is easy to forget (leak) or run twice in rarely reached error paths (double free). (B/strong, verified)
- [[bun-in-rust#^f5]] [zig/memory] Leaks of file paths in error-handling code were among those fixed by automatic cleanup; a Zig attempt at full leak tracking was not merged for lack of Drop. (B/strong, verified)
- [[bun-repo#^f7]] [zig/memory] One global default allocator (mimalloc) for almost everything. `src/CLAUDE.md`, `src/bun.zig` (A/fair, verified)
- [[bun-repo#^f8]] [zig/memory] [zig/errors] OOM crashes the process via `bun.handleOom(expr)`, which only catches `error.OutOfMemory`; `catch bun.outOfMemory()` is banned because it would swallow other errors too. `src/CLAUDE.md` (A/fair, verified)
- [[bun-repo#^f9]] [zig/memory] Rust-style smart pointers in Zig: `Shared(*T)`, `AtomicShared`, weak pointers, built from a comptime options struct; deref calls the value's `deinit`. `src/ptr/shared.zig` (A/fair, verified)
- [[bun-repo#^f10]] [zig/memory] [zig/safety] CheckedAllocator: in CI-assert builds, unmanaged containers record the allocator first used and assert every later call passes the same one. `src/safety/alloc.zig` (A/fair, verified)
- [[feng-talk-allocators#^f1]] [zig/memory] A bump (FixedBuffer) allocator only advances an end index; freeing the last allocation is an optimisation you can't depend on, but resetting the whole buffer is reliable. @12:40 (B/fair, verified)
- [[feng-talk-allocators#^f2]] [zig/memory] An arena fits work with a known lifetime, e.g. create at request start and free at request end. @17:11 (B/fair, verified)
- [[feng-talk-allocators#^f4]] [zig/memory] No default allocator is deliberate: different parts of a program want different trade-offs. @32:14 (B/fair, verified)
- [[ghostty-repo#^f10]] [zig/memory] Long-lived structs are unmanaged: Terminal stores no allocator, `deinit` takes one, and deinit ends by poisoning `self`. `src/terminal/Terminal.zig:376-385` (A/strong, verified)
- [[ghostty-repo#^f11]] [zig/memory] Config owns one arena for everything it parses; deinit frees it in one call; the parameter name says which allocator (`alloc_gpa` vs the arena). `src/config/Config.zig:4019,4041-4054` (A/strong, verified)
- [[ghostty-repo#^f12]] [zig/memory] A terminal page is one contiguous page-aligned block holding rows, cells and all metadata, so it can be copied or serialised as a unit. `src/terminal/page.zig:142-156` (A/strong, verified)
- [[ghostty-repo#^f13]] [zig/memory] Inside a page, references are typed offsets from the base, not pointers: `Offset(T)` is a packed struct over an int so it can carry methods. `src/terminal/size.zig:40-71` (A/strong, verified)
- [[ghostty-repo#^f14]] [zig/memory] Page backing memory comes from mmap/VirtualAlloc directly, not a Zig Allocator, for speed and guaranteed zeroed pages. `src/terminal/page.zig:30-37,234-240` (A/strong, verified)
- [[ghostty-repo#^f15]] [zig/memory] Zero is the empty state everywhere, so a freshly mapped page needs no initialisation of cells or metadata; a test pins `Cell` zero to empty. `src/terminal/page.zig:2135-2136,2777-2785` (A/strong, verified)
- [[ghostty-repo#^f16]] [zig/memory] Ghostty wrote UntouchedPool because std.heap.MemoryPool keeps its free list inside items, touching demand-paged memory. `src/datastruct/untouched_pool.zig:6-32` (A/strong, verified)
- [[ghostty-repo#^f17]] [zig/memory] Node pool avoids std MemoryPool because its arena backing wastes memory on growth. `src/terminal/PageList.zig:309-317` (A/strong, verified)
- [[ghostty-repo#^f18]] [zig/memory] A field with no default value forces every construction site to decide who owns the memory (pool vs heap). `src/terminal/PageList.zig:65-77` (A/strong, verified)
- [[ghostty-repo#^f19]] [zig/memory] Global allocator: libc malloc in ReleaseSafe/ReleaseFast (faster), the std safe/debug allocator in Debug for leak detection, libc under Valgrind. `src/global.zig:89-111` (A/strong, verified)
- [[ghostty-repo#^f20]] [zig/memory] OS queries take a caller buffer and return a slice into it; temporary needs use a stack FixedBufferAllocator. `src/os/homedir.zig:13,49-50` (A/strong, verified)
- [[ghostty-repo#^f21]] [zig/memory] An optional allocator in Options makes the VT stream fully allocation-free when absent; features needing heap are dropped. `src/terminal/stream.zig:22-26` (A/strong, verified)
- [[ghostty-repo#^f22]] [zig/memory] Pools are preheated with counts justified by use (one viewport pin, one cursor pin). `src/terminal/PageList.zig:46-49` (A/strong, verified)
- [[ghostty-repo#^f23]] [zig/memory] HACKING.md: Zig catches its own leaks but not C libraries', so a `run-valgrind` build step exists. `HACKING.md` (A/strong, verified)
- [[ghostty-repo#^f24]] [zig/memory] Global `alloc()` returns `std.testing.allocator` in tests, so code reaching global state still leak-checks. `src/global.zig:265-266` (A/strong, verified)
- [[liptak-check-all-allocation-failures#^f6]] [zig/memory] Typical find: two allocations in a row, the second fails, the first leaks until an `errdefer free` follows each one before ownership passes on. (A/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-aphorisms#^f4]] [zig/style] [zig/memory] Rename `alloc` to `gpa` (as opposed to `arena`), naming taken from the Zig compiler. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-comptime-orm#^f2]] [zig/memory] [zig/style] Allocator naming conveys lifetime: `gpa` (free individually), `arena` (no per-object free), `scratch` (short-lived, can't outlive the function). (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-comptime-orm#^f3]] [zig/api] [zig/memory] Only the call that gets an allocator can allocate, so allocation sites are visible from the call. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-comptime-orm#^f5]] [zig/memory] Zig pushes batching: `ensureTotalCapacity` once then `appendAssumeCapacity` without a gpa. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-comptime-orm#^f7]] [zig/memory] Table API splits fallible `reserve(gpa, extra)` from infallible `insert` with a capacity assert precondition. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-comptime-orm#^f12]] [zig/memory] [zig/errors] `create` reserves in objects and every index, then `errdefer comptime unreachable`, then inserts; else a value could land in only some indexes. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-comptime-orm#^f13]] [zig/memory] He is skeptical OOM can be handled consistently; "abort on OOM" camp. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-defer-patterns#^f1]] [zig/memory] (claim) Humans are not good at not forgetting defers, especially with optional ownership transfer (takes ownership unless an error is returned). (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-defer-patterns#^f2]] [zig/memory] [zig/perf] But defer discourages RAII-style per-object resources and pushes towards batching: many objects share one pool, e.g. all strings in one buffer. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-do-not-optimize-away#^f6]] [zig/memory] The benchmark function takes `arena: Allocator` for its inputs. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-error-context#^f4]] [zig/errors] [zig/memory] Pattern: `errdefer log.err("path={s}", .{path});` at the top of the block, then plain `try`s; context is per block and each frame in the call stack adds its own. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-finding-bugs#^f6]] [zig/test] [zig/memory] Keep iterations fast by reusing memory across iterations, "static allocation in the small". (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-hard-mode-rust#^f1]] [zig/memory] Critique of RAII: easy allocation encourages a sloppy attitude to resources, allocated and destroyed all over the place. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-hard-mode-rust#^f2]] [zig/memory] Costs: untested exhaustion paths, unpredictable resource use, poor performance vs batch allocation, spaghetti architecture. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-hard-mode-rust#^f3]] [zig/memory] Hard mode: only the small main binary asks the OS for resources; the library gets a fixed byte slice for all storage. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-hard-mode-rust#^f4]] [zig/memory] Scratch space: split memory into permanent and temporary halves; build a pointer tree in scratch, count, then allocate exact arrays in permanent and copy once. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-hard-mode-rust#^f5]] [zig/memory] He liked being able to tell precisely how much memory the program uses. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-invariant#^f4]] [zig/memory] TigerBeetle invariant: no allocation after startup, so everything has a numeric limit and you can't memcpy to ambient space. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-look-for-bugs#^f4]] [zig/memory] [zig/errors] Zig smell: an allocator and a `try` in the same scope need care (see reserve first). (B/fair, verified)
- [[matklad-reserve-first#^f1]] [zig/memory] [zig/errors] Bug 1 (internString in Andrew Kelley's talk): getOrPut inserts a key, then a later `try` fails, leaving an uninitialised table entry. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-reserve-first#^f2]] [zig/memory] [zig/errors] Bug 2 (Ghostty Atlas.grow): the errdefer restores `data_old` but the `defer alloc.free(data_old)` still frees it. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-reserve-first#^f4]] [zig/memory] Pattern: first reserve enough memory without changing the structure, then mutate in an error-free code path. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-reserve-first#^f6]] [zig/memory] Fixed internString: `ensureUnusedCapacityContext` and `ensureUnusedCapacity`, then `errdefer comptime unreachable`, then `getOrPutAssumeCapacityAdapted`. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-reserve-first#^f7]] [zig/memory] ensureUnusedCapacity holds all the failures but doesn't change the data structure; learnt from Andrew Kelley. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-reserve-first#^f8]] [zig/memory] Spicy take: Zig should drop `append` and rename `appendAssumeCapacity` to `append`; reserve in bulk up front. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-reserve-first#^f9]] [zig/memory] [zig/errors] Applications should consider aborting on OOM; he has seen only one (xit) pass a 30-minute errdefer grep. Libraries: leave allocation to the caller or test with an allocator that really fails. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-reserve-first#^f10]] [zig/memory] TigerBeetle reserves all resources in main and never allocates afterwards. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-static-alloc-compilers#^f1]] [zig/memory] TigerBeetle's static allocation means no allocation after startup, sized by runtime command line arguments, with no deallocation; not `static` arrays. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-static-alloc-compilers#^f2]] [zig/memory] It depends on the problem's physics: finite messages (1MiB) in and out mean processing needs no extra memory. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-static-alloc-compilers#^f3]] [zig/memory] (claim) In practice it is compositional, not constant vigilance; routing.zig is cited as an isolated non-allocating subsystem. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-static-alloc-compilers#^f4]] [zig/memory] Overload: unbounded concurrent messages are handled by dropping a message when resources are not available. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-static-alloc-compilers#^f5]] [zig/memory] (claim) Not allocating is simpler than allocating, if achievable. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-static-alloc-compilers#^f6]] [zig/memory] Contrast with a fixed arena: arena size is fixed but you can OOM; static allocation can't OOM but its size is known only after startup. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-static-alloc-compilers#^f7]] [zig/memory] Disk is not statically allocated in TigerBeetle: it can fail with ENOSPACE and has a dynamic block allocator. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-static-alloc-compilers#^f8]] [zig/memory] [zig/perf] Idea: an output arena for O(N) finished results plus O(1) processing of bounded chunks (e.g. a 4 MiB file limit, runtime-overridable), with indexes for output data. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-static-alloc-constant-work#^f1]] [zig/memory] [zig/safety] With a typed pool of T, a logical use-after-free aliases memory but causes no type confusion, unless the object stores an inline enum. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-static-alloc-constant-work#^f2]] [zig/memory] Hardening idea (from Fil-C): a typed allocation function (comptime T) can use type-segregated pools internally. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-static-alloc-constant-work#^f3]] [zig/memory] TigerStyle rule: no dynamic memory allocation after initialization; size from CLI (`--orders-max`), allocate in main with `gpa.alloc(Order, cli_args.orders_max)`, reject surplus requests. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-static-alloc-constant-work#^f4]] [zig/memory] Why: systems at capacity without strict limits fail catastrophically (OOM killer); a static system may fail to start but handles overload gracefully. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-static-alloc-constant-work#^f5]] [zig/memory] Pool shapes: slice plus `DynamicBitSet` of free slots, or a slice of `union { order: Order, next_free: ?u32 }` with `first_free`. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-static-alloc-constant-work#^f6]] [zig/memory] [zig/api] Constant work: always a fixed number of objects, with a neutral `pub const reserved: Order` value; init is `@memset(orders, .reserved)`. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-static-alloc-constant-work#^f12]] [zig/memory] He has never used generational indexes. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-value-interner#^f2]] [zig/memory] SegmentList: like ArrayList but growing never moves old items, so readers can hold references; 31 lazily allocated power-of-two segments cover u32 indexes. (C/fair, verified)
- [[matklad-zig-and-rust#^f2]] [zig/memory] TigerBeetle allocates all memory at startup and none after. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-zig-and-rust#^f5]] [zig/memory] The message pool is sized to the upper bound of messages in memory, so getting a message can't allocate or fail. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-zig-and-rust#^f7]] [zig/memory] (claim) Rewriting Rust in Zig would be more complex; a lot of "replace RAII with defer" Zig code has resource-management bugs. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-zig-and-rust#^f8]] [zig/memory] The answer is architecture with little resource management to do (allocate up front or at compile time). (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-zig-and-rust#^f10]] [zig/memory] Allocator passed in per method (call site dependency injection); TigerBeetle passes the allocator to `init` but not to the event loop, so the loop can't allocate. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-zig-and-rust#^f11]] [zig/memory] Request-scoped bump allocator is natural for servers; Zig's explicit allocator makes you choose. (B/strong, verified)
- [[mitchellh-ghostty-and-useful-zig-patterns#^f13]] [zig/memory] He admits memory use lagged CPU work, e.g. preallocating the whole scrollback; later pages are allocated on demand ([[ghostty-repo#^f22]]). (B/fair, verified)
- [[mitchellh-ghostty-gtk-rewrite#^f1]] [zig/memory] Keeping Zig-owned objects outside GObject's refcounting caused a whole class of bugs where one side's memory was freed and not the other's. (B/strong, verified)
- [[mitchellh-ghostty-gtk-rewrite#^f2]] [zig/memory] Fix: wrap the Zig Config in a reference-counted GObject, so the old config frees when its last reference goes, instead of updating the whole GUI before freeing. (B/strong, verified)
- [[mitchellh-ghostty-gtk-rewrite#^f3]] [zig/test] [zig/memory] Every change in the rewrite ran under Valgrind, with a suppression file mostly supplied by GTK. (B/strong, verified)
- [[mitchellh-ghostty-memory-leak-fix#^f1]] [zig/memory] Pages come from a pool of standard-size mmap blocks; bigger pages bypass the pool with their own mmap. (B/strong, verified)
- [[mitchellh-ghostty-memory-leak-fix#^f2]] [zig/memory] Free decided ownership from size: up to standard size back to the pool, larger munmap'd. (B/strong, verified)
- [[mitchellh-ghostty-memory-leak-fix#^f3]] [zig/memory] [zig/perf] Hot-path optimisation: at the scrollback limit the oldest page is reused as the newest, with zero allocations. (B/strong, verified)
- [[mitchellh-ghostty-memory-leak-fix#^f4]] [zig/memory] Bug: the reuse reset size metadata to standard but kept the large mapping, so free took it for a pool page and never munmap'd it. (B/strong, verified)
- [[mitchellh-ghostty-memory-leak-fix#^f5]] [zig/memory] Rare-by-design paths hide bugs until a new workload hits them (many graphemes plus heavy scrollback); one user saw 37 GB after 10 days. (B/strong, verified)
- [[mitchellh-ghostty-memory-leak-fix#^f6]] [zig/memory] Fix: never reuse a non-standard page; destroy it and take a fresh standard one. (B/strong, verified)
- [[mitchellh-ghostty-memory-leak-fix#^f7]] [zig/memory] He chose the simple fix over adaptive sizing until data says otherwise. (B/strong, verified)
- [[mitchellh-ghostty-memory-leak-fix#^f8]] [zig/memory] (inferred) The code has since stopped inferring ownership from size: Node carries an explicit `owned` enum with no default. See [[ghostty-repo#^f18]]. (B/strong, unquoted)
- [[mitchellh-ghostty-memory-leak-fix#^f9]] [zig/memory] An Allocator wrapper tags macOS VM regions so the PageList's memory is visible in OS tools; tests get testing.allocator. (B/strong, verified)
- [[mitchellh-ghostty-memory-leak-fix#^f10]] [zig/test] [zig/memory] Leak defences: leak-detecting allocators in debug builds and tests, Valgrind over the full unit suite in CI on every commit, Instruments for Swift, Valgrind on GTK PRs. (B/strong, verified)
- [[river-repo#^f7]] [zig/memory] One global allocator: `util.gpa = std.heap.c_allocator`, used everywhere (120 uses) rather than threading an allocator parameter; the binary links libc anyway. `river/util.zig` (A/strong, verified)
- [[river-repo#^f8]] [zig/memory] main still makes a short-lived arena on top of gpa for argument parsing. `river/main.zig` (A/strong, verified)
- [[river-repo#^f9]] [zig/memory] [zig/errors] Deliberately no leak cleanup on error in startup init, since the process exits anyway: simpler code. `river/Server.zig` (A/strong, verified)
- [[river-repo#^f10]] [zig/api] [zig/memory] Heap objects: `create()` allocates with `util.gpa.create`, `errdefer destroy`, fills `x.* = .{...}`, then registers listeners; the destroy listener frees itself. `river/XdgPopup.zig` (A/strong, verified)
- [[roc-zig-compiler#^f11]] [zig/perf] [zig/memory] Dense ids: identities are dense store-local integer `...Id`s with per-id data as parallel columns on the owning store, not hash maps; a lint rejects a HashMap keyed by an `...Id` type. `design.md` (B/strong, verified)
- [[roc-zig-compiler#^f13]] [zig/memory] [zig/errors] Every count that grows with input has a width chosen per field; narrowing a host-sized count is checked, and overflow returns `error.OutOfMemory` like a failed allocation, never an unchecked `@intCast`. `design.md`, `src/collections/safe_list.zig` (B/strong, verified)
- [[roc-zig-compiler#^f18]] [zig/memory] DebugAllocator configs must take the stack-trace depth from a build option (lint-enforced), so leak traces are tunable in one place. `ci/zig_lints.zig` (B/strong, verified)
- [[seguin-allocator-resize#^f1]] [zig/memory] `resize` promises not to move the pointer, so it is allowed to fail and return false; when it fails nothing changed. (B/fair, verified)
- [[seguin-allocator-resize#^f2]] [zig/memory] A successful `resize` doesn't change your slice's `len`; freeing with the old length crashed. (B/fair, verified)
- [[seguin-allocator-resize#^f3]] [zig/memory] When resize fails, fall back to alloc plus `@memcpy`. (B/fair, verified)
- [[seguin-allocator-resize#^f4]] [zig/memory] Checked, not there: `remap` and `realloc` (which handle the move case) are not covered. (B/fair, absence)
- [[seguin-arena-arraylist#^f1]] [zig/memory] An arena `free` only returns memory if it was the last allocation and on the arena's current node. (B/fair, verified)
- [[seguin-arena-arraylist#^f2]] [zig/memory] ArrayList growth tries remap first; on failure it allocates new, copies, and frees old, so the old buffer is never the last allocation and an arena never reclaims it. (B/fair, verified)
- [[seguin-arena-arraylist#^f3]] [zig/memory] The same applies to `std.Io.Writer.Allocating`. (B/fair, verified)
- [[seguin-arena-free-nested#^f1]] [zig/memory] Treat arena `free` as a no-op: only the most recent allocation is ever reclaimed; `deinit` is the real free. (B/fair, verified)
- [[seguin-arena-free-nested#^f2]] [zig/memory] Freeing in reverse order helps only within one internal node. (B/fair, verified)
- [[seguin-arena-free-nested#^f3]] [zig/memory] An arena on top of an arena works but gains nothing: the inner `deinit` rarely returns memory to the outer one. (B/fair, verified)
- [[seguin-assigning-arenas#^f1]] [zig/memory] Assignments, including returns, are copies; returning an array is fine, returning a slice of a local array dangles. (B/fair, verified)
- [[seguin-assigning-arenas#^f2]] [zig/memory] Bug: `.arena = arena` copies the arena, then `arena.allocator().dupe` mutates the local copy; deinit on the stored copy frees nothing, so it leaks (or crashes). (B/fair, verified)
- [[seguin-assigning-arenas#^f3]] [zig/memory] Taking `&arena` instead dangles: the local dies on return. (B/fair, verified)
- [[seguin-assigning-arenas#^f4]] [zig/memory] Robust fix: put the arena on the heap with `create`, `errdefer destroy`, and destroy it in `deinit` after `arena.deinit()`. (B/fair, verified)
- [[seguin-assigning-arenas#^f5]] [zig/memory] To save a field, store `ArenaAllocator.State` and `promote(allocator)` it in deinit. (B/fair, verified)
- [[seguin-assigning-arenas#^f6]] [zig/memory] Reordering fields so the copy happens after allocating works but is fragile. (B/fair, verified)
- [[seguin-assigning-arenas#^f7]] [zig/memory] The bug in pg.zig showed as a bus error after reallocations across copies. (B/fair, verified)
- [[seguin-getorput-string-keys#^f2]] [zig/memory] Pointers from `getPtr` die when the map grows; keep their scope small. (B/fair, verified)
- [[seguin-getorput-string-keys#^f3]] [zig/memory] He says removal doesn't shrink the map, so it doesn't move entries, but expects that to change. (B/fair, verified)
- [[seguin-getorput-string-keys#^f4]] [zig/memory] Duping the key before `getOrPut` leaks when the entry already exists. (B/fair, verified)
- [[seguin-getorput-string-keys#^f5]] [zig/memory] Right pattern: look up with the borrowed key; only on a new entry overwrite `gop.key_ptr.*` with an owned dupe. (B/fair, verified)
- [[seguin-getorput-string-keys#^f6]] [zig/memory] (inferred) Limit on ^f5: if the dupe fails after `getOrPut` inserted, the map holds a borrowed key and undefined value; pair it with `errdefer map.removeByPtr(gop.key_ptr)` or similar. (B/fair, unquoted)
- [[seguin-heap-value-address#^f1]] [zig/memory] Handing out `&local` during `init` and then returning the value by copy leaves the children pointing at the dead stack copy. (B/fair, verified)
- [[seguin-heap-value-address#^f3]] [zig/memory] Option two, more common: heap-allocate with `create`, return `*Pool`, and `destroy(self)` in `deinit`. (B/fair, verified)
- [[seguin-heap-value-address#^f4]] [zig/memory] `alloc`/`free` are for slices, `create`/`destroy` for single items. (B/fair, verified)
- [[seguin-learning-zig-coding-in-zig#^f1]] [zig/memory] A pointer from `getPtr` into a hash map is invalidated by `remove` (and by growth); reading it afterwards gives garbage. (B/fair, verified)
- [[seguin-learning-zig-coding-in-zig#^f2]] [zig/memory] Fixes: take a copy with `get`, or store pointers (`*const User`) so the map holds addresses of values it doesn't own. (B/fair, verified)
- [[seguin-learning-zig-coding-in-zig#^f3]] [zig/memory] Arguments and stored values are shallow copies: map and caller each own a separate copy. (B/fair, verified)
- [[seguin-learning-zig-coding-in-zig#^f4]] [zig/memory] Map keys must outlive the map; a key slice into a reused read buffer silently corrupts every key. (B/fair, verified)
- [[seguin-learning-zig-coding-in-zig#^f5]] [zig/memory] The fix is for the map's owner to `dupe` keys, and then free them before `deinit`: the map can't, because keys may be literals or from another allocator. (B/fair, verified)
- [[seguin-learning-zig-heap-memory#^f1]] [zig/memory] `try alloc` followed by `defer free` is the common, foolproof pairing; allocating in one place and freeing elsewhere is equally common. (B/fair, verified)
- [[seguin-learning-zig-heap-memory#^f2]] [zig/memory] Zig's `defer` runs at the end of its enclosing block, not the function (unlike Go). (B/fair, verified)
- [[seguin-learning-zig-heap-memory#^f3]] [zig/memory] In `init`, each allocation gets an `errdefer free` so a later failure undoes earlier allocations; he adds it even after the last fallible step so future edits stay safe. (B/fair, verified)
- [[seguin-learning-zig-heap-memory#^f5]] [zig/memory] Nothing enforces calling `deinit` exactly once. (B/fair, verified)
- [[seguin-learning-zig-heap-memory#^f6]] [zig/memory] Calling a function that returns allocated memory and dropping the result leaks: the caller owns it. (B/fair, verified)
- [[seguin-learning-zig-heap-memory#^f11]] [zig/memory] An arena suits short-lived state such as parser scratch: one `deinit` frees all, and `free`/`destroy` are no-ops. (B/fair, verified)
- [[seguin-learning-zig-heap-memory#^f12]] [zig/memory] The arena's limit: no individual frees, so it must be torn down before memory grows too far. (B/fair, verified)
- [[seguin-learning-zig-heap-memory#^f13]] [zig/memory] Code that receives an `Allocator` must not assume its implementation (still free what it allocates); the owner of the lifetime picks the allocator, not the container. (B/fair, verified)
- [[seguin-learning-zig-heap-memory#^f14]] [zig/memory] http.zig gives each response an arena cleared when the response is sent; a request is a natural arena lifetime. (B/fair, verified)
- [[seguin-learning-zig-heap-memory#^f15]] [zig/memory] FixedBufferAllocator: fast, hard-capped, frees only the last allocation (stack-like); reset and reuse is common. (B/fair, verified)
- [[seguin-learning-zig-heap-memory#^f16]] [zig/memory] Common app pattern: DebugAllocator in Debug builds with leak check at exit, `c_allocator` in release. (B/fair, verified)
- [[seguin-learning-zig-heap-memory#^f23]] [zig/memory] To outlive the frame, return a heap pointer from `create` and fill it with `ptr.* = .{...}`. (B/fair, verified)
- [[seguin-learning-zig-heap-memory#^f24]] [zig/memory] (inferred) Stale for 0.16+: building a DebugAllocator in `main` is replaced by `pub fn main(init: std.process.Init)` and `init.gpa`, which his Coding In Zig chapter already uses. (B/fair, unquoted)
- [[seguin-learning-zig-pointers#^f5]] [zig/memory] Copies are shallow: a copied struct's slice still points at the same bytes. (B/fair, verified)
- [[seguin-learning-zig-stack-memory#^f1]] [zig/memory] Returning a slice from `bufPrint` into a function-local buffer returns a dangling pointer: the buffer dies with the frame. (B/fair, verified)
- [[seguin-learning-zig-stack-memory#^f5]] [zig/memory] Hidden version: a parser that keeps a reference to its input outlives the input; nothing in the types enforces the contract, only docs or reading the code. (B/fair, verified)
- [[seguin-learning-zig-stack-memory#^f6]] [zig/memory] If a type needs its input to live as long as it does, it should copy it and own the copy. (B/fair, verified)
- [[seguin-leveraging-allocators#^f1]] [zig/memory] Handlers that allocate response data can't have it freed by the framework; a per-request arena solves it without API contortions. (B/fair, verified)
- [[seguin-leveraging-allocators#^f2]] [zig/memory] Arenas fit work with a short, well-defined lifetime like a request; he says use them more. (B/fair, verified)
- [[seguin-threads-freeing#^f1]] [zig/memory] A lock around the map doesn't stop one thread freeing a value another thread is still reading. (B/fair, verified)
- [[seguin-threads-freeing#^f2]] [zig/memory] Values stored inline in a hash map move when it grows; references to them become invalid. (B/fair, verified)
- [[seguin-threads-freeing#^f3]] [zig/memory] With threads, the rule becomes: free when the last reference goes; heap entries with an atomic refcount, the map holding one reference. (B/fair, verified)
- [[seguin-threads-freeing#^f4]] [zig/memory] Acquire must happen under the lock, or another thread can drop the count to zero in between. (B/fair, verified)
- [[seguin-threads-freeing#^f5]] [zig/memory] Many workloads keep clear single ownership across threads and don't need this. (B/fair, verified)
- [[seguin-threads-freeing#^f6]] [zig/memory] (inferred) His `release` uses `@atomicRmw(.Sub, ..., .monotonic)`; the usual safe ordering is `.release` on the decrement plus an acquire fence or `.acq_rel` before freeing. (B/fair, verified)
- [[seguin-writer-allocating-memory#^f1]] [zig/memory] A 1025-byte `writeAll` into a 1024-capacity `Writer.Allocating` grows it to 3204 bytes; an `ArrayList(u8)` grows to 1665. (C/fair, verified)
- [[seguin-writer-allocating-memory#^f2]] [zig/memory] Cause he reports: `drain` reserves the splat length for every slice, so a plain write reserves twice its size. (C/fair, verified)
- [[tigerbeetle-architecture#^f1]] [zig/memory] "Static allocation" means: at startup compute the worst-case count of every object type from CLI arguments, allocate exactly that, then never allocate again; not `.bss` statics and not an arena that can still run out. (A/strong, verified)
- [[tigerbeetle-architecture#^f2]] [zig/memory] Static allocation is a forcing function for limits; limits give backpressure for free and bound concurrency, since each "future" is a struct field owned by a component. Speed and low latency variance are a consequence, not the main reason. (A/strong, verified)
- [[tigerbeetle-architecture#^f5]] [zig/memory] [zig/api] Why Zig: no GC needed; Rust's temporal and thread safety matter less under static allocation and one thread; idiomatic Zig containers take the allocator per method, not in the constructor, which fits static allocation. (A/strong, verified)
- [[tigerbeetle-architecture#^f11]] [zig/memory] [zig/api] Async IO without heap-allocated contexts: each component owns fixed arrays of operation structs, linked by `IO` into an intrusive list; total concurrency is the sum of per-component limits. (A/strong, verified)
- [[tigerbeetle-architecture#^f16]] [zig/memory] (inferred) Checked, not there: ARCHITECTURE.md never claims static allocation suits every program; it justifies it through database properties (overload, bounded concurrency, determinism, byte-identical replicas). The domain framing is implicit, not a stated caveat. (A/strong, absence)
- [[tigerbeetle-blog-io-abstraction#^f3]] [zig/api] [zig/memory] Requests that don't fit the ring go to an overflow queue made of intrusive completion structs rather than allocations. (B/fair, verified)
- [[tigerbeetle-blog-no-dynamic-memory#^f1]] [zig/memory] Zig's std containers suit static allocation: `AssumeCapacity` methods (`addOneAssumeCapacity`, `putAssumeCapacityNoClobber`) catch accidental growth. (B/fair, verified)
- [[tigerbeetle-blog-no-dynamic-memory#^f2]] [zig/memory] Fixed limits handle overload: no buffer grows when a client misbehaves. (B/fair, verified)
- [[tigerbeetle-blog-no-dynamic-memory#^f3]] [zig/memory] [zig/comptime] Memory needed is a comptime sum over every user of a message, each term commented with its owner. (B/fair, verified)
- [[tigerbeetle-blog-no-dynamic-memory#^f4]] [zig/memory] [zig/test] Wrong sizing is caught by assertions plus fuzzing rather than by resource exhaustion in production. (B/fair, verified)
- [[tigerbeetle-blog-no-dynamic-memory#^f5]] [zig/memory] The dynamic part of a database is on disk; only the in-memory LSM portion is bounded and allocated at startup. (B/fair, verified)
- [[tigerbeetle-blog-no-dynamic-memory#^f6]] [zig/memory] (inferred) The precondition is that the program can bound every input up front (connections, message size, batch size); a crawler fed by an external API or a site generator over growing data cannot, without capping its input. (B/fair, unquoted)
- [[tigerbeetle-code#^f10]] [zig/memory] Static allocation is enforced mechanically: the replica allocates through a `StaticAllocator` that asserts on any alloc, resize or remap once switched to `.static` after `open`. `static_allocator.zig`, `vsr/replica.zig` (A/strong, verified)
- [[tigerbeetle-code#^f11]] [zig/memory] Free is allowed after init (moving to `.deinit`) so `errdefer` cleanup stays simple; once freeing starts, no more allocation. `static_allocator.zig` (A/strong, verified)
- [[tigerbeetle-code#^f13]] [zig/memory] [zig/errors] Init with errdefer guarded by an `initialized` flag, so the owner deinitialises components only on the error path before handover. `vsr/replica.zig` (A/strong, verified)
- [[tigerbeetle-code#^f19]] [zig/memory] [zig/safety] `stdx.copy_disjoint` replaces `@memcpy`: asserts lengths (`.exact` or `.inexact` spelled at the call site) and that the slices don't overlap; `copy_left`/`copy_right` replace `copyForwards`/`copyBackwards` with direction asserts. `stdx/stdx.zig` (A/strong, verified)
- [[tigerbeetle-code#^f27]] [zig/memory] [zig/api] `BoundedArrayType(T, capacity)`: TigerBeetle's own fixed capacity array (std's is banned); `push` asserts not full rather than returning an error; count stored as u32 behind `count()`. `stdx/bounded_array.zig` (A/strong, verified)
- [[tigerbeetle-code#^f31]] [zig/api] [zig/memory] IO operations take a caller-owned `*Completion`, a typed comptime callback and context; `erase_types` builds the `*anyopaque` trampoline at comptime, so no allocation per operation. `io/linux.zig` (A/strong, verified)
- [[tigerbeetle-tidy#^f11]] [zig/style] [zig/memory] A `defer` must be followed by a blank line (or another defer/errdefer or a closing brace), enforcing Tiger Style's grouping of a resource with its cleanup. `src/tidy.zig` (A/strong, verified)
- [[tigerbeetle-tiger-style#^f13]] [zig/memory] All memory statically allocated at startup; nothing allocated or freed after init. Why: predictable performance, no use-after-free, and simpler designs from considering all memory up front. (A/strong, verified)
- [[tigerbeetle-tiger-style#^f29]] [zig/style] [zig/memory] Allocator names carry meaning: `gpa` and `arena` tell the reader whether `deinit` must be called. (A/strong, verified)
- [[tigerbeetle-tiger-style#^f39]] [zig/api] [zig/memory] Construct large structs in place via an out pointer (`fn init(target: *T) !void`), for pointer stability and no stack copies; in-place init is viral up the containing struct. (A/strong, verified)
- [[tigerbeetle-tiger-style#^f43]] [zig/memory] [zig/style] Blank lines group a resource allocation with its `defer`, so leaks are easier to spot. (A/strong, verified)
- [[tigerbeetle-tiger-style-talk#^f6]] [zig/memory] Limits make overload handling and backpressure possible; static allocation is one case of everything having a limit, and uses less memory. @34:37 (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-011-release-notes#^f11]] [zig/memory] Allocators may refuse to shrink; ArrayList tries resize in place and copies only the used items otherwise, instead of realloc copying spare capacity. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-011-release-notes#^f12]] [zig/memory] mem.span and mem.len restricted to sentinel-terminated pointers, called footgunny on arrays and slices. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-013-release-notes#^f2]] [zig/memory] PriorityQueue changed to store items plus cap like ArrayList; the old full-slice items field led to reading undefined memory. (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-014-release-notes#^f6]] [zig/memory] Unmanaged containers are initialised with `.empty`, not `.{}`; GeneralPurposeAllocator with `.init`; the default field values were deprecated. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-014-release-notes#^f15]] [zig/memory] GeneralPurposeAllocator rewritten and renamed DebugAllocator. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-014-release-notes#^f16]] [zig/memory] SmpAllocator: a singleton for ReleaseFast multithreaded, ~200 lines, matching glibc malloc building Zig. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-014-release-notes#^f17]] [zig/memory] Recommended main: DebugAllocator in Debug and ReleaseSafe, smp_allocator in ReleaseFast and ReleaseSmall, deinit only when debugging. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-014-release-notes#^f18]] [zig/memory] Allocator.VTable gains remap (may relocate; null means caller copies); resize stays for arenas that must not move. Alignment is typed mem.Alignment. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-014-release-notes#^f19]] [zig/memory] Page size is runtime-known: std.mem.page_size removed for heap.page_size_min/max and heap.pageSize(). (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-014-release-notes#^f21]] [zig/memory] Managed containers deprecated (ArrayHashMap, ArrayList); veterans converged on unmanaged: no duplicated allocator, and the allocator parameter marks which calls can allocate, fitting reserve-then-assume-capacity. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-015-release-notes#^f21]] [zig/memory] Linked lists de-genericised: embed std.DoublyLinkedList.Node and use @fieldParentPtr; intrusive is preferred, and if not, a list is probably wrong. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-015-release-notes#^f22]] [zig/memory] ArrayList is unmanaged by default (managed is array_list.Managed, to be removed): no allocator field is the null hypothesis; costs of the field outweigh the wrong-allocator protection, which safety checks catch anyway. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-015-release-notes#^f23]] [zig/memory] LinearFifo and RingBuffer removed; a comptime enum choosing buffer ownership made three data structures in one; ask first whether Io.Reader/Writer is what you need. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-015-release-notes#^f24]] [zig/memory] BoundedArray removed. Arbitrary limit: don't guess, take a caller slice or allocate; stack buffer: ArrayList initBuffer plus *Bounded methods. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-016-release-notes#^f18]] [zig/memory] ArenaAllocator is thread-safe and lock-free, so it needs no Io and can back an Io; ThreadSafeAllocator removed as an anti-pattern (a mutex needs Io and is slow; make allocators lock-free instead). (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-016-release-notes#^f22]] [zig/memory] Unmanaged is the only container style; "managed" goes. ArrayHashMap family removed for array_hash_map.Auto/String/Custom; PriorityQueue/Dequeue lose the allocator field, init with `.empty`, add/remove renamed push/pop. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-017-release-notes#^f24]] [zig/memory] StackFallbackAllocator renamed BufferFirstAllocator and takes the buffer as an argument, like other std APIs; the old one was generic over size and `.get()` mutated it. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-017-release-notes#^f25]] [zig/memory] SafeAllocator replaces DebugAllocator (deprecated): thread-safe, never reuses memory, deinit reports all leaks, mismatches and double frees panic or segfault; fuzz-tested. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-017-release-notes#^f26]] [zig/memory] ArrayList: getLastOrNull is `last()`, getLast is `last().?`; lastPtr added; ArrayList gains pointer stability checks. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-017-release-notes#^f30]] [zig/memory] bit_set renamed (Integer, Array, Static, Dynamic) with the managed dynamic one deprecated. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-clap-repo#^f11]] [zig/api] [zig/memory] Options struct with defaults: the allocator is a required field; the rest have defaults and doc comments. `clap.zig` (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-clap-repo#^f12]] [zig/memory] The result owns an arena holding every allocation; `deinit` frees it in one call, and the doc says the caller owns it. `clap.zig` (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-clap-repo#^f13]] [zig/memory] `errdefer arena.deinit()` right after creating the arena, before any fallible call. `clap.zig` (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-compiler-dod#^f9]] [zig/memory] `parse` uses `defer tokens.deinit` then `toOwnedSlice` + `errdefer` on the slice: ownership moves once, and cleanup follows it. `std/zig/Ast.zig:167` (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-compiler-dod#^f19]] [zig/memory] The pool stores `ArenaAllocator.State`, not a full arena, per thread (the memory-saving form the arena docs offer). `src/InternPool.zig:975` (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-compiler-dod#^f20]] [zig/memory] Insert path: reserve with `ensureUnusedCapacity(1)` first, then `appendAssumeCapacity` in each branch, so the switch body cannot fail midway. `src/InternPool.zig:7263` (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-compiler-dod#^f22]] [zig/memory] Removed entries keep their slot (tag `removed`) so indices held elsewhere stay valid. `src/InternPool.zig:4790` (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-devlog-2025#^f8]] [zig/memory] [zig/comptime] DebugAllocator rework: metadata inline on the page, slot counts computed by a comptime function, with an assert that turns bad constants into a compile error instead of a handcrafted table. "No-Libc Zig Now Outperforms Glibc Zig" (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-devlog-2025#^f9]] [zig/memory] [zig/perf] The DebugAllocator rework cut a debug ast-check run from 22.8 s to 20.5 s (10.1%) and peak RSS 6.2%. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-devlog-2025#^f10]] [zig/memory] SmpAllocator is designed for multi-threaded ReleaseFast programs: thread local state to avoid contention, cross-thread reclaim for producer-consumer patterns. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-devlog-2025#^f11]] [zig/memory] [zig/perf] SmpAllocator vs glibc malloc on one ast-check: 4.9% faster wall time, 12.1% less RSS; whole compiler self-build: no wall-time difference. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-devlog-2025#^f12]] [zig/memory] [toolchain] Page size is runtime-known since 0.14; Kelley calls making it comptime-known his mistake. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-devlog-2026#^f12]] [zig/api] [zig/memory] Construction pattern in that example: allocator first, then `var threaded: std.Io.Threaded = .init(gpa, .{...})`, `defer threaded.deinit()`, pass `threaded.io()` down. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-devlog-2026#^f13]] [zig/memory] [zig/safety] `ArrayList.lockPointers()` / `unlockPointers()` assert that the list does not reallocate (or reorder) while pointers into it are held, turning a silent dangling slice into a panic with a stack trace. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-devlog-2026#^f14]] [zig/memory] Slices into an ArrayList dangle after any growth; `pop` and `orderedRemove` also trip the lock since ordered lists move elements. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-a#^f2]] [zig/memory] Robustness is defined to include out of memory: OOM is an edge case code must handle correctly, not ignore. Introduction (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-a#^f19]] [zig/memory] `const` covers only the bytes the name addresses; pointee const-ness is part of the pointer type. Assignment (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-a#^f32]] [zig/test] [zig/memory] `std.testing.allocator` makes the runner report leaks and fail the run. The 0.17 example: unmanaged `ArrayList` initialised `.empty`, allocator named `gpa` passed per call, leak report from `SafeAllocator` (new in 0.17). Report Memory Leaks (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-a#^f57]] [zig/memory] Pointer kinds and their purpose: `*T` one item, `[*]T` unknown count (indexing, arithmetic), `*[N]T` array pointer with `.len`, `[]T` slice = many-pointer plus length. Pointers (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-a#^f106]] [zig/memory] The 0.17 dispatch example uses a stack-buffer `ArrayList` with `initBuffer` and `appendBounded` (returns an error when full) instead of an allocator. Labeled switch (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-a#^f116]] [zig/memory] `defer` runs at scope exit (block, not function), in reverse order, only if the defer statement was reached; it can't `return`. defer (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-a#^f117]] [zig/memory] Checked, not there: `errdefer` is not in part A (it is under Errors, part B, [[zig-langref-017-b]]). defer (A/fair, absence)
- [[zig-langref-017-b#^f7]] [zig/errors] [zig/memory] `errdefer` runs only when the block exits with an error; the cleanup sits right after the acquisition, so every exit path is covered. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-b#^f16]] [zig/errors] [zig/memory] Allocation failure is an error (`error.OutOfMemory`), not a crash; the doc rejects the "Linux overcommits anyway" argument (Windows, embedded and real-time don't overcommit; OOM killer is nondeterministic; libraries get reused). (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-b#^f57]] [zig/memory] Don't call @wasmMemoryGrow/@wasmMemorySize unless writing an allocator; use `std.heap.WasmPageAllocator`. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-b#^f70]] [zig/safety] [zig/memory] Unchecked IB the doc names: dangling pointers into a returned stack frame, missing asm clobbers, @fieldParentPtr on the wrong field. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-b#^f73]] [zig/memory] [zig/api] No default allocator: functions that allocate take an `Allocator`. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-b#^f74]] [zig/memory] [zig/api] Allocator choice 1: libraries accept an Allocator and let the user decide. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-b#^f75]] [zig/memory] Allocator choice 2-3: linking libc, `std.heap.c_allocator`; bound known at comptime, `FixedBufferAllocator`. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-b#^f76]] [zig/memory] Allocator choice 4: a run-to-completion CLI uses one arena over page_allocator, freed once with `arena.deinit()`. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-b#^f77]] [zig/memory] [zig/perf] Allocator choice 5: cyclical work (frame, request) uses an arena per cycle, or a FixedBufferAllocator if the cycle has a memory bound. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-b#^f78]] [zig/memory] [zig/test] Allocator choice 6-7: tests use `std.testing.allocator` (leak detection), and `std.testing.FailingAllocator` to test OOM handling. (A/strong, verified)
