- [[zig-langref-017-b#^f79]] [zig/memory] Allocator choice 8-9: otherwise a general purpose allocator, set up once in main and passed down: DebugAllocator in debug, `smp_allocator` in fast. Checked, not there: the name GeneralPurposeAllocator. (A/strong, absence)
- [[zig-langref-017-b#^f80]] [zig/memory] Where bytes live: string literals and comptime-known consts in constant data; function `var`s on the stack; container-level `var`s in global data; allocations where the allocator puts them. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-b#^f81]] [zig/memory] [zig/api] Document ownership and lifetime of every returned pointer ("caller owns the returned memory"); e.g. ArrayList `.items` dies on the next resize. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-b#^f82]] [zig/memory] [zig/safety] Recursion is unbounded memory, and there is no stack overflow protection yet. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-learn-overview#^f6]] [zig/memory] Programmers must manage memory and handle allocation failure; every allocating std function takes an allocator, so std works freestanding. (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-learn-overview#^f7]] [zig/memory] [zig/errors] defer and errdefer cover all resource management, not only memory: each acquisition is followed by its errdefer release. (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-learn-samples#^f1]] [zig/memory] [zig/test] Leak detection: `std.heap.DebugAllocator(.{})` with `defer std.debug.assert(debug_allocator.deinit() == .ok)` reports leaks with traces. (A/weak, verified)
- [[zig-learn-samples#^f2]] [zig/api] [zig/memory] The generic Queue sample stores its allocator in a field named `gpa` and takes it in `init`. (A/weak, verified)
- [[zig-learn-samples#^f5]] [zig/memory] The cURL sample keeps a deinit on an arena-backed list on purpose, in case the allocator changes. (A/weak, verified)
- [[zig-learn-why#^f2]] [zig/memory] [zig/api] No hidden allocations, because hidden allocation limits where code can be reused. (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-learn-why#^f3]] [zig/memory] [zig/api] Convention: every std feature that heap allocates takes an `Allocator` parameter. (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-learn-why#^f4]] [zig/memory] Allocator roles named: the debug allocator catches use-after-free, double free and leaks; the arena frees many allocations at once. (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-repo-docs#^f14]] [zig/api] [zig/memory] main gets `std.process.Init`: `init.arena` for process-lifetime allocations, `init.io` for I/O; stdout through a stack buffer and `Io.File.Writer`, then flush. `lib/init/src/main.zig:10` (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-repo-docs#^f16]] [zig/memory] [zig/test] Unmanaged containers in 0.17: `var list: std.ArrayList(i32) = .empty; defer list.deinit(gpa); try list.append(gpa, 42);` with `std.testing.allocator` catching leaks. `lib/init/src/main.zig:35` (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-std-allocators#^f5]] [zig/memory] (inferred) Since the interface stores `&self`, the implementation must not be moved or copied after `allocator()` is called: keep it in a `var` with a stable address. (A/strong, unquoted)
- [[zig-std-allocators#^f11]] [zig/memory] ArenaAllocator wraps a child allocator; individual frees only reclaim the latest allocation; it is threadsafe in 0.17 if the child is. `std/heap/ArenaAllocator.zig:1` (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-std-allocators#^f12]] [zig/memory] Arena `State` can be stored instead of the whole arena (no child allocator copy) and promoted back; `State.init` is a decl literal. `std/heap/ArenaAllocator.zig:13` (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-std-allocators#^f15]] [zig/memory] FixedBufferAllocator bumps an index over a caller buffer and offers a separate lock-free `threadSafeAllocator()` vtable; mixing the two is documented unsafe. `std/heap/FixedBufferAllocator.zig:31` (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-std-allocators#^f16]] [zig/memory] [toolchain] 0.17 adds `std.heap.SafeAllocator`; `DebugAllocator` and its Config are deprecated aliases. `std/heap.zig:14` (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-std-allocators#^f21]] [zig/memory] DebugAllocator's doc is candid that its checks cost: over 100 bytes of overhead per one-byte allocation, so it is a debug tool, not a production heap. `std/heap/debug_allocator.zig:20` (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-std-allocators#^f22]] [zig/memory] start.zig picks main's gpa by mode: SafeAllocator in Debug and ReleaseSafe, c_allocator when linking libc, smp_allocator otherwise. `std/start.zig:780` (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-std-allocators#^f28]] [zig/memory] [toolchain] Checked, not there in 0.17 lib/std (grep): `GeneralPurposeAllocator`, `process.argsAlloc`, `argsWithAllocator`; use SafeAllocator (or main's `init.gpa`) and `init.minimal.args`. (A/strong, absence)
- [[zig-std-containers#^f4]] [zig/memory] `deinit` takes `*Self` plus the allocator and ends with `self.* = undefined`, so use after deinit is caught in safe builds. `std/array_list.zig:690` (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-std-containers#^f7]] [zig/memory] `initBuffer` backs a list with caller memory; allocator-taking methods are then illegal behaviour, so only AssumeCapacity/Bounded methods may be used. `std/array_list.zig:677` (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-std-containers#^f8]] [zig/memory] `toOwnedSlice` transfers ownership and leaves the list empty, trying `remap` before a copy; doc comment states who owns the result. `std/array_list.zig:745` (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-std-io#^f14]] [zig/memory] (inferred) Because the vtable fn uses @fieldParentPtr, the interface must be used through `&impl.interface` and never copied out: a copied Writer points its parent computation at the wrong memory. (A/strong, unquoted)
- [[zig-std-io#^f16]] [zig/memory] `Writer.Allocating` grows an allocator-backed buffer and converts to and from `ArrayList(u8)` without copying, taking ownership. `std/Io/Writer.zig:2695` (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-std-io#^f23]] [zig/memory] `allocRemaining` takes a `Limit`, so reading a whole stream always names a maximum and fails with StreamTooLong past it. `std/Io/Reader.zig:286` (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-std-json-fmt-args#^f3]] [zig/memory] `parseFromSlice` returns `Parsed(T)`, a value plus the heap-allocated arena owning everything it points to; one `deinit` frees it all. `std/json/static.zig:56` (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-std-json-fmt-args#^f4]] [zig/memory] The `*Leaky` variant takes any allocator and makes no attempt to free; the doc tells you to pass an arena. Two entry points, one per ownership model. `std/json/static.zig:86` (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-std-json-fmt-args#^f20]] [zig/memory] Build.zig allocates from a process arena and panics on OOM (58 times in the file): for a short-lived tool, OOM is fatal by design. `std/Build.zig:181` (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-talk-async-io-zigtoberfest#^f3]] [zig/errors] [zig/memory] Footgun: `try a.await(io)` before awaiting `b` leaks `b` when `a` fails (the leak checker catches it). @11:26 (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-talk-async-io-zigtoberfest#^f4]] [zig/errors] [zig/memory] Rule: if you async, defer a cancel, as you defer a free after allocating; then `try ... await` is safe and failure cancels the rest at once. @13:26 (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-talk-async-io-zigtoberfest#^f6]] [zig/memory] Treat a task like a resource (an open file, an allocation): defer cancel is structured concurrency; returning the future is the advanced case. @18:01 (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-talk-async-io-zigtoberfest#^f8]] [zig/memory] When the async function returns an allocated result, the deferred cancel must free it on success too: `defer if (fut.cancel(io)) /s/ gpa.free(s) else /_/ {}` shape. (inferred from the demo description) @26:41 (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-talk-async-io-zigtoberfest#^f14]] [zig/api] [zig/memory] Pattern from the compiler: a context struct holds gpa and io as fields and functions take the context first; free functions take io and allocator directly. @50:51 (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-talk-kelley-dod#^f4]] [zig/perf] [zig/memory] Use u32 indexes into arrays instead of pointers: halves the field on 64-bit and drops alignment from 8 to 4. @13:48 (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-talk-kelley-flush#^f1]] [zig/memory] Thread stacks are heap memory too, so large stack buffers are not free once code runs concurrently. @2:06 (A/strong, verified)
- [[ziggit-choosing-allocator#^f1]] [zig/memory] squeek502: with the pass-an-allocator convention you rarely choose an allocator outside `main`, bar the odd arena; libraries don't choose at all. (B/fair, verified)
- [[ziggit-choosing-allocator#^f3]] [zig/memory] The 0.14 notes' pattern: DebugAllocator in Debug/ReleaseSafe, `smp_allocator` in ReleaseFast/Small, leak check only when debugging. (B/fair, verified)
- [[ziggit-choosing-allocator#^f4]] [zig/memory] DebugAllocator is thread-safe by default (configurable off). (B/fair, verified)
- [[ziggit-choosing-allocator#^f5]] [zig/memory] Share one allocator across threads whenever memory is freed on a different thread from where it was allocated. (B/fair, verified)
- [[ziggit-choosing-allocator#^f6]] [zig/memory] `std.process.cleanExit` verifies cleanup in debug builds and just exits in release. (B/fair, verified)
- [[ziggit-code-smells#^f5]] [zig/memory] Allocators in many instances suggest pooling (data-oriented) or unmanaged containers sharing the parent's allocator. (C/fair, verified)
- [[ziggit-code-smells#^f6]] [zig/memory] Smell: consecutive `try allocator.alloc` lines without `errdefer`, leaking on partial failure. (C/fair, verified)
- [[ziggit-errdefer-double-free#^f2]] [zig/memory] After ownership moves to another object, end the `errdefer` with a block, or a later failure frees memory the new owner also frees. (B/strong, verified)
- [[ziggit-errdefer-scope#^f3]] [zig/memory] Reserve capacity first and the function may need no `try` and no errdefer. (C/fair, verified)
- [[ziggit-invalidate-pointers#^f1]] [zig/memory] A pointer can't tell you whether what it points at is still valid. (C/fair, verified)
- [[ziggit-invalidate-pointers#^f3]] [zig/memory] Generation-counted index handles fix dangling references cheaply (zig-gamedev zpool). (C/fair, verified)
- [[zigguide-allocators#^f2]] [zig/memory] Arena: allocate many times, free once with `deinit`; `free` on it is a no-op. (B/fair, verified)
- [[zigguide-allocators#^f3]] [zig/memory] DebugAllocator trades speed for safety: prevents double free and use-after-free, detects leaks. (B/fair, verified)
- [[zls-repo#^f7]] [zig/memory] [zig/build] Build-script OOM is not handled: `catch @panic("OOM")` in build.zig only (4 uses in all of src/ and build.zig). `build.zig` (A/fair, verified)
- [[zls-repo#^f8]] [zig/memory] Allocator chosen once in main: DebugAllocator in Debug (and single-threaded release), c_allocator when linking libc, wasm_allocator on wasi, else smp_allocator; optional tracy and randomly failing wrappers stack on top. `src/main.zig` (A/fair, verified)
- [[zls-repo#^f9]] [zig/test] [zig/memory] A build option turns on a FailingAllocator that fails 1 in N allocations at random in the real binary, to exercise OOM paths beyond unit tests. `src/testing.zig` (A/fair, verified)
- [[zls-repo#^f13]] [zig/memory] create pairs every acquisition with errdefer; fields not ready at struct init are `undefined` with a `// set below` comment. `src/Server.zig` (A/fair, verified)
- [[zls-repo#^f14]] [zig/memory] [zig/safety] deinit ends with `self.* = undefined;` so use-after-deinit shows as 0xaa garbage in safe builds. `src/DocumentStore.zig` (A/fair, verified)
- [[zls-repo#^f15]] [zig/memory] Init functions build the value, `errdefer value.deinit(allocator)` right after, then return it: deinit doubles as the error-path cleanup, so it must cope with a partly filled value. `src/DocumentScope.zig`, `src/main.zig` (A/fair, verified)
- [[zls-repo#^f16]] [zig/memory] Unmanaged containers default to `.empty` as field defaults and take the allocator on each call (0.15+ style). `src/Server.zig` (A/fair, verified)
- [[zls-repo#^f17]] [zig/memory] Request handlers get a per-request arena (92 `arena:` parameters in src/) alongside the long-lived gpa. `src/Server.zig` (A/fair, verified)

### zig/perf: data-oriented design, memory layout, SoA, SIMD, hot loops (222)

- [[ak-bun-rust-rewrite#^f4]] [zig/comptime] [zig/perf] The team warned Bun about comptime abuse for years and built the time report (0.15) to audit comptime and inline cost on compile times. (B/fair, verified)
- [[ak-bun-rust-rewrite#^f5]] [zig/build] [zig/perf] LTO was supported all along (it was on by default until LLVM bugs). (B/fair, verified)
- [[ak-bun-rust-rewrite#^f6]] [zig/build] [zig/perf] Compile speed reference: the 600k-line Zig compiler builds from clean in 16 s and rebuilds an edit in 90 ms with incremental. (B/fair, verified)
- [[bun-in-rust#^f9]] [zig/comptime] [zig/perf] Too much comptime: the port cut the binary by 3.8 to 6.8 MB per platform, largely because of comptime overuse in the Zig code. (B/strong, verified)
- [[bun-in-rust#^f11]] [zig/perf] Large Zig functions with nested scopes used much stack (no LLVM lifetime markers, zig issue 23475); they split big functions as a workaround. (B/strong, verified)
- [[bun-repo#^f14]] [zig/build] [zig/perf] Zig was one compilation unit: a debug build took about 2.5 minutes on Zig changes, much slower than C++ which is many units. `CONTRIBUTING.md` (A/fair, verified)
- [[feng-talk-allocators#^f3]] [zig/perf] Prefer arrays to linked structures for cache friendliness (slab allocators). @26:12 (B/fair, verified)
- [[ghostty-repo#^f73]] [zig/perf] Row and Cell are `packed struct(u64)` so one integer load reads every field; Backing is derived from type info, not hardcoded. `src/terminal/page.zig:2008,2092-2095,2137` (A/strong, verified)
- [[ghostty-repo#^f74]] [zig/perf] Cell arrays start on a cache line so rows don't straddle an extra line. `src/terminal/page.zig:129-140` (A/strong, verified)
- [[ghostty-repo#^f75]] [zig/perf] A `lanes(T)` helper returns twice the suggested vector length, or null when the target has no SIMD, in which case a scalar loop runs. `src/simd/main.zig:12-22` (A/strong, verified)
- [[ghostty-repo#^f76]] [zig/perf] Heaviest SIMD kernels are C++ (Highway) behind `extern "c"`, with a Zig scalar fallback chosen at comptime. `src/simd/index_of.zig:4-17` (A/strong, verified)
- [[ghostty-repo#^f77]] [zig/perf] Micro-choices cite measurement: zeroing a page as u64 rather than u8. `src/terminal/page.zig:330-332` (A/strong, verified)
- [[ghostty-repo#^f78]] [zig/perf] wasm builds default to simd128, a 50%+ win on VT streams. `src/build/Config.zig:118-124` (A/strong, verified)
- [[ghostty-repo#^f79]] [zig/perf] log_level is info outside Debug so expensive debug-log arguments compile away. `src/main_ghostty.zig:201-211` (A/strong, verified)
- [[ghostty-repo#^f80]] [zig/perf] Plain implementations first, admitted in docs: the SPSC queue is deliberately naive until benchmarks say otherwise. `src/datastruct/blocking_queue.zig:24-28` (A/strong, verified)
- [[ghostty-repo#^f81]] [zig/perf] Integer widths follow domain limits (u16 cells per dimension), declared once in size.zig and derived elsewhere; one comment there is stale (says u16, value is maxInt(u32)). `src/terminal/size.zig:4-22` (A/strong, verified)
- [[ghostty-repo#^f97]] [zig/perf] [toolchain] The everyday SIMD idiom at 0.17, in the VT print scan: `@splat` constants, whole-vector loads, `@reduce(.Or, ...)`, `@bitCast` to an `@Int` mask, `@ctz`, then the scalar loop as tail. The comment says the loop didn't auto-vectorise. `src/terminal/stream.zig:869-894` (A/strong, verified)
- [[kristoff-fix-your-asserts#^f6]] [zig/perf] In unchecked modes the optimiser propagates `unreachable` facts, sometimes non-locally. (A/strong, verified)
- [[kristoff-fix-your-asserts#^f8]] [zig/perf] Expensive assert computations are not elided in release; guard them with a comptime `if (builtin.mode == .Debug)`. (A/strong, verified)
- [[kristoff-zig-new-async-io#^f5]] [zig/perf] `Io` is a non-generic vtable: less code bloat, virtual-call cost; with a single implementation in the program calls are to be guaranteed de-virtualised. (A/strong, verified)
- [[kristoff-zig-new-async-io#^f6]] [zig/perf] Buffering in the Reader/Writer interface means a virtual call only on flush, even without de-virtualisation. (A/strong, verified)
- [[liptak-fuzz-allocators#^f1]] [zig/perf] Slow fuzz inputs came from trusting length fields in the input and allocating huge buffers. (B/strong, verified)
- [[liptak-fuzz-allocators#^f3]] [zig/perf] After the fixes the fuzzer ran consistently above 6000 inputs/sec with no timeouts. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-benchmark-ms#^f1]] [zig/perf] Tune benchmark input until it takes about 300ms. (B/fair, verified)
- [[matklad-benchmark-ms#^f2]] [zig/perf] Below ~10ms fixed costs skew results; above a second, iterating is slow. (B/fair, verified)
- [[matklad-benchmark-ms#^f3]] [zig/perf] Purpose: intuition to make a correct decision, not precise measurement. (B/fair, verified)
- [[matklad-comptime-orm#^f8]] [zig/perf] Batch API (`insert(table, values: []const Value)`) is O(N + K log K) vs O(N K) for K scalar inserts. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-comptime-wont-do#^f7]] [zig/comptime] [zig/perf] Types exist only at comptime; one copy of print per type costs code size and compile time, so roll your own RTTI: compute it at comptime, store it as data, use one `print_dyn(rtti, *const anyopaque)`. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-defer-patterns#^f2]] [zig/memory] [zig/perf] But defer discourages RAII-style per-object resources and pushes towards batching: many objects share one pool, e.g. all strings in one buffer. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-defer-patterns#^f8]] [zig/perf] Buffer sized at comptime from the format: `var buf: [fmt.count("{}\n", .{maxInt(u16)})]u8 = undefined;`. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-do-not-optimize-away#^f1]] [zig/perf] Two benchmark pitfalls: an unused result is optimised away, and compile-time known parameters get constant folded. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-do-not-optimize-away#^f2]] [zig/perf] He distrusts `mem.doNotOptimizeAway` and `hint::black_box`: their meaning can't be explained as semantics-preserving transformation. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-do-not-optimize-away#^f3]] [zig/perf] [zig/test] Scaffold: `parameter("element_count", 1_000_000)` reads an env var with a default and prints it; inputs can't be folded and reruns need no recompile. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-do-not-optimize-away#^f4]] [zig/perf] [zig/test] Output: accumulate `hash +%= insertion_point(elements, key)` and print it with the timing; a wrong optimisation shows as a changed hash. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-do-not-optimize-away#^f5]] [zig/perf] Rule: runtime-overridable inputs with compile-time defaults, and print the result or its hash. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-error-abi#^f1]] [zig/perf] [zig/errors] Large error types inflate the result size and push functions across the call stack to return through memory; one large rare error worsens code everywhere. (B/fair, verified)
- [[matklad-error-abi#^f2]] [zig/perf] Mature Rust error libraries (failure, anyhow) hide errors behind a thin pointer, which needs a global allocator. (B/fair, verified)
- [[matklad-error-abi#^f3]] [zig/perf] A better ABI reserves one register for a register-sized error. (B/fair, verified)
- [[matklad-invariant#^f5]] [zig/perf] Transaction logic can't read from disk: objects are prefetched per batch, so IO parallelises and execution is a tight serial CPU loop without locks. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-mechanical-habits#^f6]] [zig/perf] [zig/test] Micro benchmarks bitrot; make each one a test (`test "benchmark: binary search"`), chaining onto an existing entry point. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-mechanical-habits#^f7]] [zig/perf] [zig/test] Two modes by CLI shape: full suite runs benchmarks small and silent; `zig build test -- "benchmark: search"` runs big and prints. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-mechanical-habits#^f8]] [zig/perf] `bench.parameter("element_count", 1_000, 10_000_000)`: small for test, big for benchmark, named so it prints and can be overridden by env var. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-newtype-index#^f1]] [zig/perf] In efficiency-minded code indexes are idiomatic over pointers; a 32-bit index saves four bytes per pointer on 64-bit. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-newtype-index#^f2]] [zig/perf] (claim) Memory savings spread evenly and don't show in a profiler, so he defaults to indexes where speed matters before any profiling. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-newtype-index#^f3]] [zig/perf] A list can often be a range into shared storage instead of a list of indexes. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-newtype-index#^f4]] [zig/perf] Pointers encourage recursion over long chains, which overflows the stack in production; indexes model cycles and recursion more naturally. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-newtype-index#^f5]] [zig/perf] Indexes are relocatable, so arrays can be bulk serialized with memcpy; caveats: padding can leak data, checksum the result. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-newtype-index#^f9]] [zig/safety] [zig/perf] Sentinel `invalid = std.math.maxInt(u32)` for "no parent" instead of `?Node` (which wastes space), as offensive programming so bad indexes blow up. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-newtype-index#^f10]] [zig/perf] [zig/safety] `comptime { assert(@sizeOf(Data) == 12); }` inside the struct, as a comment telling the reader the size, not to prevent changes. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-push-ifs-up#^f4]] [zig/perf] Push fors down: make batch operations the base case, the scalar version a special case. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-push-ifs-up#^f5]] [zig/perf] Batches amortise startup cost and free the processing order (struct-of-arrays tricks, one field at a time). (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-push-ifs-up#^f6]] [zig/perf] Composed: branch outside, loop inside; avoids re-evaluating the condition, removes a branch from the hot loop, may unlock vectorization. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-push-ifs-up#^f7]] [zig/perf] TigerBeetle's architecture: the data plane works on batches to amortise control-plane decisions. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-scalar-select#^f1]] [zig/perf] A one-event-at-a-time loop picks an arbitrary ready event although several are usually waiting after processing the last. (B/fair, verified)
- [[matklad-scalar-select#^f2]] [zig/perf] With a batch you can prioritise (writes over reads over accepts), exert selective backpressure, eliminate superseded events and coalesce. (B/fair, verified)
- [[matklad-scalar-select#^f3]] [zig/perf] Always be batching: `batch_stream` waits for one event, then takes all available non-blocking; under load batches grow and cost grows sublinearly. (B/fair, verified)
- [[matklad-static-alloc-compilers#^f8]] [zig/memory] [zig/perf] Idea: an output arena for O(N) finished results plus O(1) processing of bounded chunks (e.g. a 4 MiB file limit, runtime-overridable), with indexes for output data. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-static-alloc-constant-work#^f8]] [zig/perf] Iterate the full set doing no-ops for reserved: if max load isn't fast enough that is a bug; gray failure is another way to break at the limit. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-static-alloc-constant-work#^f9]] [zig/perf] A plain `for (orders)` is easier to vectorize and prefetch than an indirection through `orders_active` indexes. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-static-alloc-constant-work#^f10]] [zig/perf] Constant work keeps P100 latency flat regardless of load. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-value-interner#^f1]] [zig/perf] Theoretical, no benchmarks, never implemented. (C/fair, verified)
- [[matklad-value-interner#^f3]] [zig/perf] Struct-of-arrays value table: a value is a u32 index into `tag` and `data` arrays; large payloads live in per-kind side arrays. (C/fair, verified)
- [[matklad-value-interner#^f5]] [zig/perf] Reduce contention by sharding (16 mutexes and maps by hash bits) and by threads reserving index blocks with one atomic add, which also improves locality. (C/fair, verified)
- [[matklad-zig-and-rust#^f3]] [zig/perf] No serialisation format: received bytes are cast to the desired type and checksummed, to cut moving parts more than for speed. (B/strong, verified)
- [[mitchellh-everyone-should-know-simd#^f1]] [zig/perf] Everyday SIMD follows one shape: broadcast constants, loop one vector at a time, operate on all lanes, reduce, scalar tail. (B/strong, verified)
- [[mitchellh-everyone-should-know-simd#^f2]] [zig/perf] It pays only on large data: hundreds of values or more, not dozens. (B/strong, verified)
- [[mitchellh-everyone-should-know-simd#^f3]] [zig/perf] The scalar loop stays as both the tail and the fallback when the target has no usable vector width (`simd.lanes` returns null). (B/strong, verified)
- [[mitchellh-everyone-should-know-simd#^f4]] [zig/perf] Generic `@Vector` code has no CPU intrinsics; Zig lowers it to the target's instruction set. (B/strong, verified)
- [[mitchellh-everyone-should-know-simd#^f5]] [zig/perf] First-mismatch idiom: `@reduce(.And, cmp)` for the all-pass fast path, else `@bitCast` the bool vector to an int mask and `@ctz(~mask)`. (B/strong, verified)
- [[mitchellh-everyone-should-know-simd#^f6]] [zig/perf] Measured: up to 4x NEON, 8x AVX2, 16x AVX-512 in the loop; about 5x end to end on an AVX2 desktop. (B/strong, verified)
- [[mitchellh-everyone-should-know-simd#^f7]] [zig/perf] Compile the scalar version with optimisations and look before hand-writing SIMD; compilers sometimes vectorise. (B/strong, verified)
- [[mitchellh-everyone-should-know-simd#^f8]] [zig/perf] When the loop matters he wants vectorisation explicit so a code change or compiler update can't silently undo it. (B/strong, verified)
- [[mitchellh-everyone-should-know-simd#^f9]] [zig/perf] If the SIMD version isn't simple, that is a sign to skip it for now. (B/strong, verified)
- [[mitchellh-ghostty-and-useful-zig-patterns#^f11]] [zig/perf] [zig/test] FontIndex derives its index bit width from the style bits; tests pin the size so widening is a conscious act. (B/fair, verified)
- [[mitchellh-ghostty-devlog-004#^f2]] [zig/comptime] [zig/perf] The runtime path is a comptime lookup table: no allocation or formatting, a stable pointer written to the pty. (B/fair, verified)
- [[mitchellh-ghostty-devlog-006#^f1]] [zig/perf] Compilers rarely auto-vectorise beyond trivial loops, so SIMD is one place hand-written code still wins. (B/strong, verified)
- [[mitchellh-ghostty-devlog-006#^f2]] [zig/perf] Shipping binaries means one SIMD variant per instruction set and picking at runtime by CPU feature detection. (B/strong, verified)
- [[mitchellh-ghostty-devlog-006#^f3]] [zig/perf] Benchmarks include a memcpy (just read) baseline, so "as fast as reading" is visible. (B/strong, verified)
- [[mitchellh-ghostty-devlog-006#^f4]] [zig/perf] Microbenchmark gains shrink end to end because other bottlenecks remain (7.3x became 2x on `cat`). (B/strong, verified)
- [[mitchellh-ghostty-devlog-006#^f5]] [zig/perf] Profiling found 30% of per-character time in codepoint width, which set the next target. (B/strong, verified)
- [[mitchellh-ghostty-devlog-006#^f6]] [zig/perf] Precompute the answer for every codepoint in a 3-stage table: compact and cache friendly. (B/strong, verified)
- [[mitchellh-ghostty-devlog-006#^f7]] [zig/perf] Domain preconditions (no control chars, max width 2) remove branches from the table lookup. (B/strong, verified)
- [[mitchellh-ghostty-devlog-006#^f8]] [zig/perf] With enough preconditions, grapheme-break state for every codepoint pair fits in under 1KB. (B/strong, verified)
- [[mitchellh-ghostty-devlog-006#^f9]] [zig/perf] The CSI fast path skips the state machine and assumes valid syntax, measured on a replayed real pty capture (1.4x to 2x). (B/strong, verified)
- [[mitchellh-ghostty-devlog-006#^f10]] [zig/perf] Measurement hygiene: 10 runs averaged, range within 2%. (B/strong, verified)
- [[mitchellh-ghostty-memory-leak-fix#^f3]] [zig/memory] [zig/perf] Hot-path optimisation: at the scrollback limit the oldest page is reused as the newest, with zero allocations. (B/strong, verified)
- [[mitchellh-tripwire#^f7]] [zig/comptime] [zig/perf] Zero cost outside tests via `builtin.is_test` and an inline calling convention; an empty non-inline function still emitted a call in real builds. (B/strong, verified)
- [[mitchellh-zig-comptime-tagged-union-subset#^f12]] [zig/perf] The conversion has a runtime switch; profiled in a synthetic loop, it didn't show up. (B/strong, verified)
- [[river-repo#^f4]] [zig/perf] [zig/style] Simplicity over nearly everything; performance justifies complexity only with profiling results showing a significant benefit. `CONTRIBUTING.md` (A/strong, verified)
- [[roc-zig-compiler#^f11]] [zig/perf] [zig/memory] Dense ids: identities are dense store-local integer `...Id`s with per-id data as parallel columns on the owning store, not hash maps; a lint rejects a HashMap keyed by an `...Id` type. `design.md` (B/strong, verified)
- [[roc-zig-compiler#^f12]] [zig/perf] Why dense columns: they avoid hashing, table growth, repeated key storage, allocator traffic and duplicate per-consumer indexes. `design.md` (B/strong, verified)
- [[roc-zig-compiler#^f14]] [zig/perf] [zig/api] Typed index ranges instead of slices: `SafeRange(Idx)` is a start index plus count, stable across reallocation and serialisation where a slice would dangle. `src/collections/safe_list.zig` (B/strong, verified)
- [[roc-zig-compiler#^f15]] [zig/safety] [zig/perf] No recursion over input-shaped data in any stage: walks keep pending work on heap-backed frame stacks or worklists; a bigger thread stack or depth limit is not an accepted fix; tests run deep shapes on a tiny-stack thread. `design.md` (B/strong, verified)
- [[seguin-arena-arraylist#^f4]] [zig/perf] Mitigations: size the list up front (`initCapacity`, `ensureTotalCapacityPrecise`) and avoid interleaving other allocations with appends, so remap succeeds; in an arena the worst case is about 3x the memory. (B/fair, verified)
- [[seguin-comptime-config#^f3]] [zig/perf] Comptime config allows real optimisation: pick `u8` vs `u16` and put the call stack on the stack. (B/fair, verified)
- [[seguin-getorput-string-keys#^f1]] [zig/perf] `getOrPut` does one hash lookup for insert-or-update, versus two for get then put. (B/fair, verified)
- [[seguin-learning-zig-pointers#^f2]] [zig/perf] Plain struct layout is unspecified; `packed` or `extern` give guarantees. (B/fair, verified)
- [[seguin-leveraging-allocators#^f3]] [zig/perf] Reuse one arena across iterations with `reset(.{.retain_with_limit = 8192})` to avoid going back to the parent allocator. (B/fair, verified)
- [[seguin-leveraging-allocators#^f4]] [zig/perf] `StackFallbackAllocator` needs a comptime size; for a configurable size he writes a FixedBuffer-then-arena allocator. (B/fair, verified)
- [[seguin-linkedlist-fieldparentptr#^f1]] [zig/perf] std `SinglyLinkedList`/`DoublyLinkedList` became intrusive: embed a `Node` in your struct; no separate node allocation. (B/fair, verified)
- [[tigerbeetle-architecture#^f3]] [zig/perf] Single threaded by design: the workload is contended (hot accounts), so parallel transactions don't help; one core reaches 1M TPS with efficient use, and testing gets simpler. (A/strong, verified)
- [[tigerbeetle-architecture#^f9]] [zig/perf] [zig/safety] Control plane vs data plane: keep control `if`s out of data `for`s; the cheap control plane can afford O(N) assertions for O(1) work. (A/strong, verified)
- [[tigerbeetle-architecture#^f10]] [zig/perf] The `commit` function is synchronous and does no IO; all IO happens in a separate prefetch phase that can run in parallel. (A/strong, verified)
- [[tigerbeetle-blog-copy-hunting#^f1]] [zig/perf] Idiomatic Zig can compile to implicit copies of large values; by-value loop captures are a known case, fixed with `/*item/`. (B/strong, verified)
- [[tigerbeetle-blog-copy-hunting#^f2]] [zig/perf] `zig build-lib -femit-llvm-ir` gives greppable text IR; copyhound scans it for `memcpy` above a size threshold. (B/strong, verified)
- [[tigerbeetle-blog-copy-hunting#^f3]] [zig/perf] [zig/comptime] `std.meta.eql` on plain byte structs caused copies; replaced by `equal_bytes`, which asserts at comptime that bytewise comparison is valid. (B/strong, verified)
- [[tigerbeetle-blog-copy-hunting#^f5]] [zig/perf] A rarely used slow path inlined into a hot function can blow up its stack frame; mark it cold. (B/strong, verified)
- [[tigerbeetle-blog-enum-of-arrays#^f1]] [zig/perf] Struct of arrays: amortised padding and less memory traffic when a loop needs only some fields; use SIMD across items, not within one. (B/fair, verified)
- [[tigerbeetle-blog-enum-of-arrays#^f2]] [zig/perf] Enum of arrays: one tag for a whole batch of one variant, saving tag bytes, variant padding and per-item branches. (B/fair, verified)
- [[tigerbeetle-blog-enum-of-arrays#^f3]] [zig/perf] TigerBeetle batches are homogeneous (all transfers or all accounts); the tag is control plane, the arrays data plane. (B/fair, verified)
- [[tigerbeetle-code#^f18]] [zig/perf] Hot loops are standalone functions with slices in and counts out, no `self`, with a comment saying why; results are structs checked by postcondition asserts. `lsm/compaction.zig` (A/strong, verified)
- [[tigerbeetle-tiger-style#^f24]] [zig/perf] Performance is won in design via back-of-the-envelope sketches over network, disk, memory, CPU and bandwidth, latency; optimise slowest resource first after weighting by frequency. (A/strong, verified)
- [[tigerbeetle-tiger-style#^f25]] [zig/perf] Separate control plane from data plane; batching lets the control plane carry heavy assertions without slowing the data plane. (A/strong, verified)
- [[tigerbeetle-tiger-style#^f26]] [zig/perf] Extract hot loops into standalone functions with primitive arguments and no `self`, so the compiler need not prove fields can live in registers. (A/strong, verified)
- [[tigerbeetle-tiger-style#^f38]] [zig/api] [zig/perf] Pass arguments over 16 bytes as `*const` when a copy isn't meant, to catch accidental stack copies. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-011-release-notes#^f6]] [zig/perf] `s[start..][0..len]` is the idiomatic slice-by-length; with a comptime len it yields an array pointer, and the compiler recognises the pattern. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-011-release-notes#^f13]] [zig/perf] Sorting split into stable (blocksort) and unstable (pdqsort); unstable is preferred when possible; debug builds assert the comparator is consistent. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-013-release-notes#^f4]] [zig/perf] New std.Progress: thread-safe, lock-free, infallible Node.start/end over a statically allocated buffer, bounded by terminal size. (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-014-release-notes#^f1]] [zig/perf] Labelled switch: `continue :sw value` re-dispatches a switch, equivalent to a switch in a loop, clearer for state machines. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-014-release-notes#^f2]] [zig/perf] Its main motive is codegen: one branch per continue helps the branch predictor in hot dispatch loops; the tokenizer got 13% faster. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-014-release-notes#^f9]] [zig/perf] @branchHint(.likely/.unlikely/.cold/.unpredictable) as first statement of a block or function replaces @setCold. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-014-release-notes#^f24]] [zig/perf] Unordered hash maps slow down after many removals (rehash added as a stopgap); array hash maps don't. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-015-release-notes#^f14]] [zig/perf] Buffer in the interface is more optimiser friendly, especially in Debug; vectors, splat and fd-to-fd send propagate through reader/writer graphs. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-015-release-notes#^f17]] [zig/perf] CountingWriter deleted: use Writer.Discarding, Allocating or fixed; don't add a stream node just to count, it disrupts buffering. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-015-release-notes#^f25]] [zig/perf] Copyable growable arrays waste time copying undefined memory and bloat generic code. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-015-release-notes#^f26]] [zig/perf] SeekableStream, BitReader and BitWriter deleted: use concrete types; bit reading tied to the stream keeps hot loops optimisable. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-016-release-notes#^f9]] [zig/perf] Group: many tasks with one lifetime, O(1) overhead to spawn N; `defer group.cancel(io)` then `try group.await(io)`. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-016-release-notes#^f10]] [zig/perf] Batch works on operations, not functions: efficient and portable, harder to abstract; for optimal reusable code prefer Batch, else Futures, and optimise later. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-016-release-notes#^f12]] [zig/perf] std.Thread.Pool removed for Io.async / Io.Group.async; converting needs every Thread.Mutex/Condition/ResetEvent turned into its Io type. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-016-release-notes#^f30]] [zig/perf] Runtime vector indexes forbidden: coerce the vector to an array and loop. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-016-release-notes#^f40]] [zig/perf] Looping over slices emits about 30% less safety-check code. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-compiler-dod#^f1]] [zig/perf] The AST is three flat arrays: tokens and nodes as MultiArrayList slices and a `[]u32` extra_data; nodes refer to each other by index, not pointer. `std/zig/Ast.zig:17` (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-compiler-dod#^f2]] [zig/perf] Offsets are u32 (`ByteOffset = u32`), halving index size on 64-bit. `std/zig/Ast.zig:26` (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-compiler-dod#^f4]] [zig/perf] Optional indices use a sentinel `none = maxInt(u32)` value instead of `?u32`, keeping them 4 bytes, with `unwrap()` to a real optional at use. `std/zig/Ast.zig:2936` (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-compiler-dod#^f5]] [zig/perf] A node is a 1-byte tag, a main token and an 8-byte untagged data union; the sizes are guarded by a comptime assert (only outside safety builds, where untagged unions get a hidden tag). `std/zig/Ast.zig:2978` (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-compiler-dod#^f7]] [zig/perf] Variable-size payloads go in `extra_data` as u32 words, decoded by a comptime `extraData(index, T)` that walks T's fields with inline for. `std/zig/Ast.zig:305` (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-compiler-dod#^f8]] [zig/perf] `parse` pre-sizes the token list from a measured ratio, with the number stated in the comment. `std/zig/Ast.zig:153` (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-compiler-dod#^f10]] [zig/perf] ZIR is designed so later stages never touch the AST again except for errors: one representation per phase, each self-contained. `std/zig/Zir.zig:5` (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-compiler-dod#^f11]] [zig/perf] Strings are interned into one `string_bytes` buffer and referenced by u32 index; index 0 reserved. `std/zig/Zir.zig:26` (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-compiler-dod#^f12]] [zig/perf] Instructions are tag (u8) + 8-byte Data in a MultiArrayList; the layout is guarded by a comptime size assert with a comment saying why. `std/zig/Zir.zig:2515` (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-compiler-dod#^f13]] [zig/perf] One enum(u32) `Ref` spans two spaces: values below a static length are well-known InternPool entries (u8_type...), the rest instruction indices. `std/zig/Zir.zig:2225` (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-compiler-dod#^f15]] [zig/perf] Flags are packed into `packed struct(u16)` with explicit backing size. `std/zig/Zir.zig:2581` (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-compiler-dod#^f16]] [zig/perf] InternPool splits API and storage: `Key` is a rich tagged union for callers, `Item` is a u8 tag plus u32 data in storage; `get` encodes a Key, `indexToKey` decodes. `src/InternPool.zig:3932` (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-compiler-dod#^f17]] [zig/perf] Interning makes equality cheap: two same-typed values are equal iff their indices are equal. `src/InternPool.zig:3939` (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-compiler-dod#^f18]] [zig/perf] Per-thread `Local` state, each part cache-line aligned, with a per-thread arena to avoid allocator contention and keep old pointers valid for racing readers. `src/InternPool.zig:960` (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-devlog-2024#^f5]] [zig/perf] Labeled switch with `continue :state` for state machines: rewriting the tokenizer measured a 13% wall-time gain for `zig ast-check`. (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-devlog-2025#^f9]] [zig/memory] [zig/perf] The DebugAllocator rework cut a debug ast-check run from 22.8 s to 20.5 s (10.1%) and peak RSS 6.2%. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-devlog-2025#^f11]] [zig/memory] [zig/perf] SmpAllocator vs glibc malloc on one ast-check: 4.9% faster wall time, 12.1% less RSS; whole compiler self-build: no wall-time difference. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-devlog-2025#^f13]] [zig/build] [zig/perf] Self-hosted x86_64 backend default for Debug on Linux/macOS from 0.15: hello world 918 ms to 275 ms (70%), compiler 75 s to 20 s. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-devlog-2026#^f2]] [zig/build] [zig/perf] Measured: `zig build --help` 150 ms to 14.3 ms (90.4%) because the cached configuration skips rerunning build.zig. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-devlog-2026#^f21]] [zig/perf] [toolchain] LLVM now stores odd-width integers (u4, u40) as ABI-sized in memory; the compiler itself got about 5% faster from restored optimisations. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-a#^f38]] [zig/perf] `threadlocal var` gives each thread its own copy; it may not be const and is a plain global in single-threaded builds. Thread Local Variables (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-a#^f42]] [zig/perf] Arbitrary bit-width integers (`u3`, `i7`) up to 65535 bits; signed are two's complement. Integers (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-a#^f51]] [zig/perf] Floats default to strict IEEE mode; `@setFloatMode(.optimized)` relaxes it per block (the example changes the result). Floating Point Operations (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-a#^f53]] [zig/perf] `@Vector` maps to SIMD where possible; small power-of-two lengths are typical; very long vectors can crash the compiler. Vectors (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-a#^f54]] [zig/perf] Load a vector from a runtime offset by slicing to a comptime length and dereferencing: `slice[offset..][0..N].*`. Vectors (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-a#^f56]] [zig/perf] The doc has an open TODO to recommend `std.MultiArrayList` (struct of arrays) in the vectors section. Vectors (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-a#^f65]] [zig/perf] Slice by length as `s[a..][0..n]`, never `s[a .. a + n]`: one subtraction fewer, and with comptime `n` the result is a `*[n]T` with a compile-time length. Slicing by Length (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-a#^f71]] [zig/perf] Plain structs give no field order or size guarantees, so the compiler may reorder; use `extern` only when C layout is needed. struct, extern struct (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-a#^f77]] [zig/perf] `packed struct` = a backing integer with fields laid out from the least significant bit; same ABI as the integer; suits flags. packed struct (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-a#^f84]] [zig/perf] With a result type, an anonymous literal `.{...}` builds the value directly in its result location, with no copy. Anonymous Struct Literals (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-a#^f89]] [zig/perf] An enum without a tag type gets the smallest one that fits (`u2` for four members). enum (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-a#^f105]] [zig/perf] Labelled switch with `continue :sw value` is meant for state machines and dispatch loops: comptime targets become direct jumps, runtime ones a branch per prong, which predicts better than one loop dispatch point. Labeled switch (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-a#^f112]] [zig/perf] [zig/comptime] Use `inline` loops only when comptime semantics need them or a benchmark shows unrolling is faster. inline while, inline for (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-a#^f121]] [zig/perf] Parameters are immutable, so Zig may pass structs by copy or by reference as it judges faster; don't take a parameter's address expecting it to outlive the call. Pass-by-value Parameters (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-a#^f122]] [zig/perf] `inline fn` is not a hint: it forces semantic inlining, restricts the compiler and can cost binary size, compile time and speed; for a hint use `@branchHint`. Inline Is Not A Hint (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-a#^f124]] [zig/perf] `@branchHint(.cold)` marks a rarely called function. Functions (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-b#^f12]] [zig/errors] [zig/perf] Trace cost: one memory write on the success path, a couple of math ops per error return. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-b#^f15]] [zig/errors] [zig/perf] The error name table is only emitted if @errorName is called with a runtime value. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-b#^f19]] [zig/safety] [zig/perf] `?*T` is the size of `*T` (null is address 0), so optional pointers cost nothing; plain pointers can never be null. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-b#^f33]] [zig/perf] [zig/api] `void` as a generic argument deletes the field and its code: `AutoHashMap(K, void)` is a set with no value storage. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-b#^f36]] [zig/api] [zig/perf] Anonymous initialisers write fields straight into the destination, which avoids copies and matters for pinned types. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-b#^f41]] [zig/comptime] [zig/perf] comptime is about guarantees, not speed. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-b#^f44]] [zig/comptime] [zig/perf] Container-level expressions are implicitly comptime: lookup tables can be computed by ordinary functions at declaration. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-b#^f58]] [zig/perf] In a retry loop use @cmpxchgWeak; @cmpxchgStrong only when you need the stronger guarantee. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-b#^f60]] [zig/safety] [zig/perf] @setRuntimeSafety overrides the build mode per scope, both ways; planned to become @optimizeFor. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-b#^f61]] [zig/perf] @setFloatMode(.optimized) is -ffast-math for a scope; strict IEEE is default. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-b#^f62]] [zig/perf] @call modifiers force or forbid inlining and tail calls, failing to compile if impossible; @branchHint must be the first statement of a branch or function. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-b#^f65]] [zig/perf] -fsingle-threaded makes threadlocals plain globals and std.Mutex a no-op. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-b#^f77]] [zig/memory] [zig/perf] Allocator choice 5: cyclical work (frame, request) uses an arena per cycle, or a FixedBufferAllocator if the cycle has a memory bound. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-b#^f111]] [zig/perf] Checked, not there: the Atomics chapter is only TODOs and see-also links. (A/strong, absence)
- [[zig-learn-overview#^f3]] [zig/safety] [zig/perf] Recommended stance: run a safety-enabled build and disable safety only at measured bottlenecks. (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-learn-overview#^f4]] [zig/safety] [zig/perf] Illegal behaviour serves both bug prevention and optimisation; unsigned overflow is illegal too, unlike C, which enables optimisations. (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-learn-overview#^f5]] [zig/perf] (claim) Zig is faster than C: one compilation unit, chosen illegal behaviour, SIMD vectors, std data structures instead of linked lists. (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-repo-docs#^f28]] [zig/build] [zig/perf] Memory budgets as build facts: `max_rss` on the compiler step, "should never be raised". `build.zig:907` (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-std-allocators#^f13]] [zig/perf] `reset(.retain_capacity)` keeps one big buffer for loops, so after the largest iteration no more backing allocations happen. `std/heap/ArenaAllocator.zig:89` (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-std-allocators#^f20]] [zig/perf] Per-thread state is padded to a cache line with a zero-size aligned field to avoid false sharing. `std/heap/SafeAllocator.zig:95` (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-std-containers#^f11]] [zig/perf] `ensureTotalCapacityPrecise` tries `remap` in place before alloc+copy, and the comment says why: realloc would copy unused capacity. `std/array_list.zig:1362` (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-std-containers#^f12]] [zig/perf] Growth is 1.5x plus a cache-line's worth of elements, saturating. `std/array_list.zig:1550` (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-std-containers#^f15]] [zig/perf] `appendNTimes` is `inline` for a stated codegen reason (comptime value gives a better memset), not by habit. `std/array_list.zig:1199` (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-std-containers#^f20]] [zig/perf] Adapted contexts let a map of u32 offsets into one byte buffer be looked up by a `[]const u8`: the string-interning pattern. `std/hash_map.zig:93` (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-std-containers#^f23]] [zig/perf] HashMapUnmanaged is one allocation (header, metadata, entries) with 7-bit fingerprints; comments give the cache-miss reasoning. `std/hash_map.zig:520` (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-std-containers#^f24]] [zig/perf] `@branchHint(.cold)` marks the empty-map early return and `grow`, with a comment on why cold rather than unlikely. `std/hash_map.zig:970` (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-std-containers#^f26]] [zig/perf] MultiArrayList stores each struct field (or union tag and payload) in its own array to cut padding and touch only the fields a loop needs; call `slice()` once and `items(.field)` per field. `std/multi_array_list.zig:9` (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-std-io#^f9]] [zig/perf] The buffered fast path is a plain memcpy marked `@branchHint(.likely)`, inlined at the caller with no indirect call. `std/Io/Writer.zig:608` (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-talk-kelley-dod#^f1]] [zig/perf] The CPU is fast and main memory slow; math beats a memory load, so memoising cheap computations can make code slower. @6:11 (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-talk-kelley-dod#^f2]] [zig/perf] The practical method: find the struct you have the most of in memory and make it smaller. @9:08 (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-talk-kelley-dod#^f3]] [zig/perf] Field order changes padding: u32,u64,u32 is 24 bytes, u32,u32,u64 is 16; one added bool pushes it back to 24. @11:14 (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-talk-kelley-dod#^f4]] [zig/perf] [zig/memory] Use u32 indexes into arrays instead of pointers: halves the field on 64-bit and drops alignment from 8 to 4. @13:48 (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-talk-kelley-dod#^f6]] [zig/perf] Store booleans out of band: two arrays (alive, dead) instead of a flag field; 24 to 12 bytes per monster with indexes, and the loop skips the branch and load. @16:49 (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-talk-kelley-dod#^f7]] [zig/perf] Eliminate padding with struct-of-arrays (MultiArrayList, "a five character fix, same API"): 10,000 monsters 160 KB to 91 KB. @20:26 (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-talk-kelley-dod#^f8]] [zig/perf] Store sparse data in hashmaps keyed by index: with 90% of monsters holding nothing, 366 KB to 198 KB. @21:56 (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-talk-kelley-dod#^f9]] [zig/perf] Encodings instead of polymorphism: a tag that folds flags and variants (bee colour, clothed or not) plus common data and an `extra` index into side arrays; 32 to 17 bytes per monster. @26:24 (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-talk-kelley-dod#^f10]] [zig/perf] Choose encodings from the observed distribution of your data. @29:24 (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-talk-kelley-dod#^f11]] [zig/perf] Zig tokens went from 64 bytes to 5: compute line and column lazily, u32 offsets (4 GB source limit), no end position, no parsed literals. @34:23 (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-talk-kelley-dod#^f12]] [zig/perf] AST nodes 120 bytes to 15.6 average with SoA and multiple encodings per node kind; with tokens, parsing got 22% faster in wall time. @36:33 (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-talk-kelley-dod#^f13]] [zig/perf] ZIR instructions 54 to 20.3 bytes average (u32 source locations, encodings, references that encode simple values like true/false); 39% less wall time. @40:53 (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-talk-kelley-dod#^f14]] [zig/perf] Flat arrays (tag, data, extra, string table) make serialising trivial: one writev/readv syscall to save or load the cache. @43:50 (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-talk-kelley-dod#^f15]] [zig/perf] Summary list: indexes instead of pointers (watch type safety), out-of-band bools, SoA, sparse hashmaps, encodings instead of polymorphism. @45:20 (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-talk-kelley-dod#^f16]] [zig/perf] (claim) Commenter critique: hash access is much slower than pointer access, so out-of-band data in hashmaps trades footprint for lookup cost. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-talk-kelley-flush#^f8]] [zig/perf] Buffering matters about a thousand-fold: 14 one-byte write syscalls vs one buffered write. @20:06 (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-talk-kelley-flush#^f9]] [zig/api] [zig/perf] Keep hot paths above the vtable: fields and methods of the interface struct can be inlined; vtable function pointers are opaque to the optimiser. @21:42 (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-talk-kelley-flush#^f10]] [zig/api] [zig/perf] Putting the buffer in the interface (not the implementation) turned per-byte indirect calls into a register op plus a store, while the same machine code still serves any sink. @24:07 (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-talk-roadmap-2026#^f1]] [zig/perf] [zig/style] Labeled switch with `continue :label value` for state machines: a comptime-known continue is a structured goto; branching moves to each prong, which helps the branch predictor (tokenizer 13% faster). @22:54 (A/strong, verified)
- [[ziggit-choosing-allocator#^f2]] [zig/perf] GeneralPurposeAllocator was renamed DebugAllocator because it is slow; `smp_allocator` is the fast general allocator, reportedly faster than libc's. (B/fair, verified)
- [[ziggit-code-smells#^f3]] [zig/perf] Counterpoint: vtable interfaces cost an indirection; `anytype` generates direct calls. (C/fair, verified)
- [[ziggit-invalidate-pointers#^f2]] [zig/perf] An optional costs alignment-sized space today; an invalid state of the type costs nothing. (C/fair, verified)
- [[zigguide-allocators#^f1]] [zig/perf] `page_allocator` makes a syscall per allocation and reserves whole pages: slow and wasteful for small allocations. (B/fair, verified)
- [[zigguide-allocators#^f4]] [zig/perf] `SmpAllocator` is the high-performance general allocator, with few safety features. (B/fair, verified)
- [[zls-repo#^f27]] [zig/perf] [zig/api] Typed indices: `enum(u32) { _ }` for handles into arrays, with a separate OptionalIndex using maxInt as `none` instead of `?u32` (no extra tag byte). 0.17 converts with `@fromBackingInt`/`@backingInt`. `src/DocumentScope.zig` (A/fair, verified)
- [[zls-repo#^f28]] [zig/perf] InternPool (copied from the compiler) stores items in a MultiArrayList plus a `u32` extra array and flags as `packed struct(u32)`. `src/analyser/InternPool.zig` (A/fair, verified)
- [[zls-repo#^f29]] [zig/perf] Profiling: tracy zones via `tracy.trace(@src())` + `defer zone.end()` in ~96 functions; when disabled `trace` is an inline fn returning an empty struct, so it costs nothing. `src/tracy.zig`, `src/Server.zig` (A/fair, verified)

### zig/safety: assertions, undefined behaviour, build modes, safety checks, invariants (209)

- [[ak-bun-rust-rewrite#^f2]] [zig/safety] Practices he calls bad Zig: hacks on hacks, abuse of assertions, speeding through features without time to eliminate bugs and debt. (B/fair, verified)
- [[ak-bun-rust-rewrite#^f3]] [zig/safety] [zig/test] Bugs are eliminated by dedicating engineering time, not by a style guide or language feature alone; he credits TigerBeetle for doing that work. (B/fair, verified)
- [[ak-openzfs-bug-zig#^f1]] [zig/style] [zig/safety] `zig fmt --ast-check` (no type checking) rejects redeclaring a local const, which exposed dead initialisation code in the C original. (A/fair, verified)
- [[ak-openzfs-bug-zig#^f2]] [zig/style] [zig/safety] An unused local constant is a compile error, which caught the real bug (the function returned its input, not the computed psize). (A/fair, verified)
- [[bun-in-rust#^f6]] [zig/safety] What they already did in Zig: a compiler patch for AddressSanitizer with the test suite under ASAN on every commit, ReleaseSafe builds on Windows, 24/7 fuzzing, end-to-end leak tests. (B/strong, verified)
- [[bun-in-rust#^f7]] [zig/safety] Shipping ReleaseFast on macOS and Linux removed bounds checks; an off-by-one from the Zig code panicked in Rust, and would have in Zig under ReleaseSafe. (B/strong, verified)
- [[bun-in-rust#^f8]] [zig/safety] Zig's `assert` is a plain function, so its argument (side effects included) runs in every build. (B/strong, verified)
- [[bun-repo#^f10]] [zig/memory] [zig/safety] CheckedAllocator: in CI-assert builds, unmanaged containers record the allocator first used and assert every later call passes the same one. `src/safety/alloc.zig` (A/fair, verified)
- [[bun-repo#^f11]] [zig/safety] ThreadLock: a debug lock asserting that one thread owns a structure, enabled only in CI-assert builds. `src/safety/ThreadLock.zig` (A/fair, verified)
- [[bun-repo#^f12]] [zig/safety] Own `bun.assert`: returns early when asserts are off, `unreachable` in Debug, otherwise a reported assertion failure; plus `assertf` with a message. Unlike std.debug.assert it is not an optimiser hint in release. `src/bun.zig` (A/fair, verified)
- [[bun-repo#^f15]] [zig/safety] AddressSanitizer on by default in debug builds on Linux and macOS, Zig code included (needed a compiler patch, see [[bun-in-rust]]). `CONTRIBUTING.md` (A/fair, verified)
- [[ghostty-repo#^f25]] [zig/safety] Ghostty replaces std.debug.assert with an inline assert in release builds: on Zig 0.15.2 the call was not always optimised away and cost 15-20% in hot loops; Debug keeps std's for stack traces. `src/quirks.zig:32-57` (A/strong, verified)
- [[ghostty-repo#^f26]] [zig/safety] Two tiers of checks: normal asserts, plus slow integrity checks only in Debug, because they made the terminal unusable. `src/build_config.zig:60-71` (A/strong, verified)
- [[ghostty-repo#^f27]] [zig/safety] Integrity checking is a fallible `verifyIntegrity` returning a named error set, and an `assertIntegrity` wrapper that logs and panics. `src/terminal/page.zig:336-382` (A/strong, verified)
- [[ghostty-repo#^f28]] [zig/safety] Debug-only fields use `if (cond) T else void`, so they cost nothing in release builds. `src/terminal/page.zig:231-232` (A/strong, verified)
- [[kristoff-comptime-sqlite#^f6]] [zig/safety] Debug-only checks via a field typed `if (builtin.mode == .Debug) T else void`. (B/strong, verified)
- [[kristoff-fix-your-asserts#^f1]] [zig/safety] Prefer the type system over an assert when it can express the constraint (e.g. `*T` vs `?*T`). (A/strong, verified)
- [[kristoff-fix-your-asserts#^f2]] [zig/safety] Asserts state pre- and postconditions and invariants, and with fuzzing catch more than unit tests. (A/strong, verified)
- [[kristoff-fix-your-asserts#^f3]] [zig/safety] `std.debug.assert` is `if (!ok) unreachable`; `unreachable` also works as an expression in impossible switch arms. (A/strong, verified)
- [[kristoff-fix-your-asserts#^f4]] [zig/safety] A tripped assert panics in Debug and ReleaseSafe; in ReleaseFast and ReleaseSmall it is unchecked illegal behaviour that the optimiser exploits. (A/strong, verified)
- [[kristoff-fix-your-asserts#^f7]] [zig/safety] `assert` is a function, not a macro: its argument is always evaluated, so side effects in it are fine. (A/strong, verified)
- [[kristoff-fix-your-asserts#^f9]] [zig/safety] Disabling asserts in production is in his view irredeemably bad: wrong asserts stay hidden and later code builds on them. (A/strong, verified)
- [[kristoff-fix-your-asserts#^f10]] [zig/safety] Valid choices: keep asserts on (ReleaseSafe) when misbehaviour is costly, or use ReleaseFast when speed matters more; even then, build critical C deps fast. (A/strong, verified)
- [[kristoff-fix-your-asserts#^f11]] [zig/safety] A variant he accepts: an assert that logs instead of crashing, where crashing is worse and a human reads the logs. (A/strong, verified)
- [[kristoff-fix-your-asserts#^f12]] [zig/safety] Language-level safety checks (overflow, bounds, division by zero) can't be disabled by the user's assert choices. (A/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-async-mutexes#^f4]] [zig/safety] Every continuation is an explicit named function that starts with assertions pinning the current state, or does exhaustive case analysis from scratch. (B/fair, verified)
- [[matklad-defer-patterns#^f3]] [zig/safety] Contracts: `assert(precondition); defer assert(postcondition);`, e.g. `assert(!grid.free_set.opened); defer assert(grid.free_set.opened);`. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-defer-patterns#^f4]] [zig/errors] [zig/safety] `errdefer comptime unreachable` statically proves no error path after that point; std hash_map grow: `try map.allocate(...)` then `errdefer comptime unreachable;` then swap and deinit. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-diagnostics-factory#^f9]] [zig/safety] [zig/comptime] `emit` takes `comptime fmt` and does `comptime assert(fmt[fmt.len - 1] == '\n');`: a comptime assert on a format string. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-error-model-convergence#^f2]] [zig/safety] A separate mechanism for detectable bugs; in Zig and Swift panic aborts the process. (B/fair, verified)
- [[matklad-error-model-convergence#^f3]] [zig/safety] [zig/errors] An operational error from a lower layer can be a programming error for the layer above, so there is a way to escalate a result to a panic; bugs in ordinary code are catastrophic and invisible in types. (B/fair, verified)
- [[matklad-fix-deeper#^f2]] [zig/safety] `defer assert(postcondition);` is a favourite Zig pattern; it failed in the simulator and revealed unintended reentrancy. (B/fair, verified)
- [[matklad-frng-gist#^f2]] [zig/comptime] [zig/safety] `int` asserts at comptime the integer is unsigned and the target little-endian before `@bitCast`. (C/fair, verified)
- [[matklad-invariant#^f1]] [zig/safety] In the small: state precisely what the code must do as a loop invariant (`lo <= insertion_point <= hi`), then make branches match it; fixes code that rules of thumb got wrong. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-invariant#^f2]] [zig/safety] Invariants are compressed descriptions that collapse the time dimension, covering all execution paths. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-lovely-syntax#^f7]] [zig/safety] He writes "infinite" loops as `for (0..safety_bound) /_/ {} else @panic("loop safety counter exceeded");`, because there is almost always a bound and crashes debug easier than hangs. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-memory-safety-hardest#^f1]] [zig/safety] Taking `/*p/` into a union(enum) variant, then assigning a different variant, leaves a typed pointer reading the other variant's bytes: type confusion that Zig's safety checks don't catch. (B/fair, verified)
- [[matklad-memory-safety-hardest#^f2]] [zig/safety] The most exploitable error, buffer overflow, is trivially fixed by compiler-inserted bounds checks (which Zig slices have). (B/fair, verified)
- [[matklad-monotonic-time#^f1]] [zig/safety] Don't fully trust the OS monotonic clock; clamp with an in-process guard and assert `t >= clock.guard` and `t >= t_raw`. (B/fair, verified)
- [[matklad-monotonic-time#^f2]] [zig/safety] Strictly monotonic (`@max(t_raw, clock.guard + 1ns)`) lets `assert(past <= present)` become `assert(past < present)`, also catching reuse of the same instant. (B/fair, verified)
- [[matklad-monotonic-time#^f3]] [zig/safety] Constraint: value resolution must be high enough that repeated +1 doesn't drift; nanoseconds suffice. (B/fair, verified)
- [[matklad-newtype-index#^f9]] [zig/safety] [zig/perf] Sentinel `invalid = std.math.maxInt(u32)` for "no parent" instead of `?Node` (which wastes space), as offensive programming so bad indexes blow up. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-newtype-index#^f10]] [zig/perf] [zig/safety] `comptime { assert(@sizeOf(Data) == 12); }` inside the struct, as a comment telling the reader the size, not to prevent changes. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-parsing-advances#^f1]] [zig/safety] Earlier techniques: fuel (decremented even by lookahead, topped up on consume) and a mental map of which functions always consume a token. (B/fair, verified)
- [[matklad-parsing-advances#^f2]] [zig/safety] Better: assert the parser advanced where you expect it; the asserts materialise the mental map in the source. (B/fair, verified)
- [[matklad-parsing-advances#^f3]] [zig/safety] API: `advance_push()` saves the index, `advance_pop()` asserts it grew, `advance_drop()` for exits that needn't advance. (B/fair, verified)
- [[matklad-partial-enums#^f2]] [zig/comptime] [zig/safety] Fix: `inline .a, .b => /_, ab/ { handle_ab(); switch (ab) { .a => ..., .b => ..., else => comptime unreachable } }`. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-partial-enums#^f4]] [zig/comptime] [zig/safety] Adding `.c` to the inline prong fails with "reached unreachable code", so a later refactor bug is a compile error. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-random-numbers-included#^f3]] [zig/api] [zig/safety] Exclusive ranges are panicky (an empty range below zero crashes); inclusive pushes `- 1` to the caller, making the non-zero precondition visible: `prng.int_inclusive(u8, replica_count - 1)`. (B/fair, verified)
- [[matklad-reserve-first#^f5]] [zig/errors] [zig/safety] `errdefer comptime unreachable;` marks "no errors after this point" (end of reservation phase), checked at compile time. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-retry-loop#^f1]] [zig/safety] Wants an obvious upper bound: `for (0..5)` rather than `while (true)` with a break in the middle (links TigerBeetle PR 1367). (B/fair, verified)
- [[matklad-retry-loop-retry#^f3]] [zig/safety] Bounded version: `for (0..retry_count + 1) { ... } else @panic("runaway loop");` restores the syntactic bound. (B/fair, verified)
- [[matklad-retry-loop-retry#^f4]] [zig/safety] Weakness: forgetting the `+1` panics. (B/fair, verified)
- [[matklad-snapshot-testing#^f9]] [zig/test] [zig/safety] Tests use `assert` heavily, and assert container counts before and after loops. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-static-alloc-constant-work#^f1]] [zig/memory] [zig/safety] With a typed pool of T, a logical use-after-free aliases memory but causes no type confusion, unless the object stores an inline enum. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-static-alloc-constant-work#^f7]] [zig/safety] Objects circulate rather than being created and destroyed; write transition functions per pair of states and assert the expected state at every point. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-static-alloc-constant-work#^f11]] [zig/safety] In the small: instead of a search loop with early `break`, run the loop to completion and assert the match is unique (TigerBeetle grid.zig @0.17.9). (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-test-minimization#^f4]] [zig/comptime] [zig/safety] `int(frng, Int: type)` asserts at comptime that Int is unsigned and the target is little-endian, rather than being endian-clean. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-test-minimization#^f14]] [zig/test] [zig/safety] Assertion failures crash and Zig has no unwinding, so the searcher runs each test as a child process with entropy on stdin; a run is reproduced from (size, seed). (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-test-minimization#^f16]] [zig/safety] The search loop is `for (0..1024) /_/ { ... } else @panic("safety counter");`, his bounded-loop habit. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-value-interner#^f4]] [zig/safety] Invariant: possessing an index proves the item is allocated, initialised and published; manufacturing indexes out of thin air is unchecked. (C/fair, verified)
- [[matklad-zig-and-rust#^f4]] [zig/safety] Everything has an explicit upper bound, checked at the edges of the system. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-zig-and-rust#^f12]] [zig/safety] TigerBeetle runs ReleaseSafe (spatial safety), avoids dynamic allocation (temporal) and has a fuzzer-driven suite. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-zig-and-rust#^f13]] [zig/safety] His biggest worry: fuzzy semantics around aliasing, provenance and structs with interior pointers. (B/strong, verified)
- [[mitchellh-ghostty-gtk-rewrite#^f4]] [zig/safety] The Zig core showed one leak (inside a third-party C call) and one undefined memory access, fewer than he expected given its memory tricks. (B/strong, verified)
- [[mitchellh-ghostty-gtk-rewrite#^f5]] [zig/safety] Protections that did the work: the leak-detecting debug allocator and safety checks in Debug and test builds, plus Zig's Valgrind integration that marks memory set to `undefined`. (B/strong, verified)
- [[mitchellh-ghostty-gtk-rewrite#^f6]] [zig/safety] He concludes Debug-only safety works even though release builds have none of it. (B/strong, verified)
- [[mitchellh-ghostty-gtk-rewrite#^f7]] [zig/safety] [zig/api] Every other issue (dozens) sat on C API boundaries, where lifetime is transferred or blurred; safety there is only as good as the wrapper. (B/strong, verified)
- [[mitchellh-ghostty-gtk-rewrite#^f8]] [zig/safety] Example of a boundary bug: a GObject WeakRef not cleared in dispose caused rare undefined access hours or days later; Valgrind found it. (B/strong, verified)
- [[river-repo#^f16]] [zig/safety] Assertions document state-machine invariants (188 assert calls in 15k lines), e.g. a window is mapped or closing before a step runs. `river/Window.zig` (A/strong, verified)
- [[roc-zig-compiler#^f2]] [zig/safety] [zig/errors] One rule for compiler invariants everywhere: a debug-only assertion in debug builds, `unreachable` in release; never a release runtime check, panic, error return or recovery. User-facing failures (bad input, missing files, allocation failure, corrupt caches) are reported... (B/strong, verified)
- [[roc-zig-compiler#^f3]] [zig/safety] Every `unreachable` carries an end-of-line comment naming the upstream invariant that makes the case impossible; a build step enforces it for every file. `design.md`, `src/check/dispatch_evidence.zig` (B/strong, verified)
- [[roc-zig-compiler#^f4]] [zig/safety] Where the invariant cannot be named at the site, `base.invariant(fmt, args)`: panics with the message when runtime safety is on, else `unreachable`. `src/base/invariant.zig` (B/strong, verified)
- [[roc-zig-compiler#^f5]] [zig/test] [zig/safety] Death tests pin that an invariant fires: the test re-runs itself in a child copy of the test binary and passes when the child panics with the expected message, because a panic ends the whole test process. `src/base/death_test.zig` (B/strong, verified)
- [[roc-zig-compiler#^f7]] [zig/style] [zig/safety] Lint: a `switch` may have an `else` prong only when it also switches on literals or errors; over enums every case must be listed, so a new variant fails to compile. `ci/zig_lints.zig` (B/strong, verified)
- [[roc-zig-compiler#^f15]] [zig/safety] [zig/perf] No recursion over input-shaped data in any stage: walks keep pending work on heap-backed frame stacks or worklists; a bigger thread stack or depth limit is not an accepted fix; tests run deep shapes on a tiny-stack thread. `design.md` (B/strong, verified)
- [[seguin-learning-zig-heap-memory#^f22]] [zig/safety] From 0.16 the compiler rejects returning `&local` from a function. (B/fair, verified)
- [[seguin-learning-zig-language-overview-2#^f8]] [zig/safety] `.?` on null panics; `if (opt) /v/` or `orelse` unwrap safely. (B/fair, verified)
- [[seguin-learning-zig-stack-memory#^f2]] [zig/safety] Reading through it is undefined: garbage, a segfault, or apparently correct output. (B/fair, verified)
- [[seguin-learning-zig-stack-memory#^f3]] [zig/safety] By 0.16 the compiler rejects his original example, so he had to change it; the slice-through-bufPrint case still compiles. (B/fair, verified)
- [[seguin-linkedlist-fieldparentptr#^f3]] [zig/safety] Never hard-code field offsets: plain struct layout is not guaranteed; use `@offsetOf` (or `@fieldParentPtr`). (B/fair, verified)
- [[seguin-new-io-unsafe#^f1]] [zig/safety] A stream may require a minimum buffer on the other side; zstd decompression streamed into a writer with a 64-byte buffer fails an assertion in Debug and loops forever in release. (C/fair, verified)
- [[superhtml-repo#^f19]] [zig/errors] [zig/safety] A root `pub fn panic` override routes panics and stack traces to the log file in LSP mode (stderr belongs to the protocol) and breaks into the debugger in Debug. `src/main.zig` (B/strong, verified)
- [[superhtml-repo#^f25]] [zig/safety] Idiom counts in src/ (43k lines): 106 `assert(`, 37 `@panic(`, 19 `catch unreachable`, 23 `errdefer`, 8 `anytype`, 5 `inline for`. (B/strong, unquoted)
- [[tigerbeetle-architecture#^f9]] [zig/perf] [zig/safety] Control plane vs data plane: keep control `if`s out of data `for`s; the cheap control plane can afford O(N) assertions for O(1) work. (A/strong, verified)
- [[tigerbeetle-architecture#^f12]] [zig/safety] Assertions stay on in production: better to stop than run in an incorrect state; thousands of assertions plus simulator checkers. `docs/internals/vopr.md` (A/strong, verified)
- [[tigerbeetle-blog-asserting-implications#^f1]] [zig/safety] [zig/style] Implications are asserted often; `assert(!a or b)` is hard to read, `if (a) assert(b)` is clearer. (B/fair, verified)
- [[tigerbeetle-blog-asserting-implications#^f2]] [zig/safety] Real before and after from TigerBeetle. (B/fair, verified)
- [[tigerbeetle-blog-pair-assertions#^f1]] [zig/safety] Design by contract needs no language feature in Zig: `assert` for preconditions and `defer assert(...)` for postconditions. (A/strong, verified)
- [[tigerbeetle-blog-pair-assertions#^f2]] [zig/safety] A contract is real only with two assertions of the same condition: one at the call site, one at the definition. (A/strong, verified)
- [[tigerbeetle-blog-pair-assertions#^f3]] [zig/safety] Call-site asserts also document what the returned value means. (A/strong, verified)
- [[tigerbeetle-blog-pair-assertions#^f4]] [zig/safety] The pair is an airlock: check the two sets of asserts agree, then reason about each side separately. (A/strong, verified)
- [[tigerbeetle-blog-pair-assertions#^f5]] [zig/safety] Pairs survive refactors and catch slips: the author found while writing the post that the definition site lacked one of the call-site checks. (A/strong, verified)
- [[tigerbeetle-blog-pair-assertions#^f6]] [zig/safety] The strongest pairs reach one property by different paths (sender vs receiver, replayed vs restored state), like property tests that compute a result two ways. (A/strong, verified)
- [[tigerbeetle-blog-pair-assertions#^f7]] [zig/safety] `maybe(condition)` is a no-op assert that marks a condition as sometimes true, for readability. (A/strong, verified)
- [[tigerbeetle-blog-pair-assertions#^f8]] [zig/safety] Rule of thumb: when writing an assert, find the distant code that relies on it and add an equivalent assert there. (A/strong, verified)
- [[tigerbeetle-code#^f4]] [zig/build] [zig/safety] Default optimise mode is ReleaseSafe, not ReleaseFast: safety checks stay on in shipped builds. `build.zig` (A/strong, verified)
- [[tigerbeetle-code#^f9]] [zig/safety] `config_verify` (extra, expensive assertions) defaults on in Debug and off in release builds; fuzzers and VOPR assert at comptime it is on. `build.zig` (A/strong, verified)
- [[tigerbeetle-code#^f12]] [zig/safety] [zig/api] `Replica.open` starts with a block of argument assertions, including implications (`if (p) assert(...)`), and passes a long options struct field by field. `vsr/replica.zig` (A/strong, verified)
- [[tigerbeetle-code#^f14]] [zig/comptime] [zig/safety] Constants are checked against each other in `comptime` blocks with long comments giving the invariant. `constants.zig` (A/strong, verified)
- [[tigerbeetle-code#^f16]] [zig/safety] Pair assertions, write then read: before writing a block the grid zeroes sector padding and asserts it is zeroed; after reading it checks checksum, command, checksum of body, expected checksum and zeroed padding, returning a fault enum for disk corruption (operating error) while asse... (A/strong, verified)
- [[tigerbeetle-code#^f19]] [zig/memory] [zig/safety] `stdx.copy_disjoint` replaces `@memcpy`: asserts lengths (`.exact` or `.inexact` spelled at the call site) and that the slices don't overlap; `copy_left`/`copy_right` replace `copyForwards`/`copyBackwards` with direction asserts. `stdx/stdx.zig` (A/strong, verified)
- [[tigerbeetle-code#^f20]] [zig/safety] `maybe(cond)` is the dual of assert: documents that a condition can go either way. 303 uses in src/. `stdx/stdx.zig` (A/strong, verified)
- [[tigerbeetle-code#^f22]] [zig/comptime] [zig/safety] `no_padding(T)` and `equal_bytes(T, a, b)` check at comptime that a type has no implicit padding and no pointers before comparing raw bytes; extern structs with u128 fields must be 16-aligned. `stdx/stdx.zig` (A/strong, verified)
- [[tigerbeetle-code#^f40]] [zig/test] [zig/safety] VOPR refuses ReleaseFast and ReleaseSmall (assertions off), and without a seed requires ReleaseSafe for speed; leaks fail the run. `vopr.zig` (A/strong, verified)
- [[tigerbeetle-code#^f42]] [zig/safety] Measured (our script, innermost function, excluding type functions and tests, 15 files): 861 functions, 3174 asserts, 3.69 per function. VSR and LSM files exceed two (replica 5.9, superblock 4.8, compaction 4.1, journal 3.8, grid 3.2, tree 2.2); infrastructure is under one (bounded_a... (A/strong, unquoted)
- [[tigerbeetle-how-we-use-zig#^f1]] [zig/safety] Integer overflow is illegal behaviour, checked (panics) by default; downcasts need an explicit truncate or checked cast. @3:58 (C/weak, verified)
- [[tigerbeetle-tiger-style#^f1]] [zig/safety] Design goals are ranked: safety, then performance, then developer experience; style exists to serve them, readability is only table stakes. (A/strong, verified)
- [[tigerbeetle-tiger-style#^f3]] [zig/safety] Builds on NASA's Power of Ten: simple explicit control flow, no recursion so that bounded executions stay bounded, few abstractions because none is zero cost. (A/strong, verified)
- [[tigerbeetle-tiger-style#^f4]] [zig/safety] Put a limit on everything: every loop and queue has a fixed upper bound, fail fast; a loop that must not end (event loop) asserts that. (A/strong, verified)
- [[tigerbeetle-tiger-style#^f6]] [zig/safety] Assertions are for programmer errors, distinct from operating errors which must be handled; the only correct response to corrupt code is to crash. (A/strong, verified)
- [[tigerbeetle-tiger-style#^f7]] [zig/safety] Assert arguments, return values, pre and postconditions and invariants; minimum average of two assertions per function. (A/strong, verified)
- [[tigerbeetle-tiger-style#^f8]] [zig/safety] Pair assertions: enforce each property on two different code paths, e.g. before writing to disk and right after reading back. (A/strong, verified)
- [[tigerbeetle-tiger-style#^f9]] [zig/safety] Split compound assertions; use one-line `if` for implications. (A/strong, verified)
- [[tigerbeetle-tiger-style#^f10]] [zig/safety] [zig/comptime] Assert relationships between compile-time constants: checks design integrity before the program runs. (A/strong, verified)
- [[tigerbeetle-tiger-style#^f11]] [zig/safety] Golden rule: assert the positive space and the negative space; tests must cover invalid data and valid data turning invalid. (A/strong, verified)
- [[tigerbeetle-tiger-style#^f42]] [zig/safety] Buffer bleeds: zero padding so stale bytes don't leak or break determinism. (A/strong, verified)
- [[tigerbeetle-tiger-style-talk#^f3]] [zig/safety] Assert every argument and return value, and how they relate to each other and program state: a function's job is correctness. @27:06 (A/fair, verified)
- [[tigerbeetle-tiger-style-talk#^f4]] [zig/safety] Assert the negative space, as a hacker would: what you do not expect. @30:06 (A/fair, verified)
