- [[tigerbeetle-tiger-style-talk#^f5]] [zig/safety] Avoid `while (true)` except for an event loop; bound loops and assert both upper and lower bounds. @31:36 (A/fair, verified)
- [[tigerbeetle-tiger-style-talk#^f7]] [zig/safety] A cache-versus-disk assertion caught a rare bug during benchmarking that tests had missed; it took 8 hours to debug instead of months. @36:07 (A/fair, verified)
- [[tigerbeetle-tiger-style-talk#^f8]] [zig/errors] [zig/safety] Operating errors (e.g. out of memory: refuse new connections) are handled; programming errors can't be predicted, so the only safe response is to shut down. @39:08 (A/fair, verified)
- [[tigerbeetle-tiger-style-talk#^f9]] [zig/safety] Keep assertions on in production; in a fault-tolerant cluster crashing a faulty process raises availability. @40:38 (A/fair, verified)
- [[tigerbeetle-tiger-style-talk#^f10]] [zig/safety] Zig's checked arithmetic in safe builds should be standard; opt out per block only for covered data-plane code. Comptime assertions check design before the program runs, one main reason they chose Zig. @43:38 (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-011-release-notes#^f2]] [zig/safety] @memcpy takes (dest, source); overlap is safety-checked UB, which is the reason to prefer it over std helpers. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-011-release-notes#^f4]] [zig/safety] Cast builtins dropped the destination type and infer it from the result location; motive: an explicit type at the call site goes silently wrong when the parameter type changes. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-012-release-notes#^f17]] [zig/safety] Pointer stability locks (std.debug.SafetyLock, lockPointers) make hash map mutation while a value pointer is held panic instead of UB; assume-capacity variants are allowed. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-014-release-notes#^f10]] [zig/safety] @fence removed: it changes orderings of all atomics and is hard to model in a sanitizer; use stronger orderings or another atomic. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-014-release-notes#^f20]] [zig/safety] Comparing Allocator.ptr (or Random.ptr) is illegal behaviour: it is undefined for stateless allocators. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-014-release-notes#^f34]] [zig/safety] UBSan runtime on by default in Debug for C code. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-015-release-notes#^f8]] [zig/safety] Arithmetic on undefined is illegal behaviour (compile error at comptime) for any operator that can trigger IB; best practice: never operate on undefined. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-015-release-notes#^f9]] [zig/safety] Lossy comptime int-to-float coercion is a compile error; write a float literal to opt in to rounding. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-015-release-notes#^f10]] [zig/safety] @ptrCast can turn a single-item pointer into a byte slice; planned to move to a safer @memCast. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-015-release-notes#^f31]] [zig/safety] UBSan mode per module: `-fsanitize-c=trap/full`; build API sanitize_c is an enum. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-016-release-notes#^f28]] [zig/safety] Returning the address of a local is a compile error when trivially visible; more such errors planned. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-016-release-notes#^f31]] [zig/safety] Packed types: no pointers; packed unions need equal-size fields; enums and packed types in extern contexts need explicit tag or backing integers (ABI must not be implicit). (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-017-release-notes#^f14]] [zig/safety] @bitCast now reinterprets the logical bit representation, endian-agnostic; array and vector casts may change meaning silently, so audit them; casts on extern structs are rejected: type-pun with @ptrCast or an extern union. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-017-release-notes#^f16]] [zig/safety] @backingInt / @fromBackingInt replace @intFromEnum / @enumFromInt (deprecated; zig fmt upgrades); invalid tag values are safety-checked, and @bitCast into an enum is now checked too. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-017-release-notes#^f27]] [zig/safety] debug.SafetyLock gains lockShared/unlockShared for many readers. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-017-release-notes#^f31]] [zig/safety] OptimizeMode renamed Optimize with tags debug/safe/fast/small; `std.lang.Optimize.runtimeSafety` preferred over std.debug.runtime_safety because it knows the caller's module. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-compiler-dod#^f3]] [zig/safety] Indices are non-exhaustive `enum(u32)` types (Node.Index, OptionalIndex, Offset), so a node index cannot be passed where a token index is expected. `std/zig/Ast.zig:2919` (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-compiler-dod#^f21]] [zig/safety] Invariants are asserted at the point of use (161 `assert(` in InternPool.zig), e.g. on keys before interning. `src/InternPool.zig:7276` (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-devlog-2024#^f1]] [zig/api] [zig/safety] Andrew Kelley names integer indexes as distinct non-exhaustive `enum(u32) { _ }` types in all his projects, so the type system catches mixed-up indexes at compile time. "The Unreasonable Effectiveness of Naming Integers" (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-devlog-2025#^f15]] [zig/safety] `zig cc` enables `-fsanitize=undefined` by default for C and now has a UBSan runtime that prints the error and location instead of SIGILL. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-devlog-2026#^f13]] [zig/memory] [zig/safety] `ArrayList.lockPointers()` / `unlockPointers()` assert that the list does not reallocate (or reorder) while pointers into it are held, turning a silent dangling slice into a panic with a stack trace. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-a#^f20]] [zig/safety] Every variable needs an initialiser; leaving it uninitialised must be written as `= undefined`, which means "a bug if read". Assignment, undefined (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-a#^f21]] [zig/safety] Once coerced, `undefined` can't be detected; the 0xaa fill in Debug and ReleaseSafe is a debugging aid, not semantics code may rely on. undefined (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-a#^f41]] [zig/safety] Runtime integers can overflow and divide by zero (illegal behaviour); wrapping (`+%`) and saturating (`+/`) operators state the intent instead. Runtime Integer Values (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-a#^f43]] [zig/safety] [toolchain] `/` on signed ints needs comptime-known positive operands; otherwise pick the rounding explicitly with `@divTrunc`, `@divFloor`, `@divCeil` (new in 0.17) or `@divExact`; `%` likewise needs `@rem` or `@mod`. Table of Operators (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-a#^f44]] [zig/safety] A runtime shift amount must have the log2 type of the shifted operand (e.g. `u5` for `u32`), so out-of-range shifts don't type-check. Table of Operators (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-a#^f55]] [zig/safety] Arrays and vectors share bit layout (`@bitCast` fine) but vectors have no defined byte layout, so `@ptrCast` between them is illegal behaviour. Relationship with Arrays (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-a#^f58]] [zig/safety] Prefer slices to many-item and sentinel pointers: slices are bounds checked. Pointers (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-a#^f59]] [zig/safety] For pointer arithmetic, copy to a `[*]T` and increment that; bumping a slice's `.ptr` leaves its `len` wrong. Pointers (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-a#^f60]] [zig/safety] `@ptrCast` can cause undetectable illegal behaviour and depends on endianness; prefer other conversions, e.g. `@bitCast` for value reinterpretation. Pointers (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-a#^f61]] [zig/safety] `volatile` is for MMIO only; using it for concurrency is a bug (use atomics). volatile (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-a#^f62]] [zig/safety] `@alignCast` raises a pointer's alignment with a runtime safety check; over-aligned pointers coerce to less-aligned ones, not the reverse. Alignment (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-a#^f63]] [zig/safety] `allowzero` is only for freestanding targets; to represent null use an optional pointer. allowzero (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-a#^f67]] [zig/safety] `slice.ptr` is unchecked; `&slice[0]` asserts a non-empty slice. Slices (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-a#^f69]] [zig/safety] `[:x]T` guarantees the sentinel at `len` but not its absence earlier; `data[a..b :0]` checks the sentinel at runtime. Sentinel-Terminated Slices (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-a#^f78]] [zig/safety] An explicit backing integer `packed struct(u32)` is checked against the fields' total width at compile time. packed struct (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-a#^f80]] [zig/safety] A pointer to a non-byte-aligned packed field has a bit-offset type and can't be passed as a normal `*T`. packed struct (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-a#^f81]] [zig/safety] Packed field writes are read-modify-write shifts, not atomic: for MMIO build the whole value and store it once through the volatile pointer. packed struct (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-a#^f93]] [zig/safety] `@fromBackingInt` on a non-exhaustive enum is always valid; on an exhaustive enum an invalid value is safety-checked illegal behaviour (release notes). Non-exhaustive enum (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-a#^f94]] [zig/safety] Bare unions have no layout guarantee and can't reinterpret memory; reading the inactive field panics in safe modes; switch to another field by assigning the whole union. union (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-a#^f118]] [zig/safety] `unreachable` panics in Debug and ReleaseSafe but is an optimiser assumption in ReleaseFast and ReleaseSmall; `std.debug.assert` is just `if (!ok) unreachable`, so asserts are checks in safe modes and hints in fast ones. unreachable (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-b#^f5]] [zig/errors] [zig/safety] `catch unreachable` is an assertion: it panics in debug and safe builds if the assumption is wrong, and is the sanctioned way to ignore an error. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-b#^f18]] [zig/errors] [zig/safety] Checked arithmetic: std.math.add/sub/mul/divTrunc/divFloor/divCeil/ divExact/shl return errors; @addWithOverflow and friends return a (result, u1) tuple; `+%` `-%` `*%` wrap by definition. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-b#^f19]] [zig/safety] [zig/perf] `?*T` is the size of `*T` (null is address 0), so optional pointers cost nothing; plain pointers can never be null. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-b#^f21]] [zig/safety] Coercion is for conversions that are completely safe; explicit casts (builtins) for those that should not happen by accident. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-b#^f24]] [zig/safety] `@intCast` asserts the value fits (safety-checked); `@truncate` deliberately drops high bits and is defined for any value. Pick by intent. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-b#^f27]] [toolchain] [zig/safety] 0.17: @intFromFloat is deprecated; @round, @floor, @ceil and @trunc convert straight to an integer result type, with a safety check that it fits. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-b#^f28]] [zig/safety] @floatFromInt is always legal; it rounds to the closest float. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-b#^f29]] [zig/safety] @ptrCast is deliberately narrow: const, volatile, address space and alignment each have their own cast, and pointer-to-slice uses slicing. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-b#^f30]] [zig/safety] @bitCast needs equal sizes and a defined layout: not for bare structs, slices, optionals, error unions or pointers (use @ptrCast/@ptrFromInt). (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-b#^f31]] [zig/safety] @alignCast inserts a runtime alignment check. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-b#^f32]] [zig/safety] Integers coerce to floats only when exact (u24 to f32, u53 to f64); larger need @floatFromInt. A comptime-known value may narrow implicitly when it fits. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-b#^f37]] [zig/safety] Gotcha: `arr = .{ arr[1], arr[0] }` is not a swap, because the initialiser desugars into field-by-field stores into the destination. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-b#^f56]] [zig/safety] Use `std.crypto.secureZero`, not @memset, to wipe secrets. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-b#^f59]] [zig/safety] @divExact/@divFloor/@divTrunc/@divCeil/@mod/@rem put the preconditions on the caller; `std.math` versions return errors instead. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-b#^f60]] [zig/safety] [zig/perf] @setRuntimeSafety overrides the build mode per scope, both ways; planned to become @optimizeFor. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-b#^f63]] [toolchain] [zig/safety] 0.17 optimise modes are debug, safe, fast, small (`-Odebug` etc.); `builtin.mode` is deprecated for `builtin.optimize`. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-b#^f64]] [zig/safety] Mode traits: debug fast compile, safety on; safe medium speed, safety on; fast safety off; small safety off, small binary; all but debug reproducible. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-b#^f66]] [zig/safety] Illegal behaviour: compile error if caught at comptime; otherwise safety-checked (panics) or unchecked (anything can happen, like C UB). (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-b#^f67]] [zig/safety] Most IB is checked, but fast and small turn checks off, and then checked IB becomes unchecked. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-b#^f68]] [zig/safety] (inferred) The safety-checked list, one section each: reaching unreachable, index out of bounds, negative to unsigned cast, cast truncates data, integer overflow (+ - neg * / and the @div builtins), exact shift overflow (left, right), division and remainder by zero, exact division re... (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-b#^f69]] [zig/safety] Bare-union field checks don't exist for `extern` or `packed` unions; switch the active field by assigning the whole union (with `undefined` payload if needed). (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-b#^f70]] [zig/safety] [zig/memory] Unchecked IB the doc names: dangling pointers into a returned stack frame, missing asm clobbers, @fieldParentPtr on the wrong field. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-b#^f71]] [zig/safety] `unreachable` panics in debug, safe and zig test; in fast and small it is an optimiser assumption. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-b#^f72]] [zig/safety] (inferred) The unwrap-null and unwrap-error sections give the fix as testing with `if (x) /v/` instead of assuming, so `.?` and `catch unreachable` are assertions to use only where the invariant really holds. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-b#^f82]] [zig/memory] [zig/safety] Recursion is unbounded memory, and there is no stack overflow protection yet. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-b#^f107]] [zig/style] [zig/safety] Doc vocabulary: "assume" for invariants whose violation is unchecked IB, "assert" for safety-checked IB. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-learn-overview#^f2]] [zig/safety] Four build modes; safety checks are on in Debug and ReleaseSafe and can be toggled per scope with `@setRuntimeSafety`. (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-learn-overview#^f3]] [zig/safety] [zig/perf] Recommended stance: run a safety-enabled build and disable safety only at measured bottlenecks. (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-learn-overview#^f4]] [zig/safety] [zig/perf] Illegal behaviour serves both bug prevention and optimisation; unsigned overflow is illegal too, unlike C, which enables optimisations. (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-learn-overview#^f10]] [zig/errors] [zig/safety] `catch unreachable` asserts no error; it is undefined behaviour in unsafe modes, so only where success is guaranteed. (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-std-allocators#^f7]] [zig/safety] `free` sets the memory to undefined before releasing it, so in safe builds use-after-free reads garbage patterns rather than plausible old data. `std/mem/Allocator.zig:468` (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-std-allocators#^f17]] [zig/safety] SafeAllocator guarantees: deinit reports leaks, mismatches panic or fault, cross-instance frees caught by a canary, write-after-free mostly detected. `std/heap/SafeAllocator.zig:1` (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-std-allocators#^f19]] [zig/safety] Layout invariants are checked with `comptime { assert(...) }` blocks next to the type they constrain. `std/heap/SafeAllocator.zig:110` (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-std-containers#^f10]] [zig/safety] Containers carry a `pointer_stability: debug.SafetyLock` (zero-size in release) and check it in every method that may move memory; `lockPointers` turns a dangling-pointer bug into an assertion. `std/array_list.zig:651` (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-std-containers#^f30]] [zig/safety] Asserts are dense but targeted: 26 `assert(` in array_list.zig, 16 in hash_map.zig, mostly preconditions on capacity and indices (counted with grep). (A/strong, unquoted)
- [[zig-std-io#^f20]] [zig/safety] Reader functions that need contiguous bytes assert the buffer is big enough rather than allocate, so the caller sizes the buffer. `std/Io/Reader.zig:512` (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-std-json-fmt-args#^f9]] [zig/safety] Untrusted-input limit by default: 4 MiB per string or number value, with a `*Max` variant to change it. `std/json/Scanner.zig:1569` (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-std-testing#^f20]] [zig/safety] std.zig pins its own identity with a top-level `comptime` assert, a configuration check that costs nothing at runtime. `std/std.zig:247` (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-talk-kelley-dod#^f5]] [zig/api] [zig/safety] Warning: bare integer indexes lose the type safety pointers had; use distinct types where the language allows. @15:18 (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-talk-kelley-tagged-union#^f5]] [zig/safety] Open moonshots: eliminate recursion and guarantee bounded stack usage, ranged integer types against overflow, removing all unchecked illegal behaviour. @44:59 (A/fair, verified)
- [[ziggit-code-smells#^f4]] [zig/safety] Smells: `undefined` as a default for non-array fields; allocators stored in many small structs; treating `unreachable` as a panic. (C/fair, verified)
- [[zls-repo#^f14]] [zig/memory] [zig/safety] deinit ends with `self.* = undefined;` so use-after-deinit shows as 0xaa garbage in safe builds. `src/DocumentStore.zig` (A/fair, verified)
- [[zls-repo#^f23]] [zig/safety] Assertions: `const assert = std.debug.assert;` alias per file; ~234 assert calls in 37k lines, plus doc comments stating preconditions ("Asserts that ..."). `src/DocumentScope.zig`, `src/analyser/InternPool.zig` (A/fair, verified)
- [[zls-repo#^f24]] [zig/safety] [zig/comptime] comptime asserts check buffer-size relations at compile time. `src/main.zig` (A/fair, verified)

### zig/style: naming, file and module layout, formatting, comments and doc comments (241)

- [[ak-openzfs-bug-zig#^f1]] [zig/style] [zig/safety] `zig fmt --ast-check` (no type checking) rejects redeclaring a local const, which exposed dead initialisation code in the C original. (A/fair, verified)
- [[ak-openzfs-bug-zig#^f2]] [zig/style] [zig/safety] An unused local constant is a compile error, which caught the real bug (the function returned its input, not the computed psize). (A/fair, verified)
- [[ak-openzfs-bug-zig#^f3]] [zig/style] (inferred) Naming each intermediate as a new const (asize_shifted, parity_adjusted, psize) instead of mutating one var is what lets the unused check fire. (A/fair, verified)
- [[bun-in-rust#^f12]] [zig/style] Style guides (TigerStyle named) need enforcement; review and linters were the only tools. (B/strong, verified)
- [[bun-repo#^f2]] [zig/style] [toolchain] Uses `#`-prefixed private struct fields, a feature of its fork, not upstream Zig (which has no private fields). `src/CLAUDE.md` (A/fair, verified)
- [[bun-repo#^f3]] [zig/style] Convention: `@import`s go at the bottom of the file (ptr/shared.zig ends with them), never inline in functions, since imports have no side effects. `src/CLAUDE.md`, `src/ptr/shared.zig` (A/fair, verified)
- [[bun-repo#^f13]] [zig/style] Logging by scope enabled with an env var per scope in debug builds. `CONTRIBUTING.md` (A/fair, verified)
- [[ghostty-repo#^f1]] [zig/style] Files are structs: a file starts with a `//!` module doc and `const Name = @This();`, then imports, then fields. `src/terminal/PageList.zig:1-4` (A/strong, verified)
- [[ghostty-repo#^f2]] [zig/style] Generic type functions name the returned struct `Self` (102 uses); file-structs use the real type name (114 files), e.g. `const Config = @This();`. `src/datastruct/untouched_pool.zig:35` (A/strong, verified)
- [[ghostty-repo#^f3]] [zig/style] A package's `main.zig` only re-exports public names and runs `refAllDecls` in a test, so every container is compiled and tested. `src/datastruct/main.zig:10-25` (A/strong, verified)
- [[ghostty-repo#^f4]] [zig/style] To keep the import graph small, Config imports single files into a local namespace struct instead of the whole terminal package. `src/config/Config.zig:46-55` (A/strong, verified)
- [[ghostty-repo#^f5]] [zig/style] Doc comments state the why and the cost, not the what; e.g. a flag that can be a false positive exists because it makes erase operations faster. `src/terminal/page.zig:2032-2035` (A/strong, verified)
- [[ghostty-repo#^f6]] [zig/style] Config fields use `@"kebab-case"` identifiers so field names are the CLI and file keys, and doc comments on them feed the generated man pages. `src/config/Config.zig:1-9,177` (A/strong, verified)
- [[ghostty-repo#^f7]] [zig/style] Each file declares one scoped logger (189 files), named after the module. `src/terminal/PageList.zig:31` (A/strong, verified)
- [[ghostty-repo#^f8]] [zig/style] Workarounds for other software are collected in one quirks.zig file, as a last resort. `src/quirks.zig:1-5` (A/strong, verified)
- [[ghostty-repo#^f9]] [zig/style] AGENTS.md: `zig fmt .`, and run filtered tests because the full suite is slow. `AGENTS.md` (A/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-aphorisms#^f4]] [zig/style] [zig/memory] Rename `alloc` to `gpa` (as opposed to `arena`), naming taken from the Zig compiler. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-aphorisms#^f5]] [zig/style] [zig/api] Name the configuration parameter `options`, not config, props or params (TigerBeetle). (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-comptime-orm#^f2]] [zig/memory] [zig/style] Allocator naming conveys lifetime: `gpa` (free individually), `arena` (no per-object free), `scratch` (short-lived, can't outlive the function). (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-defer-patterns#^f7]] [zig/style] defer as post-increment: `defer self.scan_buffer_used += 1; return &self.scan_buffers[self.scan_buffer_used];` after a limit check returning `Error.ScansMaxExceeded`. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-diagnostics-factory#^f6]] [zig/style] Naming tip: `line_index` for 0-based internal indexes, `line_number` for 1-based user-visible ones. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-frng-gist#^f1]] [zig/test] [zig/style] File-as-struct with one field `entropy: []const u8,`, `pub const Error = error{OutOfEntropy};`, `const FRNG = @This();`. (C/fair, verified)
- [[matklad-look-for-bugs#^f3]] [zig/style] Name things consistently (`connection` everywhere) and avoid `self`, so a text search for `connection.peer` finds every use. (B/fair, verified)
- [[matklad-look-for-bugs#^f5]] [zig/style] A tricky isolated function is probably fine; a tricky interaction between functions is a smell. (B/fair, verified)
- [[matklad-lovely-syntax#^f1]] [zig/style] Integer literals are `comptime_int` coerced on assignment, not inferred; so `var x = 92;` needs an explicit type. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-lovely-syntax#^f2]] [zig/style] `\\` multiline strings dodge indentation problems and lex line by line. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-lovely-syntax#^f3]] [zig/style] `.x = 1` in literals matches `obj.x = 1`, so grepping `.x =` finds every write of a field, which is what data flow needs. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-lovely-syntax#^f4]] [zig/style] Labeled block `blk: { ... break :blk value; }` yields a value; blocks are otherwise void. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-lovely-syntax#^f5]] [zig/style] One-line `if (a) b else c` is fine because zig fmt is part of the compiler and catches formatting that masks bugs (e.g. `1 -2` is an error). (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-lovely-syntax#^f6]] [zig/style] [zig/comptime] Loops are expressions with `else`: a comptime search `for (.{ u8, u16, ... }) /W/ { if (...) break W; } else unreachable`. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-lovely-syntax#^f8]] [zig/style] Zig forbids shadowing, has no prelude, no glob imports; `x.foo()` is always a method declared on x's type. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-lovely-syntax#^f13]] [zig/style] Built-ins split casts into named variants (`@intCast`, `@ptrCast`, ...) instead of one overloaded cast. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-lovely-syntax#^f14]] [zig/style] He dislikes `while (i < 10) : (i += 1)`: control flow out of source order and a sigil; `for (0..10) /i/` makes it redundant. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-lovely-syntax#^f15]] [zig/style] `defer increment` inside a while body is not equivalent: return, break or try run the increment one extra time. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-newtype-index#^f6]] [zig/api] [zig/style] Typed index: `const ItemIndex = enum(u32) { _ };`, a distinct u32 (popularised by Andrew Kelley); no strong encapsulation, anyone can `@enumFromInt`. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-newtype-index#^f7]] [zig/style] Define the collective noun first (`Tree`, not `Node`); don't suffix index types with `Index`. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-newtype-index#^f8]] [zig/style] Nested types are good (`Node.Data`); order within a struct: fields, then nested types, then functions. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-printing-lists#^f1]] [zig/style] Comma-separated list: `for (items, 0..) /item, item_index/ { if (item_index > 0) print(", "); print(...) }`; note the `item_index` naming. (C/weak, verified)
- [[matklad-push-ifs-up#^f2]] [zig/style] Centralise complex control flow in one function, delegating work to straight line subroutines; dead branches become visible. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-push-ifs-up#^f3]] [zig/style] "Dissolving enum" refactor: an enum built by one branch and matched by another is the same condition triplicated; pull the branch up. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-size-matters#^f1]] [zig/style] TigerStyle: hard limit of 70 lines per function, 100 columns per line, yet TigerBeetle has some very large files. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-size-matters#^f2]] [zig/style] Size itself is irrelevant: optimise the ratio of module size to its interface; keep related things together. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-size-matters#^f3]] [zig/style] Move a data structure to its own file when it is self contained, whether ten or ten thousand lines (replica.zig vs timestamp_range.zig). (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-size-matters#^f4]] [zig/style] Functions should have bodies large relative to their interface; hourglass functions or modules are a smell. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-size-matters#^f5]] [zig/style] 100 columns lets two copies sit side by side, to compare versions and see caller and callee. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-size-matters#^f6]] [zig/style] Function length bound is "fit on a screen", about 60-70 lines. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-size-matters#^f7]] [zig/style] No inherent limit on file size or count; rely on fuzzy file and symbol search. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-steering-zig-fmt#^f1]] [zig/style] zig fmt is steerable: the layout variant is chosen from what's in the file; a trailing comma puts one argument per line, none puts the call on one line. (B/fair, verified)
- [[matklad-steering-zig-fmt#^f2]] [zig/style] Decide the layout, add commas, reformat; 90% of good formatting is blank lines between logical blocks and well-chosen intermediate variables. (B/fair, verified)
- [[matklad-steering-zig-fmt#^f3]] [zig/style] Arrays: zig fmt also uses the first line break, giving aligned columns of that many items per line. (B/fair, verified)
- [[matklad-steering-zig-fmt#^f4]] [zig/style] `++` concatenation varies items per line, e.g. `--key value` pairs for a subprocess. (B/fair, verified)
- [[matklad-test-minimization#^f2]] [zig/style] File-as-struct: `entropy: []const u8,` field at top level, `const FRNG = @This();`, `pub fn init(entropy) FRNG`. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-wrapping-comments#^f1]] [zig/style] Code to ~100 columns, comment content to ~70 measured from the comment's start, still within 100. (C/fair, verified)
- [[matklad-wrapping-comments#^f2]] [zig/style] Hard-wrap comments: soft wrap can't indent wrapped markdown list items without understanding the text. (C/fair, verified)
- [[mitchellh-zig-comptime-tagged-union-subset#^f6]] [zig/style] Capitalised function names signal that a function returns a type. (B/strong, verified)
- [[river-repo#^f2]] [zig/style] Style: follow the Zig Style Guide and run `zig fmt` before every commit; lines under 100 columns in general, readability first; use trailing commas to make zig fmt wrap. `CONTRIBUTING.md` (A/strong, verified)
- [[river-repo#^f3]] [zig/style] The one extra rule: no multi-line `if` without braces; a one-line `if (foo) bar();` or a braced block. `CONTRIBUTING.md` (A/strong, verified)
- [[river-repo#^f4]] [zig/perf] [zig/style] Simplicity over nearly everything; performance justifies complexity only with profiling results showing a significant benefit. `CONTRIBUTING.md` (A/strong, verified)
- [[river-repo#^f5]] [zig/style] Commit messages prefixed by the affected part (`build:`, `Keyboard:`). `CONTRIBUTING.md` (A/strong, verified)
- [[river-repo#^f6]] [zig/style] Almost every file is a TitleCase struct (Window.zig, Seat.zig, XdgPopup.zig) opening with `const X = @This();`, then imports, then `const log = std.log.scoped(...)`, then fields; one file per object type; ARCHITECTURE.md maps the objects. `river/XdgPopup.zig`, `ARCHITECTURE.md` (A/strong, verified)
- [[river-repo#^f17]] [zig/style] Logging: std.log with a closed `LogScope` enum filtered at run time by `-log-scopes`, and level filtering left to logFn by setting `log_level = .debug` (same as ZLS). `river/main.zig` (A/strong, verified)
- [[river-repo#^f26]] [zig/style] Comments carry the why, often with an issue link for a workaround. `build.zig` (A/strong, verified)
- [[roc-zig-compiler#^f7]] [zig/style] [zig/safety] Lint: a `switch` may have an `else` prong only when it also switches on literals or errors; over enums every case must be listed, so a new variant fails to compile. `ci/zig_lints.zig` (B/strong, verified)
- [[roc-zig-compiler#^f8]] [zig/style] Lints: every `.zig` file starts with a `//!` module doc comment; every top-level `pub` decl has a `///` doc comment (except init, deinit, imports, re-exports); no horizontal-line separator comments. `ci/zig_lints.zig`, `AGENT.md` (B/strong, verified)
- [[roc-zig-compiler#^f9]] [zig/style] Docs and comments explain WHY, not WHAT or HOW, since implementation detail changes; every src/ directory has a README.md. `AGENT.md` (B/strong, verified)
- [[roc-zig-compiler#^f10]] [zig/style] The lints are themselves Zig programs using `std.zig.Ast.parse` over the source, run as build steps. `ci/zig_lints.zig` (B/strong, verified)
- [[seguin-decl-literals#^f5]] [zig/style] He finds it unclear which style to use in his own structs. (B/fair, verified)
- [[seguin-learning-zig-coding-in-zig#^f9]] [zig/style] No private fields or properties: a field access is always a field access. He prefixes internal fields with an underscore and thinks the lack of privacy a mistake. (B/fair, verified)
- [[seguin-learning-zig-generics#^f3]] [zig/style] Inside the returned struct, `const Self = @This();` names the type; PascalCase because it holds a type. (B/fair, verified)
- [[seguin-learning-zig-language-overview-2#^f6]] [zig/style] An `else` arm on an enum switch silently absorbs newly added variants, losing exhaustiveness. (B/fair, verified)
- [[seguin-learning-zig-pointers#^f1]] [zig/style] A `var` that is never mutated is a compile error; it must be `const`. (B/fair, verified)
- [[seguin-learning-zig-style-guide#^f1]] [zig/style] Types, variables holding types and functions returning types are PascalCase; functions camelCase, variables snake_case. (B/fair, verified)
- [[seguin-learning-zig-style-guide#^f2]] [zig/style] Builtins follow the same rule: `@TypeOf` is PascalCase because it returns a type. (B/fair, verified)
- [[seguin-learning-zig-style-guide#^f3]] [zig/style] Unused locals and parameters are compile errors; discard with `_ = x;`. He prefers that over naming the parameter `_`, which hides what it was. (B/fair, verified)
- [[seguin-learning-zig-style-guide#^f4]] [zig/style] Shadowing an outer identifier is a compile error; he dislikes it because it pushes towards short names like `n`. (B/fair, verified)
- [[seguin-learning-zig-style-guide#^f5]] [zig/style] `zig fmt` fixes indentation and braces but not identifier casing, so naming is on the author. (B/fair, verified)
- [[seguin-learning-zig-style-guide#^f6]] [zig/style] Unused imports (`const std = @import("std")`) are not an error. (B/fair, verified)
- [[seguin-temporaries-const#^f2]] [zig/style] Temporaries are const: `DefaultPrng.init(0).random()` fails; bind the value to a `var` first. (B/fair, verified)
- [[seguin-temporaries-const#^f3]] [zig/style] Make things const whenever possible. (B/fair, verified)
- [[superhtml-repo#^f20]] [zig/style] Logging: `std_options.logFn` filters scopes, in Debug by a hard-coded list, otherwise by a build option list of enabled scopes checked at comptime with `inline for`; 43 `std.log.scoped` uses in src/. `src/cli/logging.zig` (B/strong, verified)
- [[tigerbeetle-architecture#^f15]] [zig/style] Code review: one assigned reviewer, co-responsible for the PR landing; an extra reviewer when a change affects version `v` and `v+1` compatibility. `docs/internals/HACKING.md` (A/strong, verified)
- [[tigerbeetle-blog-asserting-implications#^f1]] [zig/safety] [zig/style] Implications are asserted often; `assert(!a or b)` is hard to read, `if (a) assert(b)` is clearer. (B/fair, verified)
- [[tigerbeetle-blog-index-count-offset-size#^f1]] [zig/style] `count` is a number of items, `index` points at one; the positive invariant is `index < count`. (A/fair, verified)
- [[tigerbeetle-blog-index-count-offset-size#^f2]] [zig/style] Bytes get their own words: `size = @sizeOf(T) * count`, `offset` is the byte counterpart of `index`. (A/fair, verified)
- [[tigerbeetle-blog-index-count-offset-size#^f3]] [zig/style] No `length`: it is ambiguous (bytes in Rust's `str::len`, code points in Python). (A/fair, verified)
- [[tigerbeetle-blog-index-count-offset-size#^f4]] [zig/style] With consistent suffixes, wrong combinations show in review even without understanding the code; `@divExact(node_offset, node_size)` gives `node_index`. (A/fair, verified)
- [[tigerbeetle-blog-index-count-offset-size#^f5]] [zig/style] Synergy with big-endian naming (`source`, `source_words`, `source_index`) and equal-length duals (`source`/`target`): parallel lines align. (A/fair, verified)
- [[tigerbeetle-blog-index-count-offset-size#^f6]] [zig/style] Newtype indexes (`enum(u32)`) help only without index arithmetic. (A/fair, verified)
- [[tigerbeetle-code#^f28]] [zig/style] Type-returning functions end in `Type` (`BoundedArrayType`, `QueueType`, `BitSetType`); the instance is named via `const X = @This()`. `stdx/bounded_array.zig` (A/strong, verified)
- [[tigerbeetle-code#^f43]] [zig/style] Measured: 84 of those 861 functions (10%) exceed 70 lines; longest `Replica.init` 382, `open_data_file` 322, `create_transfer` 277. The 70-line rule is ratcheted (only 71-72 flagged), not enforced on old code ([[tigerbeetle-tidy#^f6]]). (A/strong, unquoted)
- [[tigerbeetle-code#^f44]] [zig/style] Measured: `_max`/`_min` suffix names (449 distinct) far outnumber `max_`/`min_` prefixes (28), so units-last naming is followed. `usize` appears 1042 times against 1824 `u32` and 1722 `u64`, mostly for slice indexing: "avoid usize" is not absolute. 214 TODOs exist despite "zero techni... (A/strong, unquoted)
- [[tigerbeetle-code#^f45]] [zig/style] (inferred) Receiver naming is mixed: about 800 `self:` parameters and 1600 named receivers (`grid: *Grid`, `array: *BoundedArray`); Tiger Style is silent on it, tidy bans only `Self = @This()`. (A/strong, unquoted)
- [[tigerbeetle-how-we-use-zig#^f2]] [zig/style] No operator overloading, destructors or move semantics; `defer` is lexically scoped: all to make code review easier. @5:32 @7:02 (C/weak, verified)
- [[tigerbeetle-tidy#^f1]] [zig/test] [zig/style] Lint is an ordinary Zig `test "tidy"` that lists files with `git ls-files`, reads each once into a fixed 1 MiB buffer, and runs all checks streaming; it ran with `zig build test -- tidy`. `src/tidy.zig` (A/strong, verified)
- [[tigerbeetle-tidy#^f2]] [zig/style] Line length: over 100 codepoints is an error, except lines with a URL and multiline string literals whose value fits; a few named snapshot tables are exempt. `src/tidy.zig` (A/strong, verified)
- [[tigerbeetle-tidy#^f3]] [zig/style] [zig/errors] Ban list (string match): std types replaced by stdx (`std.BoundedArray`, `StaticBitSet`, `std.time.Instant`), `@memcpy(` for `stdx.copy_disjoint`, std int parsing and PRNG range functions; library and language footguns. `src/tidy.zig` (A/strong, verified)
- [[tigerbeetle-tidy#^f5]] [zig/style] Also banned: `debug.assert(` (import `assert` unqualified), `Self = @This()` (use the real type name), `!comptime`, `usingnamespace`; leftover `FIXME` and `dbg(` fail the check, FIXME being the agreed "fix before merge" marker. `src/tidy.zig` (A/strong, verified)
- [[tigerbeetle-tidy#^f6]] [zig/style] Function length is a ratchet, not a hard cap: only functions of 71 or 72 lines fail, so small functions can't creep past 70 while long old ones are left alone. `src/tidy.zig` (A/strong, verified)
- [[tigerbeetle-tidy#^f7]] [zig/style] Functions in CamelCase (they return a type) must end in `Type`. `src/tidy.zig` (A/strong, verified)
- [[tigerbeetle-tidy#^f8]] [zig/style] Dead code: a non-`pub` const or fn whose identifier appears once in its file is an error (lexer-based, may miss some, never wrong). `src/tidy.zig` (A/strong, verified)
- [[tigerbeetle-tidy#^f10]] [zig/style] Mixing bitwise and arithmetic operators without parentheses is an error (`1 + foo << 3`). `src/tidy.zig` (A/strong, verified)
- [[tigerbeetle-tidy#^f11]] [zig/style] [zig/memory] A `defer` must be followed by a blank line (or another defer/errdefer or a closing brace), enforcing Tiger Style's grouping of a resource with its cleanup. `src/tidy.zig` (A/strong, verified)
- [[tigerbeetle-tidy#^f13]] [zig/style] Policy: the last 32 commits are scanned for agent names (aider, claude, codex, copilot, cursor, gemini) in author and trailers; any hit fails with "does not allow LLM contributions". `src/tidy.zig` (A/strong, verified)
- [[tigerbeetle-tidy#^f15]] [zig/style] Checked, not there: no check for assertions per function, comment style, `usize`, units-last naming, recursion or allocation after init: those rules rest on review (allocation also on `StaticAllocator` at runtime). (A/strong, absence)
- [[tigerbeetle-tiger-style#^f2]] [zig/style] Zero technical debt policy: showstoppers found in design are fixed before shipping, because code is cheapest to change while hot. (A/strong, verified)
- [[tigerbeetle-tiger-style#^f5]] [zig/style] Explicitly sized integers everywhere, avoid `usize`. (A/strong, verified)
- [[tigerbeetle-tiger-style#^f14]] [zig/style] Smallest possible scope, fewest variables in scope. (A/strong, verified)
- [[tigerbeetle-tiger-style#^f15]] [zig/style] Hard limit of 70 lines per function, for a physical reason (fits on a screen). (A/strong, verified)
- [[tigerbeetle-tiger-style#^f16]] [zig/style] Splitting rules: inverse hourglass shape (few params, simple return, meaty body); keep control flow (`switch`/`if`) in the parent, push non-branchy work to helpers, keep leaf functions pure, parent owns state. Cites matklad's "push ifs up and fors down". (A/strong, verified)
- [[tigerbeetle-tiger-style#^f19]] [zig/style] Split compound conditions into nested if/else; every `if` should consider whether it needs an `else` to handle or assert the negative space. (A/strong, verified)
- [[tigerbeetle-tiger-style#^f20]] [zig/style] State invariants positively: `if (index < count)` rather than `if (index >= count)`. (A/strong, verified)
- [[tigerbeetle-tiger-style#^f22]] [zig/style] Always say why: rationale raises understanding, compliance, and gives criteria to judge the decision. (A/strong, verified)
- [[tigerbeetle-tiger-style#^f27]] [zig/style] Names: `snake_case` for functions, variables and files, also files holding a struct (departs from Zig's `CamelCase.zig`); no abbreviations; acronyms capitalised (`VSRState`); otherwise follow the Zig style guide. (A/strong, verified)
- [[tigerbeetle-tiger-style#^f28]] [zig/style] Units and qualifiers go last, most significant word first: `latency_ms_max` not `max_latency_ms`, so related names group and line up. (A/strong, verified)
- [[tigerbeetle-tiger-style#^f29]] [zig/style] [zig/memory] Allocator names carry meaning: `gpa` and `arena` tell the reader whether `deinit` must be called. (A/strong, verified)
- [[tigerbeetle-tiger-style#^f30]] [zig/style] Related names of equal length (`source`/`target`, not `src`/`dest`) so derived names line up. (A/strong, verified)
- [[tigerbeetle-tiger-style#^f31]] [zig/style] Helper and callback names carry the caller's name (`read_sector_callback`); callbacks go last in parameter lists. (A/strong, verified)
- [[tigerbeetle-tiger-style#^f32]] [zig/style] Order: important things first, `main` first; struct order is fields, then types, then methods. (A/strong, verified)
- [[tigerbeetle-tiger-style#^f33]] [zig/style] Don't overload a name with context-dependent meanings; prefer nouns because they work in prose and compose (`config.pipeline_max`). (A/strong, verified)
- [[tigerbeetle-tiger-style#^f35]] [zig/style] Commit messages matter more than PR descriptions, which `git blame` can't see. (A/strong, verified)
- [[tigerbeetle-tiger-style#^f36]] [zig/style] Comments are sentences (capital, full stop); explain why and, for tests, how (goal and method at the top). (A/strong, verified)
- [[tigerbeetle-tiger-style#^f37]] [zig/style] Don't duplicate variables or alias them, to keep state in sync. (A/strong, verified)
- [[tigerbeetle-tiger-style#^f40]] [zig/style] Check variables close to their use to avoid place-of-check to place-of-use gaps (POCPOU). (A/strong, verified)
- [[tigerbeetle-tiger-style#^f43]] [zig/memory] [zig/style] Blank lines group a resource allocation with its `defer`, so leaks are easier to spot. (A/strong, verified)
- [[tigerbeetle-tiger-style#^f44]] [zig/style] Treat index, count and size as distinct types with explicit conversions (+1, times unit); show division intent with `@divExact`, `@divFloor`, `div_ceil`. (A/strong, verified)
- [[tigerbeetle-tiger-style#^f45]] [zig/style] `zig fmt`, 4-space indent, hard 100-column limit (two copies side by side), trailing comma to let `zig fmt` wrap; braces on `if` unless one line. (A/strong, verified)
- [[tigerbeetle-tiger-style#^f48]] [zig/style] Docs style: every line of documentation is a line spent; hard wrap at 100; American English; Oxford comma. `docs/internals/docs.md` (A/strong, verified)
- [[tigerbeetle-tiger-style#^f49]] [zig/style] (inferred) Checked, not there: the guide never marks a rule as specific to databases or safety-critical code; static allocation, no recursion and `u32` are stated as absolutes. The domain framing is in ARCHITECTURE.md instead ([[tigerbeetle-architecture#^f2]]). (A/strong, absence)
- [[tigerbeetle-tiger-style-talk#^f1]] [zig/style] Most time goes to testing and production, so spend time on design; "go slow to go fast". @9:03 (A/fair, verified)
- [[tigerbeetle-tiger-style-talk#^f11]] [zig/style] Assertions document both positive and negative space for the next reader, beyond comments. @45:08 (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-011-release-notes#^f1]] [zig/style] Multi-object for loops iterate several equal-length sequences at once, so code no longer picks one to iterate and indexes the other. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-011-release-notes#^f3]] [zig/style] Casting builtins renamed @xToY to @yFromX so information flows right to left. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-011-release-notes#^f5]] [zig/style] With no result type, a cast needs `@as(T, ...)`, but annotations, field types, parameters and return types normally supply one. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-011-release-notes#^f9]] [zig/style] Bound functions removed: a method call is only the exact form `a.b(args)`. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-011-release-notes#^f22]] [zig/style] zig fmt canonicalises quoted identifiers and now formats .zon files. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-012-release-notes#^f1]] [zig/style] A local `var` never mutated is a compile error; use `const`. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-012-release-notes#^f2]] [zig/style] Result types now flow through `&`, so `&.{ ... }` and casts work behind address-of; result locations no longer pass through `@as` or `T{ ... }`. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-012-release-notes#^f3]] [zig/style] Destructuring of tuples, arrays and vectors: `const x, var y = ...`; slices must be sliced to a comptime length first. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-012-release-notes#^f10]] [zig/style] @fabs replaced by @abs, for ints and floats. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-012-release-notes#^f12]] [zig/style] Naming convention: enum fields are snake_case; std.builtin enums lowercased to match. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-012-release-notes#^f16]] [zig/style] Doc comments that only repeat field and type names were removed. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-014-release-notes#^f7]] [zig/style] Fields and declarations may not share names; standard naming (snake_case fields, TitleCase types) avoids the clash. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-014-release-notes#^f8]] [zig/style] @splat fills arrays too, e.g. `@splat(@splat(.black))` with decl literals. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-014-release-notes#^f11]] [zig/style] Packed structs compare with == and work in atomics without @bitCast. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-014-release-notes#^f29]] [zig/style] Incremental does not support usingnamespace; avoid it. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-015-release-notes#^f1]] [zig/style] usingnamespace removed: it hid where a declaration is defined, from people and tools (Autodoc); without it every identifier traces back trivially. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-015-release-notes#^f2]] [zig/style] It also encouraged poor namespacing: a separate file usually deserves its own namespace. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-015-release-notes#^f7]] [zig/style] Switch on non-exhaustive enums can mix named tags with `_`, and have both `else` (named) and `_` (unnamed). (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-016-release-notes#^f21]] [zig/style] Naming convention stated: one word per concept, names concatenated from concepts; mem "indexOf" becomes "find" (find, pos, last, linear, scalar); new cut functions. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-016-release-notes#^f29]] [zig/style] Small ints coerce to floats when exact; unary float builtins forward the result type; @floor/@ceil/@round/@trunc convert to ints, deprecating @intFromFloat. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-016-release-notes#^f33]] [zig/style] switch prongs accept decl literals and packed values; tag captures on all prongs; `=> comptime unreachable` for impossible errors. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-017-release-notes#^f17]] [zig/style] `[1]T{0} ** n` is replaced by `@splat(0)` with a typed result, also in `.init` declarations. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-017-release-notes#^f18]] [zig/style] @divCeil added, replacing `std.math.divCeil(a, b) catch unreachable`. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-017-release-notes#^f20]] [zig/style] `void{}` removed; write `{}`. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-017-release-notes#^f22]] [zig/style] i0 removed (use u0); internal and link_once linkage removed. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-017-release-notes#^f37]] [zig/style] `zig fmt --complexity` counts tokens and AST nodes, a better measure of an edit than line count. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-clap-repo#^f15]] [zig/style] Imports at the bottom of every file (`const std = @import("std");` last), like Bun. `clap.zig`, `build.zig`, `example/simple.zig` (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-compiler-dod#^f6]] [zig/style] Each Tag value's doc comment says which Data field is active and what it means, since the union is untagged. `std/zig/Ast.zig:3001` (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-compiler-dod#^f14]] [zig/style] Payload structs document their trailing variable data in a `Trailing:` doc comment listing what follows in extra. `std/zig/Zir.zig:2566` (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-compiler-dod#^f23]] [zig/style] Receivers are named after the type, short (`ip: *InternPool`, `tree: *const Ast`, `w: *Writer`), not `self`: 85 `(ip: *` vs 12 `(self: ` in InternPool.zig, 102 `(tree: ` vs 1 in Ast.zig (counted with grep). `src/InternPool.zig:6451` (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-compiler-dod#^f24]] [zig/style] File-as-struct files name themselves (`const Ast = @This();`, `const Writer = @This();`); 128 std files do this at top level vs 3 with `const Self = @This();` (counted with grep over lib/std). `std/zig/Ast.zig:11` (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-compiler-dod#^f26]] [zig/style] A file-level `//!` comment says what the structure is and how it relates to neighbours in the pipeline. `std/zig/Zir.zig:1` (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-devlog-2024#^f3]] [zig/style] Each index type carries a doc comment naming the array it indexes. (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-devlog-2024#^f6]] [zig/style] Multi-object `for` over parallel slices with pointer captures, `@min` and `@abs` replace an index loop with casts (stb_truetype port). (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-devlog-2026#^f15]] [zig/style] [zig/comptime] Types that double as namespaces are a common modern pattern; since the 0.16 type resolution redesign their fields are analysed only if the type is instantiated. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-a#^f6]] [zig/style] Only line comments exist, by design: every line tokenises on its own. Comments (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-a#^f7]] [zig/style] `///` documents whatever follows (declarations and fields); misplaced doc comments are compile errors, so docs can't silently detach. Doc Comments (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-a#^f8]] [zig/style] `//!` documents the file's (or namespace's) type and must come first; inside non-file namespaces it is currently ignored by doc generation, so put module docs at the top of the file. Top-Level Doc Comments (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-a#^f9]] [zig/style] The doc's example: `///` on the type, each field and each pub fn; a plain `//` trailing comment for an implementation note (why the field is signed). Doc Comments (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-a#^f11]] [zig/style] [toolchain] 0.17 calls struct, enum, union and opaque bodies namespaces (section "Namespace", "Namespace Level Variables"); test names in the examples still say "container", the older term. Namespace, Variables (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-a#^f12]] [zig/style] A namespace's braces are not a block: it holds declarations, not statements. Namespace (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-a#^f14]] [zig/style] Top-level declarations are order-independent (the example puts `print` before the `std` import it uses). Values (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-a#^f16]] [zig/style] Strings are byte slices by convention, UTF-8; literals are `*const [N:0]u8` and coerce to `[]const u8` and `[:0]const u8`. Slices, String Literals (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-a#^f17]] [zig/style] Multiline string literals (`\\` lines) take no escapes; good for embedded text and code. Multiline String Literals (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-a#^f18]] [zig/style] Prefer `const` over `var`: less work for readers and the compiler, more optimisation. Variables (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-a#^f23]] [zig/style] Destructuring splits tuples, arrays and vectors; the left side may mix new `const`/`var` declarations and existing lvalues, `_` discards; block scope only. Destructuring (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-a#^f25]] [zig/style] Destructuring makes swizzles readable. Destructuring Arrays (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-a#^f35]] [zig/style] No shadowing, anywhere: so an identifier means one thing throughout its scope; reusing a name in sibling scopes is fine. Variables, Shadowing (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-a#^f37]] [zig/style] A function-local `const S = struct { var x ... }` gives a static local (global lifetime, local name). Locally-Scoped Global Variables (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-a#^f45]] [zig/style] No operator overloading: an operator always means one of the built-in operations, so reading code needs no lookup. Operators (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-a#^f50]] [zig/style] Array literals: `[_]T{...}` infers length; with a typed declaration the anonymous `.{...}` literal initialises the result location. Arrays (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-a#^f52]] [zig/style] No literal syntax for NaN or infinity: use `std.math.inf` and `std.math.nan`. Float Literals (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-a#^f68]] [zig/style] Empty slice of a known type: `&.{}`, valid even for a mutable slice. Slices (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-a#^f91]] [zig/style] Enum literals (`.auto`) name a field when the type is known from context; the doc uses them in switches to avoid repeating the type. Enum Literals (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-a#^f99]] [zig/style] Blocks are expressions: a labelled block returns a value with `break :label value`, replacing a helper variable or function. Blocks (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-a#^f101]] [zig/style] switch: no fallthrough (combine cases with `,`), inclusive ranges `a...b`, arbitrary comptime-known case expressions, `else` required unless exhaustive. switch (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-a#^f110]] [zig/style] while has a continue expression `: (i += 1)` (run on `continue` too); while and for are expressions whose `else` runs when the loop ends without `break`. while (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-a#^f111]] [zig/style] `while (opt()) /x/` loops until null (the iterator idiom); `while (eu()) /x/ ... else /err/` until an error. while with Optionals, Error Unions (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-a#^f113]] [zig/style] for iterates slices, arrays and ranges; several sequences at once (lengths must match, checked); index via an extra `0..`; mutate via `&items` with `/*v/`; unbounded ranges are errors. for (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-a#^f114]] [zig/style] Labelled while and for loops let `break :outer` and `continue :outer` target an outer loop. Labeled while, Labeled for (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-a#^f115]] [zig/style] `if` is the ternary; it also unwraps optionals (`/v/`) and error unions (`/v/ ... else /err/`, the else capture is mandatory), `/*v/` for in-place edits; test null alone with `== null`. if (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-b#^f22]] [zig/style] Prefer `@as` (coercion) whenever it works. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-b#^f23]] [zig/style] Cast builtins take their destination from the result type; give one with a typed `const`, a field or `@as` where context has none. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-b#^f25]] [toolchain] [zig/style] 0.17: @enumFromInt is deprecated for @fromBackingInt (or @bitCast). (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-b#^f26]] [toolchain] [zig/style] 0.17: @intFromEnum is deprecated for @backingInt (or @bitCast); both new builtins also cover packed structs and packed unions. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-b#^f34]] [zig/style] Non-void results must be used or discarded explicitly with `_ =`. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-b#^f54]] [zig/comptime] [zig/style] `const Self = @This();` inside a returned struct; at file scope @This() is the file's struct. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-b#^f84]] [zig/style] [zig/api] A file with top-level fields is a type: TitleCase file name and `const Name = @This();` at the top. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-b#^f96]] [zig/style] The Style Guide is the official reference style, not compiler-enforced. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-b#^f97]] [zig/style] Keep Value, Data, Context, Manager, State, utils, misc and initials out of type names: they apply to every type and say nothing. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-b#^f98]] [zig/style] utils/misc modules are a categorisation failure: put the declarations at the root of the module that needs them. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-b#^f99]] [zig/style] Name against the fully qualified path: `json.JsonValue` should be `json.Value`; file names count as namespace segments. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-b#^f100]] [zig/style] No underscore prefixes: there are no private fields; name and document fields for their invariants, and note semver exceptions in doc comments. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-b#^f101]] [zig/style] Name collisions: longer names at outer scopes, shorter at inner; keyword clashes use `@"name"`; reflection should rely on types, not name conventions. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-b#^f102]] [zig/style] Whitespace: 4 spaces, braces on the same line, lists over 2 items one per line with a trailing comma, aim for 100 columns. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-b#^f103]] [zig/style] Names: namespaces (0-field structs) snake_case; types and type-returning functions TitleCase; other callables camelCase; everything else snake_case. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-b#^f104]] [zig/style] Acronyms are ordinary words, even two-letter ones: `XmlParser`, `readU32Be`. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-b#^f105]] [zig/style] Files with top-level fields TitleCase, others snake_case; directories snake_case; established names like ENOENT may stay. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-b#^f106]] [zig/style] Doc comments: drop what the name says; duplicating text across similar functions is fine (tools show it). (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-b#^f107]] [zig/style] [zig/safety] Doc vocabulary: "assume" for invariants whose violation is unchecked IB, "assert" for safety-checked IB. (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-langref-017-b#^f108]] [zig/style] Source is UTF-8, LF line endings including the last line, no BOM, no CR or other control characters; zig fmt can repair encodings. (A/strong, verified)

- [[zig-learn-overview#^f1]] [zig/style] No hidden control flow: control flow only through keywords and function calls, for readability. (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-learn-overview#^f19]] [zig/style] Top-level declarations are order independent and lazily analysed. (A/fair, verified)
- [[zig-learn-tools#^f1]] [zig/style] Recommends a language server (ZLS) over syntax-highlighting extensions. (A/weak, verified)

- [[zig-repo-docs#^f32]] [zig/style] Checked, not there: no CONTRIBUTING.md, style guide or lint config in the repo at 0.17.0; formatting is enforced by `zig fmt` via `test-fmt`. (A/strong, absence)
- [[zig-std-allocators#^f14]] [zig/style] Long-form design reasoning sits in `//` comments inside the function body (two reset strategies weighed), API contract in `///` above it. `std/heap/ArenaAllocator.zig:113` (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-std-containers#^f5]] [zig/style] Doc comment names the parameter `gpa` for the allocator a container keeps across calls (general purpose), first after `self`. `std/array_list.zig:1026` (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-std-containers#^f9]] [zig/style] Every mutating method documents pointer invalidation, complexity and asserts in fixed phrases ("Invalidates element pointers if", "This operation is O(N).", "Asserts that"). `std/array_list.zig:806` (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-std-containers#^f28]] [zig/style] Generic type functions use `const Self = @This();` inside the returned anonymous struct (array_list, hash maps, MultiArrayList all do). `std/multi_array_list.zig:176` (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-std-containers#^f29]] [zig/style] std.zig lists types (TitleCase) then namespaces (snake_case files), with deprecations as doc-commented aliases kept for a release. `std/std.zig:67` (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-std-json-fmt-args#^f12]] [zig/style] json/static.zig still uses `std.array_list.Managed`, deprecated elsewhere: the std does not migrate all its code at once. `std/json/static.zig:5` (A/fair, verified)

- [[zig-talk-build-system-intro#^f5]] [zig/style] [zig/build] Convention: sources under `src/`, a module's entry point named `root.zig`; consumers see only the root file, so re-export from it. @4:39 (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-talk-roadmap-2026#^f1]] [zig/perf] [zig/style] Labeled switch with `continue :label value` for state machines: a comptime-known continue is a structured goto; branching moves to each prong, which helps the branch predictor (tokenizer 13% faster). @22:54 (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-talk-roadmap-2026#^f2]] [zig/style] He finds labeled switch more readable than a while-switch loop: each continue says where control goes. @26:19 (A/strong, verified)
- [[zig-talk-roadmap-2026#^f3]] [zig/style] Zig had goto and removed it as redundant with labeled break and continue. @22:06 (A/strong, verified)
- [[ziggit-self-and-this#^f1]] [zig/style] Some regulars never write `self: Self`; they name the receiver after the type, as a Loris Cro post argued. (C/fair, verified)
- [[ziggit-self-and-this#^f2]] [zig/style] The rename argument for `Self` in file-structs is weak: editors rename easily, and `lexer: Lexer` reads clearer. (C/fair, verified)
- [[ziggit-self-and-this#^f3]] [zig/style] Middle ground: `self: Widget`, using `Self` only where required (anonymous generic structs). (C/fair, verified)
- [[ziggit-self-and-this#^f4]] [zig/style] Zig has no `self` keyword: the receiver is just the first parameter. (C/fair, verified)

- [[zls-repo#^f22]] [zig/style] The log handler formats into a fixed 4096-byte stack buffer with a `std.Io.Writer.fixed`, truncating with "..." rather than allocating. `src/main.zig` (A/fair, verified)
- [[zls-repo#^f25]] [zig/style] File-as-struct: TitleCase files (Server.zig, DocumentStore.zig, InternPool.zig) are structs with top-level fields and `const Server = @This();`; snake_case files (offsets.zig, ast.zig) are namespaces. Fields come first, then imports. `src/Server.zig`, `src/analyser/InternPool.zig` (A/fair, verified)
- [[zls-repo#^f26]] [zig/style] A root file re-exports modules and forces analysis of every decl in tests with `std.testing.refAllDecls(@This())` in a comptime block. `src/zls.zig` (A/fair, verified)
- [[zls-repo#^f39]] [zig/style] Checked, not there: no CONTRIBUTING or style document in the repo; style is `zig fmt` plus convention. (A/fair, absence)

### zig/test: tests, testing allocator, fuzzing, snapshot and simulation testing (243)

- [[ghostty-repo#^f69]] [zig/test] Fuzzing is AFL++ on exported C entry points, with a fixed-buffer bump allocator reset per input; the first input byte picks the SIMD or the scalar path. `test/fuzz-libghostty/src/fuzz_stream.zig:8-65` (A/strong, verified)
- [[ghostty-repo#^f70]] [zig/test] Checked, not there: no `std.testing.fuzz` use in src/ (grep at this commit); fuzzing lives outside the build's test step. (inferred) (A/strong, absence)
- [[ghostty-repo#^f71]] [zig/test] Regression tests explain the invariant and link the issue. `src/terminal/page.zig:2762-2766` (A/strong, verified)
- [[ghostty-repo#^f72]] [zig/test] Some things aren't automated: the input/IME stack has a manual test matrix in HACKING.md. `HACKING.md` (A/strong, verified)
- [[kristoff-snapshot-testing#^f1]] [zig/test] Snapshot testing: assert against known-good outputs and let the tests regenerate them. (B/strong, verified)
- [[kristoff-snapshot-testing#^f2]] [zig/test] For in-source snapshots, TigerBeetle's approach rewrites the Zig test source; for file outputs he uses build-system integration tests. (B/strong, verified)
- [[kristoff-snapshot-testing#^f4]] [zig/test] Snapshots are only as useful as their diffs are readable. (B/strong, verified)
- [[kristoff-talk-better-software#^f3]] [zig/test] Unit tests are code with momentum and couple to internals; asserts are one line and, with fuzzing, find more. @10:30 @11:20 (B/fair, verified)
- [[liptak-check-all-allocation-failures#^f1]] [zig/test] OOM paths are rarely hit organically, so `try` handling of them goes untested in normal runs. (A/strong, verified)
- [[liptak-check-all-allocation-failures#^f2]] [zig/test] Strategy: count allocations in one run, then rerun failing allocation 0, 1, ... N; needs a deterministic allocation count. (A/strong, verified)
- [[liptak-check-all-allocation-failures#^f3]] [zig/test] Wrap the test body in a function taking the allocator first and returning `!void`; pass other inputs as a tuple. (A/strong, verified)
- [[liptak-check-all-allocation-failures#^f4]] [zig/test] State shared between runs must be reset inside the test function. (A/strong, verified)
- [[liptak-check-all-allocation-failures#^f5]] [zig/test] With `std.testing.allocator` as backing it also catches double and invalid frees on the failure paths. (A/strong, verified)
- [[liptak-check-all-allocation-failures#^f7]] [zig/test] Code that recovers from OOM internally trips `NondeterministicMemoryUsage`/`SwallowedOutOfMemoryError`; treat it as a bug unless you know the recovery is intended. (A/strong, verified)
- [[liptak-check-all-allocation-failures#^f8]] [zig/test] Make it switchable with a build option, since it runs the body N+1 times. (A/strong, verified)
- [[liptak-check-all-allocation-failures#^f9]] [zig/test] Projects found real bugs with it (arocc; zss found a dangling pointer). (A/strong, verified)
- [[liptak-fuzz-allocators#^f2]] [zig/test] A wrapping allocator that fails on allocations larger than a multiple of the input size turned those into crashes the fuzzer reports. (B/strong, verified)
- [[liptak-fuzz-allocators#^f4]] [zig/test] Fuzz OOM paths by hashing the input into a reproducible FailingAllocator index. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-catch-flakes#^f1]] [zig/test] Keep running the full suite on main and keep a list of recent main failures: these are the flakes to eradicate. (B/fair, verified)
- [[matklad-catch-flakes#^f2]] [zig/test] If every main commit passed the tests, every main failure is a flake by definition. (B/fair, verified)
- [[matklad-diagnostics-factory#^f8]] [zig/errors] [zig/test] Polymorphism for tests: in a run errors go to stderr, in tests to an in-memory buffer (`if (errors.captured) /*captured/` else `std.debug.print`). (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-do-not-optimize-away#^f3]] [zig/perf] [zig/test] Scaffold: `parameter("element_count", 1_000_000)` reads an env var with a default and prints it; inputs can't be folded and reruns need no recompile. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-do-not-optimize-away#^f4]] [zig/perf] [zig/test] Output: accumulate `hash +%= insertion_point(elements, key)` and print it with the timing; a wrong optimisation shows as a changed hash. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-finding-bugs#^f1]] [zig/test] When a bug dodged the fuzzers, treat it as a fuzzer bug first: change the fuzzer to find it and related bugs, only then fix and add a unit test. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-finding-bugs#^f2]] [zig/test] Most important technique for algorithms: compare with an oracle (e.g. an O(N^2) version against the O(N log N) one). (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-finding-bugs#^f3]] [zig/test] Design reliable systems to have an oracle: TigerBeetle exposed internal timestamps for Jepsen and is co-designed with its simulator VOPR. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-finding-bugs#^f4]] [zig/test] Even a plain PRNG is brutally effective if used well. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-finding-bugs#^f5]] [zig/test] Generate small, tricky inputs, not huge ones; strings from a random subset of a fixed alphabet (sorted unique chars of the unit tests), random length. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-finding-bugs#^f6]] [zig/test] [zig/memory] Keep iterations fast by reusing memory across iterations, "static allocation in the small". (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-finding-bugs#^f7]] [zig/test] Swarm testing: make the distribution itself random (feature weights 0-100, some features off per run). (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-finding-bugs#^f8]] [zig/test] Recursive generators thread a size budget, split between children. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-finding-bugs#^f9]] [zig/test] Takeaway: co-designing the system and its test harness is leverage. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-frng-gist#^f1]] [zig/test] [zig/style] File-as-struct with one field `entropy: []const u8,`, `pub const Error = error{OutOfEntropy};`, `const FRNG = @This();`. (C/fair, verified)
- [[matklad-frng-gist#^f3]] [zig/test] `weighted(frng, weights: anytype)` returns `std.meta.FieldEnum(@TypeOf(weights))` via `inline for` over `std.meta.fieldNames`. (C/fair, verified)
- [[matklad-frng-gist#^f5]] [zig/test] (inferred) The search loop checks `if (size > driver.buffer.len) break;` but then runs `size_next`, which can exceed the buffer and trip the assert in `run_once`; check `size_next` if copying. (C/fair, verified)
- [[matklad-generate-all#^f1]] [zig/test] Uniform random inputs are ordinary (e.g. rarely equal elements); most bugs have small tricky examples, so check every input up to small n and m. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-generate-all#^f2]] [zig/test] A fuzzer's byte stream can serve as the random number source for a structured generator. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-generate-all#^f3]] [zig/test] Imperative `Gen` with `while !g.done()` enumerates any combinatorial structure without recursion; bounds inclusive so `gen(0)` doesn't panic. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-generate-all#^f4]] [zig/test] Examples come out ordered by complexity, so the first failure is the smallest counterexample. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-how-to-test#^f1]] [zig/test] Use a single `check` function that wraps the API under test, so API changes touch one place instead of every test. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-how-to-test#^f2]] [zig/test] Make adding a test trivial; if writing a test costs more than the fix, testing gets skipped. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-how-to-test#^f3]] [zig/test] Test features, not code; the neural network test: could the suite be reused if the software were an opaque model? (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-how-to-test#^f4]] [zig/test] Tests are slow because of IO, not code volume; keep as much as possible sans IO: caller does IO, callee computes. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-how-to-test#^f5]] [zig/test] Slow tests are skipped by default via an env var check, not conditional compilation; print each test's time. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-how-to-test#^f6]] [zig/test] Expect tests: gold output inline, updated in place by a special mode. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-how-to-test#^f7]] [zig/test] He prefers plain asserts, adding a crafted message on first real failure, over fluent assertion libraries. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-how-to-test#^f8]] [zig/test] Make hidden behaviour observable (cache hit logging, coverage marks) instead of testing internals. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-how-to-test#^f9]] [zig/test] Fire-and-forget background work is untestable; don't do it (structured concurrency). (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-how-to-test#^f10]] [zig/test] Write integrated tests per layer, so a change to a low layer doesn't rebuild the whole stack before its tests run. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-how-to-test#^f11]] [zig/test] Move automation into tests: formatting, history checks, docs, licences, linear-time checks via regression on input sizes. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-how-to-test#^f12]] [zig/test] Over-represented, not useful: fluent assertions, mocks, BDD. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-invariant#^f3]] [zig/test] He tests insertion_point exhaustively over small arrays (N=5, M=5) with exhaustigen. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-look-for-bugs#^f1]] [zig/test] Bugs can be found by just reading code; careful slow reading builds the mental model. (B/fair, verified)
- [[matklad-look-for-bugs#^f2]] [zig/test] Read along control flow from main ("goto definition"), and stare at the state: find all places key fields are created and modified. (B/fair, verified)
- [[matklad-look-for-bugs#^f6]] [zig/test] Review the whole subsystem, not just the diff; use git blame and `git log -S` for the historical why. (B/fair, verified)
- [[matklad-mechanical-habits#^f1]] [zig/test] A tidy file collects project lint checks as tests; its value is mere existence, adding a check is easier than building infrastructure. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-mechanical-habits#^f2]] [zig/test] TigerBeetle tidy checks: no large blobs in git history (actual git objects), line and function length, banned std APIs, no `// FIXME`, no dead code. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-mechanical-habits#^f3]] [zig/test] `// FIXME` used positively: marks code to change before merge, tidy blocks it. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-mechanical-habits#^f4]] [zig/test] Tidy checks find files with `git ls-files -z`. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-mechanical-habits#^f5]] [zig/test] Not rocket science rule: advance main only to a commit whose tests already passed, by a robot; forces CI to stay tidy. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-mechanical-habits#^f6]] [zig/perf] [zig/test] Micro benchmarks bitrot; make each one a test (`test "benchmark: binary search"`), chaining onto an existing entry point. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-mechanical-habits#^f7]] [zig/perf] [zig/test] Two modes by CLI shape: full suite runs benchmarks small and silent; `zig build test -- "benchmark: search"` runs big and prints. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-random-numbers-included#^f1]] [zig/api] [zig/test] Random number APIs work best with closed intervals, unlike most APIs which prefer half-open. (B/fair, verified)
- [[matklad-snapshot-testing#^f1]] [zig/test] His order: property/fuzz/randomised test first; if you can't fuzz, a snapshot test. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-snapshot-testing#^f2]] [zig/test] Snapshot test: render the outcome as text, compare with an inline string literal by diff, and a tool updates the literal automatically. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-snapshot-testing#^f3]] [zig/test] API: `try snap(@src(), \\expected).diff_fmt("2 + 2 = {}", .{result});`, `.update()` or `Snap.update_all = true` rewrites the gold value. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-snapshot-testing#^f4]] [zig/test] Main benefit: software is easy to change; on new requirements only the stringification changes and tests re-run once in update mode. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-snapshot-testing#^f5]] [zig/test] Even for stable code: asserts stop at the first failure; snapshots show all failures inline next to the code, a repeatable REPL. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-snapshot-testing#^f6]] [zig/test] `std.testing.expectEqualSlices` prints a useful diff, better than `std.debug.print`. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-snapshot-testing#^f7]] [zig/test] Keep a few example tests as smoke checks even with fuzzing: a fuzz test may miss reversed order, `0 1 2 3 4 -> 119` would not. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-snapshot-testing#^f8]] [zig/test] For small N, an exhaustive test over all inputs with a generator (`exhaustigen.zig`, `while (!g.done())`, `g.index(...)`) instead of random. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-snapshot-testing#^f9]] [zig/test] [zig/safety] Tests use `assert` heavily, and assert container counts before and after loops. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-test-minimization#^f1]] [zig/test] Core abstraction: a Finite RNG, a PRNG whose bytes are pre-generated and can run out (`error{OutOfEntropy}`). (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-test-minimization#^f7]] [zig/test] Code the system to crash on anything unexpected and feed random inputs; then coverage of inputs gives confidence. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-test-minimization#^f8]] [zig/test] [zig/comptime] `weighted(weights: anytype)` returns `std.meta.FieldEnum(@TypeOf(weights))`, picking an action proportional to weight; used as `switch (try world.frng.weighted(.{ .request = 10, .message = 20, .crash = 1 }))`. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-test-minimization#^f10]] [zig/test] Swarm testing: generate the action weights themselves at random at test start (`swarm_weights`). (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-test-minimization#^f11]] [zig/test] Running out of entropy counts as a pass; decisions are made as you go from the current World state, not a pre-generated action list. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-test-minimization#^f13]] [zig/test] Entropy size measures test complexity, so binary search the minimal failing size; a fresh shorter random slice works better than mutating the failing one. (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-test-minimization#^f14]] [zig/test] [zig/safety] Assertion failures crash and Zig has no unwinding, so the searcher runs each test as a child process with entropy on stdin; a run is reproduced from (size, seed). (B/strong, verified)
- [[matklad-zig-and-rust#^f6]] [zig/test] All IO including time is externalised, so testing tries permutations of environment effects: deterministic randomised simulation. (B/strong, verified)
- [[mitchellh-building-large-technical-projects#^f1]] [zig/test] Unit tests are how backend work shows results before any UI exists. (C/fair, verified)

- [[mitchellh-ghostty-and-useful-zig-patterns#^f14]] [zig/test] Fuzzing the VT parser was planned after early fuzzing found bugs; it now exists as AFL++ harnesses ([[ghostty-repo#^f69]]). (B/fair, verified)

- [[mitchellh-ghostty-memory-leak-fix#^f11]] [zig/test] None caught it because tests didn't reproduce the conditions; the fix adds a test that does. (B/strong, verified)
